LEGAL SECRETARIES, INCORPORATED

From 

Constitution of the

LEGAL SECRETARIES, INCORPORATED CODE OF ETHICS Article XX Section 1. The First duty of a legal secretary is loyalty to her employer.

Section 2. It shall be the duty of each and every member of any Legal Secretaries Association within the State of California at all times to maintain a standard of courtesy and helpfulness in all dealings between law offices, employees thereof, clients, courts, and all offices with which she may have contact. 

Section 3. It shall be unethical for any legal secretary to violate any statute that may now be in effect or hereafter be enacted governing privileged communications.

Section 4. It shall be unethical for any secretary or employee of any law office within the State of California to divulge the contents of any written documents in the possession of his or her employer without first having obtained the consent of said employer, or to discuss, maliciously or otherwise, with any person, matters of a confidential nature, knowledge concerning which may come to her by virtue of her position in such law office.

Section 5. It shall be the duty of every legal secretary to maintain harmonious cooperation with her fellow associates. vi

PREFACE 

Purpose. The second requisite of every legal secretary is efficiency, the first, according to the Code of Ethics of the California Federation of Legal Secretaries being "loyalty to her employer." The value and importance of an efficient legal secretary to her employer cannot be overemphasized. Much of her work, like that of her employer, is done under the inexorable pressure of time. All of her work must be accurate. From the crucible of experience, legal and secretarial, has evolved conviction that her efficiency, both as to time and accuracy, would be materially augmented if there were always at her hand one book to which she could immediately turn for guidance in the maze of legal secretarial procedure, not as a lawyer seeking the law of the case, but solely as the ministerial executive of the lawyer, carrying out the mechanics of his directions. 

Such a book should abrogate much of the tedium, delay and uncertainty of searching through files of past cases or other more or less inconvenient sources for a similar form or pertinent incident or chronology of procedure, and tend to minimize the expenditure of valuable time by her employer in personally detailing such steps in the routine she must perform. Hence, the primary purpose of this book is to offer suggestions which, when intelligently used under the supervision and direction of an attorney at law, we hope will contribute to that efficiency.

To the ends above stated no effort has been spared to achieve legal accuracy of text. The identities of our professional collaborators and editor would command respect for any book in the preparation of which they participated. But their purpose in aiding, like ours in invii

augurating this work, and which purpose they request that we emphasize in presenting such book, has been to produce a guide for the secretary to use under the authority of her employer, not an authority for her employer to cite. 

Legal secretaries, who are not members of the State 

Bar, may not practice law; we thoroughly disapprove of any such legal secretary doing any act which would encroach upon the "practice of law." Directions contained herein to do those things which would involve the practice of law are suggestive to the secretary, not to do them herself, but rather to make proper entries on her employer's calendar, or otherwise to assist him, to do them himself. 

Contents. The material in this handbook was compiled by associations of the Federation acting under a committee in collaboration with prominent California legal authorities. Our purpose, as outlined above, of course, is more lofty than our accomplishment. The book is presented, not as a perfect work, nor as a complete one; in attempting to write a small book on such a large subject many details must necessarily be omitted and variance in interpretation by different learned minds and inexactness of the law render perfection an impossibility. We humbly offer it, "as is," with the hope that it will save time and mistakes and thereby tend toward the achievement of our declared goal.

History. It was during 1931 that Zillah Stanton, a member of the first Legal Secretaries Association which had just been organized in Long Beach, conceived and developed the idea of a secretary's outline of legal procedure in loose-leaf form. These sheets were called "Aids to 

Legal Secretaries." viii

Later, the San Diego Association, with the assistance of Louise Cord, first Federation president, enlarged upon the idea and produced a similar loose-leaf booklet bearing the same title, and the Los Angeles Association also commenced the preparation of material. 

During her administration, Ruth M. Bauchet, second 

Federation president, Zillah Stanton, and the San Diego

Association turned over to Inez Ingram, the chairman of the Legal Procedure Committee, all available material, with the hope that it could be combined and enlarged into a book of substantial value to legal secretaries throughout the state. Members of that committee were given specific assignments of subjects in which they were deemed particularly well qualified and as to which they had access to outstanding authorities, which assignments they completed with the aid of other members of their associations, members of the bench and bar, and court attaches. This material was then reorganized, revised and supplemented by Miss Ingram, acting for the Secretaries, and by Judge B. 

Rey Schauer, acting as editor for the profession, with the generous cooperation of other prominent legal authorities. 

A contest was held to title the book. From the 52 titles submitted, the Executive Board of the Federation selected "Legal Secretary's Handbook (California)" offered by Leta Lyle Niccum, who later became the third 

Federation president.

Acknowledgments. In addition to the Federation presidents, committee members and collaborators heretofore mentioned, all of whom have contributed so materially at some stage to the concept, structure or contents of this book, we are appreciative of the fact that it could not have ix

reached fruition without the generous and patient cooperation of other members of the Associations and of those members of the Bar who are employers of the legal secretaries who have assisted, prominent among whom are Ralph J. Brown, of Los Angeles, whose secretary, Leta Lyle 

Niccum, in addition to her multifarious duties as president of the Federation of Legal Secretaries has also rendered unstinting assistance to the committee; Hardy & Horwin, present employers, and Paul E. Palmer, former employer, of Inez Ingram.

HOW TO USE HANDBOOK 

The procedure, forms and suggestions should be used only with the permission and under the direction and supervision of an attorney at law.

Procedure varies in the several counties and courts, so before using that suggested here, ascertain the local court rules and check with the clerk's office or elsewhere, as may be expedient, to determine that this procedure conforms to that required by the particular court and county. 

When it does, indicate that fact in the margin. When it does not, note the difference. Thus, the Handbook will be of more value to your office and its accuracy in those respects assured.

Brevity and simplicity are appreciated by all, especially by judges and clerks who deal with many pleadings every day. Only simple basic forms have been suggested. These we have endeavored to make concise, in keeping with the sentiment of the following recent statement of Charles A. 

Beardsley, President of the American Bar Association: "Most lawyer-drawn documents would be much improved if the draftsman would omit 75% of the words." †Used throughout book as a reminder that a duplicate, as well as original, must be prepared for filing, in addition to other necessary copies, in courts where branch sessions are held in places other than county seats.

I 

(CCP 33-301) The courts are established or authorized by the California 

Constitution, Article VI, Section 1. Certain courts are courts of record, all other courts are known as "inferior courts."

COURTS OF RECORD 1. The Court of Impeachment (CCP 36-39) 

The Senate may sit as the Court of Impeachment 

2. Supreme Court (see APPEALS, p 160)

The Supreme Court has jurisdiction in the following instances: (a) Issuance of Writs

Application may be made directly to the Supreme Court for the issuance of certain writs, such as 

Writ of Mandamus Supersedeas 

Habeas Corpus Prohibition Certiorari (b) Appellate Jurisdiction 

In Equity

In all cases except those arising in 

Municipal and Justices' Courts 

In Law

In cases involving the legality of a tax or fine and those involving title or possession of real estate, except those within jurisdictional limits of Municipal Court, in which cases appeals are taken to 

Superior Court

In Probate

All judgments or orders that are appealable

In Criminal Cases 

On questions of law alone where judgment of death has been rendered

All Matters which are ordered transferred from the District Courts of Appeal 

3. District Courts of Appeal 

(a) Writs 

Same as Supreme Court 

(b) Appellate Jurisdiction (see APPEALS, p 160) 

In Equity 

None, except cases transferred from the Supreme Court 

In Law (1) Cases in which the Superior

Court has original jurisdiction, except where appellate jurisdiction is in Supreme Court (2) Forcible entry and unlawful entry or detainer, except those arising in Municipal or inferior courts (3) Proceedings in insolvency (4) Nuisances, writs, and eminent domain (5) Special proceedings as may be provided by law (6) Cases transferred from Supreme Court

In Criminal Cases 

4. Superior Courts

On questions of law only, except where judgment of death is rendered (a) Original Jurisdiction Writs 

Same as Supreme Court 

In Equity

All cases except where given to the 

Municipal and inferior courts 

In Law (1) All cases except where given to Municipal and inferior courts (2) Special cases and proceedings not otherwise provided for; naturalization, etc.

In Criminal Cases 

All felonies; misdemeanors not otherwise provided for (b) Appellate Jurisdiction (see APPEALS, pp 

172, 181)

In cases arising in the Municipal and inferior courts 

5. Municipal Courts (CCP 85-98)

There are four in California established in accorddance with CCP 85: 

Long Beach 

Los Angeles 

San Diego 

San Francisco

(a) Original Jurisdiction in Civil Actions 

(CCP 89) 

Monetary limit is $2,000.00. If action is is based on obligation for more than $2,000.00 the excess may be remitted by allegation in complaint thereby establishing jurisdiction in the Municipal Court. (CCP 396) 

Note: Attorney's fees claimed are to be included in determining jurisdiction, Taylor v. Datig, 123 C A 782 (1) In all cases at law where the demand, exclusive of interest, or value of the property is $2,000.00 or less (2) In actions for dissolution of partnership where the total assets of the partnership do not exceed $2,000.00 (3) In actions of interpleader, where the amount of money or value of the property involved does not exceed $2,000.00 (4) To cancel, rescind or revise contracts in certain cases (CCP 89-c) (5) In all proceedings in forcible entry or forcible or unlawful detainer, where the rental is $200.00 or less per month and the whole amount of damages is $2,000.00 or less

(6) In all actions to enforce and foreclose liens on personal property where the amount of such liens is $2,000.00 or less (7) In all actions to enforce and foreclose liens of mechanics, materialmen, etc., where the amount of such liens is $2,000.00 or less (with certain exceptions; CCP 89-f) (8) To issue temporary restraining orders or preliminary injunctions, and appoint receivers in certain cases (CCP 89-g) (9) To charge interest of debtor partner with judgment under CC 2422 (10) To determine third party claims under CCP 689 (jurisdiction in this capacity apparently is not limited to $2000.00) (11) All cases in equity where pleaded as defensive matter in any case properly pending (CCP 89(3)) 

(b) Original Jurisdiction in Criminal Actions 

(1) Municipal court has exclusive jurisdiction in all misdemeanor cases which which are committed within the city, and concurrent jurisdiction on all misdemean-

or cases which are committed within the the county, except where the Juvenile Court or an inferior court has exclu sive jurisdiction (Penal Code 

1462) (2) Municipal Court may accept pleas of guilty in felony cases not punishable with death and certify the defendant to the Superior Court for sentence (Penal Code 

859-a) 

Appeals from the Municipal Court are taken to the Appellate Department of the Superior Court (see 

APPEALS, p 172)

INFERIOR COURTS 

Inferior courts are not courts of record.

Classification of Justices' Courts as A or B is dependent upon population of township being 30,000 or more; such fact may be determined by proceeding in mandamus or as shown by last census, whichever may be later. (CCP 81) 

1. Class A Justices' Courts (CCP 81) 

These courts are established in cities, towns and judicial townships which have a population of 

30,000 or more. 

(a) Original Jurisdiction in Civil Actions 

(CCP 112)

(1) Where demand, exclusive of interest, or value of property is $1,000.00 or less. (Except cases involving title or possession of real estate or legality of tax, fine, etc.) (2) Forcible entry, forcible or unlawful detainer where the rental value is $100.00 per month or less and the whole amount of damages claimed is $1000.00 or less (3) To enforce or foreclose liens on personal property where the amount is $1000.00 or less (4) Charge interest of debtor partner with judgment under CC 2422 and appoint receiver (5) Determine third party claim of title to personal property (CCP 689) (6) Appoint receiver to care for perishable property attached and to do other acts mentioned in CCP 547-a; also to appoint receivers in cases mentioned in CCP 564(4) (7) In cases in equity where pleaded as defensive matter in any case properly pending 

(b) Original Jurisdiction in Criminal Actions 

Misdemeanors exclusive of jurisdiction of any Municipal Court established in the County, (except cases within the jurisdiction of the Juvenile Court) (Penal Code 1425(1))

Appeals from Class A Justices' Courts are taken to the Superior Court (CCP 973, 974, 982, Penal Code 1466-1469) (see APPEALS, pp 181-2) 

2. Class B Justices' Courts (CCP 81) 

These courts are established in cities, towns and judicial townships which have a population of less than 30,000. 

(a) Original Jurisdiction in Civil Actions 

(CCP 112) (1) Cases at law where the demand, exclusive of interest, or value of property is $300.00 or less, (except cases involving title or possession of real estate, or legality of tax, fine, etc.) (2) Forcible entry, forcible or unlawful detainer where the rental value is $75.00 a month or less and the whole amount of damages claimed is $300.00 or less (3) To enforce or foreclose liens on personal property where the amount is $300.00 or less (4) To charge interest of debtor partner with judgment under CC 2422 (5) To determine third party claim under CCP 689 (6) To appoint receiver to care for perishable property attached and to do other acts mentioned in CCP 547a

(b) Original Jurisdiction in Criminal Actions 

(1) Misdemeanors punishable by fine not exceeding $1000.00 or imprisonment not exceeding six months or both (Penal Code 1425 (2)) Appeals may be taken to Superior Court (see pp 181-2) 

3. Police Courts (CCP 121-122(e), see title, Police 

Courts General Laws)

These courts are established in incorporated cities and towns where there is no Municipal 

Court and have (1) Jurisdiction in civil actions over violation of ordinances (2) Criminal jurisdiction substantially same as Class B Justices' Courts

Appeals may be taken to Superior Court (see pp 181-2) 

4. Small Claims Court (CCP 117) 

Parties must appear in propria persona. Actions must not be brought in name of assignee. 

All justices of the peace and, in jurisdictions where there is no other court inferior to the Superior Court, Municipal court judges exercise the functions of the small claims court and while so sitting are referred to as the Small Claims Court.

The jurisdiction extends to claims for money only not exceeding $50.00 and includes actions in tort as well as in contract. (Appeals from Small Claims Courts, allowed only to defendants, are taken to the Superior 

Court; CCP 117j)

PROCEDURE Affidavit Answer 

Serve- Hearing- (takes place of complaint in other courts) drawn by party, clerk, deputy or justice. (CCP 117-a, 117-c). No attachment (CCP 117-ha), and no assigned claims allowed (CCP 117-f) 

Fee $1.00 (CCP 117-p) (not necessary, although answer and counter claim may be served and filed CCP 117-h) Defendant with copy of affidavit and order to appear (CCP 117-c) 

Evidence presented personally 

Attorneys not allowed, except as witnesses (CCP 117-g) Judgment Abstract of judgment (CCP 117-m) Execution

Defendant only can appeal (CCP 117-j) 

Taken to Superior Court (CCP 117-j) (There trial is handled as if case never tried before (CCP 117-j and 976) unless taken on questions of law alone) (CCP 975) 

Attorneys allowed to appear on appeal and $15 will be charged against defendant if judgment goes for plaintiff (CCP 117-j) To cover amount of judgment, costs and attorneys' fees must be filed; may be undertaking with sureties or cash deposit (CCP 117-1, 117-11) 

Amount of judgment (CCP 117-1) 

Fee $8.00 (CCP 117-j, Pol Code 4300-e)

II 

Explanation (CCP 117, 307-1062a)

A civil action is an ordinary proceeding in a court of justice by which one party prosecutes another for the declaration, enforcement or protection of a right, or the redress or prevention of a wrong. 

Civil actions are commenced in the following courts: 

1. Superior Court 

2. Municipal Court (includes Small Claims) 3. Justices' Courts, Class A and Class B (includes 

Small Claims Court) 

4. U. S. District Courts (see U. S. DISTRICT 

COURTS, p 365)

TIME OF COMMENCING ORDINARY CIVIL ACTIONS STATUTES OF LIMITATIONS 

(CCP 312-363, etc.)

Civil actions must be commenced within prescribed periods of time after the cause of action accrues, otherwise, in the absence of waiver or estoppel, they may be barred by a proper pleading of the applicable statute of limitations. The Statute of Limitations and precise subdivision thereof MUST BE PLEADED as a defense (for correct phraseology, see forms of demurrer, p 51, Answer, p 57).

No acknowledgment or promise is sufficient evidence of a new or continuing contract, by which to take the case out of the operation of this statute, unless the same is contained in some writing, signed by the party to be charged. (CCP 360)

The Statute of Limitations does not run while defendant is out of the state. (CCP 351; other exceptions 

CCP 12a, 328, 352, 354)

The respective periods for the principal types of actions are: 

10 years (CCP 337.5) 

An action upon any bonds or coupons issued by the State of California 

6 years (CCP 336a) 

Action on corporation bonds, coupons, or mortgage or trust deed securing them; not applicable to bonds of public corporations or district 

5 years (CCP 336) (a) Upon a judgment (CCP 336) (but execution on it is not necessarily barred CCP 685; pp 

125, 151, 152) (b) Adverse possession, recovery or possession of real property (CCP 318) (c) For mesne profits of real property (CCP 336) 

4 years (CCP 337 and 343) (a) Written contract 

On a demand note, 4 years from the date of execution, not from date of demand. Demand unnecessary (1928), 205 Cal 284) (b) Book account v. Hueter (Bass v. 

4 years from date of last entry, debit or credit (c) Account stated 

4 years from date of last entry, debit or credit

(d) Mutual, open and current book account (e) Annulment of marriage (Statute begins to run at different times, CC 83) (f) All actions not otherwise provided for in sections preceding CCP 343 

3 years (CCP 338) (a) Relief on ground of fraud or mistake 3 years from discovery of fraud (if discovery made and action commenced more than three years after perpetration, the pleading must specify facts and circumstances showing excuse for failure to make earlier discovery. Consolidated L. & P. Co. v. Scarborough, 216 Cal 698) (b) Trespass upon real property (c) Injury to real property (d) Taking, detaining, injuring or for specific recovery of personal property (e) Liability created by statute, except penalty or forfeiture 

2 years (CCP 339) (a) Oral or implied contract (b) On certificate, abstract or guarantee of title, or policy of title insurance-cause of action, however, not deemed to have accrued until discovery of loss (c) Against sheriff, coroner, or constable for an act or omission to perform official duty 

(d) For certain Workmen's Compensation benefits as provided in Labor Code 5405b (e) (See DIVORCE-Grounds, p 185)

1 year (CCP 340) (a) Personal injury or death (b) Malpractice (c) Libel (d) Slander (e) Assault (f) Battery (g) False imprisonment (h) Seduction of person below age of legal consent (i) Upon a statute for a penalty or forfeiture (j) Against bank for payment of forged or raised check or forged endorsement (k) Forcible entry or detainer (CCP 1172) (1) To set aside conveyance of community property (CC 172a, 164) 

(m) Death benefit under Workmen's Compensation 

Act. Labor Code 5406 (1 year from date of death and not more than 2 years from date of injury except as therein provided) 

6 months (CCP 341) (a) Against tax collector for seizure of property (b) Claims against county, after rejection by supervisors (CCP 342) (c) Right to proceed under Workmen's Compensation for medical, disability, or other benefits (Labor Code 5405) (d) Contest after probate of will (Pr C 380) (e) To recover stock sold for delinquent assessment (CCP 341, CC 339)

(f) To set aside act by trustees of corporation dissolved by operation of law 3 months (a) Confirm, modify, correct or vacate award of arbitrators (CCP 1287, 1290) (b) Claim due, and rejected by executor or administrator (Pr C 714) (c) Action for deficiency judgment after sale under deed of trust or mortgage (CCP 337(1)) 

90 days

Recovery or conversion of personal property left in hotel, furnished apartment, etc. (CCP 341a) 2 months After due, on claim rejected by executor or administrator before due date (Pr C 714) 

30 days (CCP 349)

Action to contest local improvement assessment levied under local improvement act of 1901 

No Limitation 

(a) Recovery of money or other property deposited with any bank, unless in liquidation (CCP 348) (b) Annulment of marriage for unsound mind or bigamy (CC 83) (No limitation in years but, if brought by a party thereto, must be brought within lifetime of parties) (c) Public nuisance obstructing a public right (CC 3490) The Statute of Limitations does not run while defendant is out of the state. (CCP 351;-other exceptions 

CCP 12a, 328, 352, 354)

Plaintiff

PARTIES 

(CCP 367-390) The party complaining is known as the plaintiff. (CCP 308)

If plaintiff is one of the following or in a representative capacity, complaint must so allege (see 

ALLEGATIONS, p 29): 

Executor, administrator, or trustee (CCP 369)

Guardian or guardian ad litem for a minor, insane or incompetent person (CCP 372) 

Defendant (CCP 308)

The party against whom suit is brought is known as the defendant.

Cross Complainant 

A defendant who files a cross complaint asking affirmative relief in connection with plaintiff's action, otherwise than by counterclaim. 

Cross Defendant

A plaintiff, or outside party, named as cross defendant in the cross complaint. 

Intervener (CCP 387)

A third person who is permitted by court to become a party to an action or proceeding between other persons by filing complaint in intervention (a) May join plaintiff in claiming what is sought by complaint (b) May unite with defendant in resisting claims of plaintiff (c) May demand anything adversely to both plaintiff and defendant

Appellant

Party who appeals action to another court. Respondent Party adverse to appellant on appeal.

COURT FOR TRIAL (Jurisdiction) 

Jurisdiction is right and power of court to proceed with respect to (a) subject matter and (b) to persons concerned. (See specific court, Jurisdiction, and p 11 Commencement of Civil Actions)

PLACE OF TRIAL (Venue) 

(CCP 392-400) Venue has to do not with the power of the court in the abstract, but with its right to proceed in a given case by reason of the residence of parties, the place of performance of the contract or the place of happening of the incident. Venue refers to the proper place for the trial of an action. 

1. The following actions must be commenced in the county where the subject matter (or part thereof) is located: (CCP 392) (a) To quiet title (b) Recovery of real property, or estate or interest therein (actions in ejectment and unlawful detainer) (c) Foreclosure on real property (d) Determination of any rights in real property (such as to establish an easement) (e) Injury to real property (f) Partition of real property 

2. Except as otherwise especially provided, the proper

county for the commencement and prosecution of civil actions is the county in which defendant resides 

3. If defendant's residence is unknown, action may be tried in any county 4. Where there are two or more defendants in different counties, the action may be brought in the county of residence of any defendant 5. In an action for injury to person or to personal property or for death from wrongful act the action may be brought either where the defendant resides or where the injury occurred 

6. Where brought on a contract the action may be brought in county where defendant resides or where obligation is to be performed or in which the contract in fact was entered into 

7. Where the defendant is domestic corporation the action may be brought in any one of 5 places: (Cal 

Constitution, Art XII, Section 16) 

(a) Where contract is made (b) To be performed (c) Where obligation arises (d) Where breach occurs (e) Where principal place of business is located 

8. Where defendant is a foreign corporation it would seem possible to sue it in any county of the state. 

Long v. General Pet. Co., 11 C A (2d) 708

Change of Venue (CCP 396, 397) 

1. Except for matters within jurisdiction of Justices'

Courts of Class B, as provided in CCP 396a, a motion for change of venue on ground of wrong court must be made at the time the answer or demurrer is filed and must be supported by an affidavit of merits, and notice of motion, or the objection is waived. (CCP 396b)

2. Costs in transferring an action: (CCP 399) (a) If brought in wrong court, plaintiff must pay transfer costs (b) If made on grounds set out in CCP 397, subdivisions 2, 3 and 4, the party requesting change must pay transfer costs at the time the notice of motion is filed Procedure Prepare (a) Notice of motion to change venue (b) Affidavit (c) Affidavits of merits †1 original of each to file with proof of service

There are specific allegations required in certain instances, such as: (a) Residence-complaints in certain types of action MUST show that action is brought in proper county (Plaintiff) (Defendant) was at all times herein mentioned, and now is, a resident of California. County, (b) Fictitious Names and Capacities-In many instances it is quite important to include fictitious names in a complaint so that all possible defendants are included in suit. 

When true names are ascertained an amendment must be made. (See AMENDED 

PLEADINGS, p 67) (CCP 474)

When the following paragraph is used it is not necessary to set out any fictitious. capacities as "Doe One Company, a corporation, etc."

The true names or capacities, whether individual, corporate, associate or otherwise, of defendants, Doe One, Doe Two, Doe Three are unknown to plaintiff, who therefore sues said defendants by such fictitious names, and will ask leave to amend this complaint to show their true names and capacities when same have been ascertained. (c) Licensed Status-where work is done which can lawfully be done only if the person doing the same is at the time possessed of a license, a complaint to recover for such services must aver possession of the license at the time involved. Failure to comply with this bars recovery. Applies to actions for attorney's fees, physician's fees, contractor's claims, etc. (Example-see Contractor's License Law, section 12, 

Deering's General Laws, 1937, page 864; 

Alvarado v. Davis, 115 CA 782)

At all times mentioned herein plaintiff possessed a valid and subsisting license. issued by the as (appropriate agency). 

(d) Action on Contract-Complaint must allege whether contract is written or oral (CCP 430(10))

The contract on which this cause of action is based is (in writing) (oral).

SUMMONS 

(CCP 406-416) A summons gives actual or constructive notice to the defendant of an action against him. The original is issued by the clerk of the court upon filing a complaint (or within a year thereafter) and copy of summons is attached to copy of complaint for service upon the defendant.

Contents 

The summons is usually a printed form furnished by the court. 

1. Heading 

The heading is the same as the complaint, however, all the names of defendants need not appear in the title, just use name of first defendant and designate others "et al." (Although it is not necessary to have all the names in heading, some consider it advisable and good practice to include names of all defendants in heading) 

2. Body (a) Name all defendants (b) A direction that defendant appear and answer complaint within time specified (see

APPEARANCE, p 46) 

(c) A notice that unless defendant so appears and answers plaintiff will take judgment for any money or damages demanded in complaint if arising upon contract, or will apply to the court for any other relief demanded in the complaint 3. Signature 

The summons is dated and signed by clerk issuing it and the seal of the court is affixed. 

Note: A form of affidavit of service is usually on back of the printed summons. 

Kinds of Summonses 

Ordinary Summons

This is used in most cases. It requires defendant to appear within 10 days if served in county, or 30 days if served elsewhere.

Alias Summons 

If original summons is returned to clerk without being served on any or all of defendants, or if lost, the clerk may issue alias summons upon request made by affidavit. If summons has been lost, affidavit to that effect must be filed. The alias summons is in the same form as original summons except the word "alias" is inserted before the word "summons" wherever it appears. Any number of alias summonses may be issued (within 3 years after commencement of action) and they are numbered consecutively after the first one, such as "second alias summons" etc. 

Each is dated as of date issued, not as of date of original summons. (CCP 408, 581a)

Other Summonses 

In certain actions a slightly different form of summons is used. (1) Partition Suits Summons must contain a description of the property, etc. (CCP 756) 

(2) Forcible Entry and Detainer Suits 

Summons requires defendant to appear and answer within 3 days (CCP 1167) (usually printed form) 

(3) Actions to Enforce Power of Eminent 

Domain Summons must contain a description of property, a statement of the public use for which it is sought, etc. (CCP 1245) 

(4) Actions Against Joint Debtors 

The summons is issued upon affidavit and it must describe the judgment and require the person summoned to show cause why he should not be bound by it. (It is not necessary to file a new complaint) 990) (CCP

(5) Actions to Quiet Title Against Unknown 

Owners Procedure

Summons must contain a description of the property and a statement of the object of the action (CCP 750) (1) Prepare same number of copies as of complaint 

(2) Fill in printed form of summons as follows: 

(a) Names of plaintiffs and defendants in head- (b) ing (Only first defendant need be named, rest may be designated by "et al.")

Names of all defendants in the body. (c) Last names of first plaintiff and defendant on back of printed form and "et al." (3) Attach copies of summons to copies of complaint.

Do NOT attach original summons to original complaint. (4) Have original summons issued at time of filing complaint (summons may be issued at any time within one year after filing of complaint). (5) Fill in number, date and clerk's name on copies (6) Serve a copy of the summons attached to a copy of the complaint on each defendant. (See SER 

VICE OF SUMMONS, p 38) Note: 

Summons must be served and return made within three years from date of filing complaint. (CCP 581a) (7) File original summons with affidavit of service

SERVICE OF SUMMONS AND COMPLAINT 

(CCP 410-416) Explanation 

Personal service must be made when possible. If not possible, then service may be made by publication. 

Must be made and return made thereon within 3 years after commencement of action. (CCP 581a)

May be transmitted by telegraph for service in any place and return may be made on the telegraphic copy. (CCP 1017)

Personal Service (CCP 410) (1) BY WHOM and HOW SERVED 

Sheriff, constable, marshal or any citizen over age of 18 not a party to the action, by handing a copy of summons and complaint to defendant 

Note: Exhibiting original summons unneces sary.

For personal service outside state see SERVICE BY PUBLICATION, p 41-43 (2) WHOM TO SERVE (CC 373; CCP 411) (a) Domestic corporation which is not a bank, trust company, insurance company, or subject to Railroad Commission (CC 373) 

Secretary of State or his assistant or deputy unless such corp. has filed certificate stating name and complete address of its natural person agent and officers; if certificate is filed then serve any so designated agent or officer; provided, that if service on such agent or officer cannot be made with exercise of due diligence, court may order service on Secretary of State, his assistant or deputy. Such service is deemed personal; corporation has 30 days to appear after delivery of papers to Secretary of State.

(b) Insurance Co. or corporation subject to

Railroad Commission (CCP 411) 

President or other head of corporation, a vice-president, a secretary, an assistant secretary, general manager or a person designated or authorized to receive service of process (c) Bank or trust company (CCP 411) 

Same as insurance company or to a cashier or assistant cashier (d) Foreign corporations (CC 406a and CCP 1018)

Substantially same as domestic corporation if designated officer or agent is found in this state. If no designated person is found in this state then Secretary of State. 

If bank, serve Supt. of Banks (Sec. 7, Act 

652, General Laws). (e) Minor under 14 (CCP 373(2); 411(3)) 

Minor personally and father, mother or guardian, or if none, the person having his care or control, or with whom he resides, or by whom he is employed (f) Incompetent person (CCP 373(3); 411(4)) 

Incompetent person and guardian (g) County, city or town (CCP 411(5))

Chairman Board of Supervisors; Presi dent of Council or Trustees (h) Corporation charter forfeited (CC 402a; 

CCP 411(6)) 

Trustees and stockholders or members (i) An officer of a corporation individually, and the corporation

Said officer must be served with two

copies, one for corporation and other for himself individually. (Service on corp. must comply with CC 373) (j) Copartnership

If a judgment is desired against several copartners individually, each must be served as an individual, in addition to the copartnership by serving any member thereof as such (k) All other cases Defendant personally 

When service is on a nonresident owner or operator of a motor vehicle involved in an accident, the service is made on the 

Director of Motor Vehicles and a notice of service and copy of summons and complaint is sent by registered mail to defendant or served personally on defendant, and there should be filed affidavit to show such service on director and affidavit of mailing together with return receipt or the return of a duly qualified public officer showing personal service on the defendant in whatever state or jurisdiction he may be; all to conform to Section 404, Vehicle Code. (A fee of $2 must be paid to Director for each nonresident to be so served.) 

(3) PROOF OF SERVICE (CCP 415) 

(a) If by sheriff, marshal or constable, file his certificate or affidavit of service (b) If by any other person, his affidavit (printed form on back of summons is usually used)

(c) Written admission of defendant endorsed on original summons or separate paper containing caption of case and acknowledged before a notary (ex.) Service of summons in the above entitled action this............day of admitted. 

19......, at 

California, is hereby (Notarial acknowledgment) Defendant (d) If by publication-see p 43 

Service by Publication (CCP 412, 413, 415, 416, 585)

Service of summons may be made by publication in certain instances. Personal service out of state is equivalent to publication and mailing after order for publication is obtained 

1. Permissible when defendant (a) Resides out of state (b) Has departed from state (c) After due diligence cannot be found within state (d) Conceals himself to avoid service (e) Is a corporation of which no officer or agent can be found in the state after due diligence (but see CC 373, 406a, CCP 1018) (f) Is unknown in an action to determine adverse claims to, or for partition of real property (CCP 750, 757) 

2. Procedure for PUBLICATION after filing complaint and issuance of summons (a) Fill out printed forms of or type

Affidavit for publication (see Contents of affidavit, p 44)

Order for publication for filing †1 original for filing 

 (b) Present original affidavit and order to presiding judge for signature on order (c) Send copy of summons to newspaper for publication (d) In compliance with court order mail copy of summons and complaint (by registered mail in counties where so required) to defendant at last known address

Important-publication and mailing must follow immediately after order is obtained (e) Proof of Service

Publisher files affidavit of publication. 

Person designated in order files affidavit of mailing (with registry receipt attached in counties where required) (f) Statutory time within which defendant must appear 30 days after completion of publication (g) Prepare default entry by court, for judge's signature at hearing (h) Hearing-balance of procedure same as uncontested civil actions (or set for trial if defendant answers) 

3. Procedure for PERSONAL SERVICE OUT OF STATE 

Personal service out of state is equivalent to publication and deposit in mail (CCP 413) (a) Obtain order for publication—see 2-a, b, above

(b) Immediately after obtaining order mail to sheriff at county seat of county where defendant resides, if defendant is to be personally served out of state and his residence is known: (1) Copy of summons and complaint (2) Check to cover approximate service fee (3) Affidavit of service for his return (Certificate by sheriff of another state is NOT sufficient return) (to avoid loss, it is best NOT to send original summons for sheriff's return) 

Note: Some prefer to wait until service is made and bill for service fee is received before sending check and form of affidavit of service-some sheriffs have their own forms for returns (c) Proof of service

File sheriff's affidavit of service with original or alias summons (d) Subsequent to 30 days after expiration of time prescribed by order for publication (ordinarily 60 days) case may be set for trial, date of actual service out of state being equivalent of commencement of publication (e) Prepare default entry by court, for judge's signature at hearing (f) Hearing and balance of procedure same as uncontested civil actions (or set for trial if defendant answers)

Contents of Affidavit for Publication (CCP 412) (must contain facts, not conclusions) (a) When defendant resides out of state Statement upon personal and positive knowledge that defendant resides at a specified place and information upon which such statement of residence is made. 

If information is contained in a letter, affidavit must state the fact and give substance of letter or copy of letter; original letter must be attached to the affidavit, if court rules require same. Remove letters from envelopes before attaching to affidavits.

An envelope postmarked out of the state does not, merely of itself, show that the party who mailed it RESIDES out of the state (an itinerant traveller may mail an envelope). Statements in a letter may show the party's place of residence or continued correspondence may show it; such an envelope may tend to show that a previously known to exist residence has continued; in any event there must be something beyond a single postmarked envelope. 

The information must be recent (b) If defendant has departed from the state 

Statement upon personal and positive knowledge that defendant has departed from the state and all information upon which such statement is made

(c) When defendant cannot be found within state after due diligence 

Must show that defendant has not filed certificate of residence required by CC 1163, or sheriff, etc., must certify that he cannot be found at address given therein (CCP 412) 

Must show with accuracy and considerable detail all that has been done in the effort to locate defendant, such as 

Search of telephone directories 

City directory

County tax collector's record Register of voters, and any other possible source of information and all inquiries made as to whereabouts 

Give names and addresses of persons from whom inquiries were made and dates of inquiries (d) Conceals himself to avoid service 

Must show facts from which the court may infer both that defendant conceals himself and that he does so in order to avoid service of summons

Note: In ordinary civil actions execution on a judgment based upon service by publication can be levied only on property which has been subjected to the jurisdiction of the court in that particular action by attachment levied prior to the entry of judgment; sometimes the property sought to be subjected to such jurisdiction is described in the complaint with appropriate allegations declaring that

it will be subjected to the lien of any judgment procured in the action (15 Cal Jur 234).

By Chapter 484, Statutes of 1939, Section 149 has been added to the Civil Code providing that in divorce actions based on constructive service (service by publication) the court without the levy of attachment or appointment of a receiver, shall have jurisdiction of community real property in the state the same as though the spouse had been personally served with process in this state. 

Explanation APPEARANCE 

(CCP 1014)

A defendant appears in an action when he answers, demurs, or gives plaintiff written notice of his appearance, or when an attorney gives notice of appearance for him. After appearance a defendant or his attorney is entitled to notice of all subsequent proceedings of which notice is required to be given. 

Statutory Time in which defendant has to appear after service of summons and complaint: 

10 days if service made in county (3 days in Unlawful Detainer and Forcible Entry cases) (CCP 1167) 30 days if service made in state, outside county, or on 

Secretary of State for a corporation 

90 days elsewhere (30 days after completion of publication-publication usually runs for 60 days) (see SUMMONS- 

SERVICE BY PUBLICATION, p 41) 

Kinds of Appearances (1) GENERAL 

Answer to complaint 

Demurrer to complaint 

Filing notice of appearance 

A stipulation

(2) SPECIAL 

When defendant appears for a specific purpose only, such as to object to the jurisdiction of the court or sufficiency of notice Applying to Court for any relief which can be granted only if court has jurisdiction, constitutes a general appearance and waives objection to jurisdiction of persons (3 Cal. Jur. 13). Except that Court may conditionally extend time to plead. (See Davenport v. Superior Court (1920), 183 

Cal 506, 511; Chilcote v. Pacific Air Transport (1937), 24 CA (2d) 32, 35) Procedure General 

See ANSWER, p 54, DEMURRER, p 49 Special (a) Prepare notice of special appearance and motion; affidavit and order extending time to plead +1 original 

1 copy to serve upon opposing counsel 

1 copy for office (b) Set motion for hearing (see court rules) (c) Serve copy on opposing counsel (d) File original with proof of service 

Fee Municipal Court $1.00 library fee plus $1.00 each appearing defendant

Superior Court $1.00 library fee plus $1.00 each appearing defendant 

Justice's Court None (e) Hearing (f) Prepare order (same number as notice (a) (g) Have order signed by judge (h) Serve copy of order on opposing counsel (i) File original with proof of service

DEMURRER TO COMPLAINT 

(CCP 430-31) Explanation

A demurrer is a pleading objecting to defects appearing on the face of a complaint (or answer) and creates issues of law only; it alleges nothing. The defendant may demur to the complaint within the time required in the summons to answer. A demurrer is statutory and may be taken only on one or more of the grounds specified in the CCP.

Caution: When a partnership is sued be sure to answer or demur as case may be, for the firm as well as for the individual partners. (CCP 388)

5. Signature of attorney

7. Memorandum of points and authorities 

Note: A demurrer avers no facts and is never verified Procedure

ANSWER TO COMPLAINT COUNTERCLAIM 

(CCP 437) An answer is a pleading by which the defendant appears and answers plaintiff's complaint within the time required in the summons, (or as extended by written. stipulation or court order).

A counterclaim is a part of the defense and either defeats or diminishes plaintiff's cause of action; it is a part of the answer. If defendant omits to plead a counterclaim he may not thereafter maintain an action against plaintiff therefor. (CCP 439) If defendant pleads a counterclaim in an amount in excess of jurisdiction of court, the court shall transfer case to a court of proper jurisdiction. (CCP 396)

A cross complaint should never be included in the answer, but should be filed as a separate document.

However, if it is erroneously so included, it should be answered the same as though filed separately. 

Caution: When a partnership is sued be sure to answer or demur as case may be, for the firm as well as for the individual partners. (CCP 388)

Contents 

1. Caption, same as complaint 

2. Title (Answer to Complaint) 3. Introduction 

4. General and specific denials of the material allegations controverted by defendant 

5. Statement of new matter constituting a defense 6. Statement of new matter constituting a counter claim 

7. Prayer 

8. Signature of attorney 

9. Verification, when required (see VERIFICA- 

TIONS, p 463) (a) If complaint is verified, the answer must be verified 

EXCEPT Class B Justices' Courts (CCP 

446), and 

When state, county or city or officer thereof is answering defendant (CCP 446) (b) If state, county or city, or officer thereof is plaintiff, answer must ordinarily be verified. 

NOTE re Written Instrument 

In Complaint

When an action is brought upon a written instrument, and the complaint contains a copy of such instrument, or a copy is annexed thereto, the genuineness and due execution of such instrument are deemed admitted, unless the answer denying the same be verified. (CCP 447, 449)

Procedure 1. Prepare 

In Answer

All affirmative allegations in an answer are deemed denied except when the answer contains a copy of a written instrument the genuineness and due execution of such instrument are deemed admitted unless affidavit denying same is served and filed within 10 days. (CCP 448) †1 original to file 

1 copy to serve on plaintiff's attorney 

2. Serve copy on opposing counsel (see GENERAL 

SERVICE, p 445) 

3. File original with proof of service 

FEE If first appearance of party (a) Superior Court (Pol C 4300a) $1.00 law library, plus $1.00 for each answering defendant (b) Municipal Court (B & PC 6322) $1.00 law library, plus $1.00 for each answering defendant (c) Justices' Courts (Pol C 4300e)

None 4. After answer has been filed, either party may request the action to be set for trial (see SETTING FOR 

TRIAL, p 101) 

5. Plaintiff need not reply to a counterclaim 

6. Plaintiff MUST reply to cross complaint, whether included in answer or set up separately, otherwise default on cross complaint may be entered, and this may end the lawsuit.

CROSS COMPLAINT 

(CCP 442) If defendant seeks affirmative relief against any person relating to or depending upon the contract, transaction, matter or accident, upon which the action is brought, he may, in addition to his answer, but not as a part thereof, file a cross complaint at the same time, or later by permission of the court. 

A cross complaint must be answered within same time as an original complaint. It should never be included in the answer, but should be filed as a separate document. 

However, if it is erroneously so included it must be answered the same as though filed separately.

Contents 

1. Caption, may be same as complaint, unless new parties are added. (see Forms, pp 63-65) 

2. Title (Cross Complaint) 

3. Introduction 

4. Allegations (For the sake of convenience and to avoid confusion, if parties are the ones named in original complaint refer to them by their original designation of plaintiff and defendant) 

5. Prayer 

6. Signature of attorney 

7. Verification (see VERIFICATIONS, p 463) Procedure 

1. Prepare +1 original to file 

1 copy to serve on each cross defendant or his attorney 

2. Serve copy of cross complaint on each cross defendant personally or on his attorney 

Note: If any party affected by the cross complaint has not appeared in the action, summons on the cross complaint must be issued and copy of summons and cross complaint served upon him, in the same manner as summons and complaint are served. If he has appeared, serve his attorney.

3.

(a) File original with proof of service as to original parties (see GENERAL SERVICE, p 445) 

No filing fee (b) File original, have summons issued for parties not named in original complaint, and serve a copy of summons attached to copy of cross complaint in same manner as original summons 4. Cross defendant has the same time within which to demur or answer the cross complaint as in the case of an original complaint

AMENDED PLEADINGS 

(CCP 432, 472, 473, 389)

An AMENDED PLEADING is a pleading which is entirely rewritten and is used in place of and supersedes the original or preceding pleading.

An AMENDMENT TO A PLEADING is merely a pleading (part of a pleading) setting forth an addition, substitution or change in the original pleading relating to matters occurring prior to commencement of the action; it does not supersede, but it together with the

original constitutes the whole of that particular pleading. 

The court may allow a party to amend any pleading or proceeding by adding or striking out the name of any party, or by correcting a mistake in the name of a party or a mistake in any other respect; or an entire cause of action may be repleaded to state additional facts. (Matters occurring after commencement of action are set out in supplemental pleading)

Verification as a general rule an amended pleading or an amendment to a pleading is verified the same as the original. Procedure 

Pleadings may be amended as follows: 

1. Without Leave of Court, before appearance of adverse party or before trial of issue of law raised by demurrer (a) First Amended Complaint

If the complaint is amended prior to the filing of an answer or demurrer or hearing on a demurrer, a first amended complaint may be filed without first obtaining leave of court and must be served upon the defendant the same as the original complaint (or upon his counsel if he has appeared by counsel) (no new summons need be issued unless there is a change in or addition to the parties) (b) First Amended Answer 

May be filed without first obtaining leave of court before trial of an issue of law upon a demurrer to answer or before time for filing demurrer thereto has elapsed

Note: A demurrer, affidavit of merits on motion for change of venue, summons, writ of attachment, affidavit and undertakings, etc., may also be amended. (CCP 128 (8); re Class B Justice's Court amendment, etc., see CCP 472, 473)

2. By Court Order After Hearing on Demurrer 

Within the time ordered by the court. Time runs from service of notice of court's ruling or from date of entry of waiver of notice in minutes (a) Prepare amended pleading or amendment †1 original for filing 

1 copy to be served on opposing counsel or adverse party 

 (b) Serve copy on opposing counsel or party (c) File original with proof of service 

3. By ex parte motion (a) Substituting true name for fictitious name (CCP 474)

SUPPLEMENTAL PLEADINGS 

Explanation 

Complaint and Answer (CCP 464, 465)

A party may be allowed, on motion or on stipulation, to file a supplemental complaint or answer alleging facts material to the case occurring after the former complaint or answer has been filed. A supplemental pleading may be filed at any time before judgment.

A supplemental pleading is merely an extension of or addition to the case made by the original pleading and it can only bring up matters which occur after the commencement of the action. It is not in any legal sense an amendment to the original pleading. The original and amended pleadings deal only with matters occurring prior to the filing of the original; a supplemental pleading deals only with matters occurring subsequent to the filing of the original.

Contents 

Same general form as original pleading but alleges only the new material. (see forms)

Verification Supplemental pleadings should be verified in same manner as originals. (see VERIFICA- TIONS, p 463) Answer to Supplemental Complaint-Supplemental complaint must be answered, otherwise allegations will be deemed to be admitted. Procedure 

1. Prepare (a) Supplemental Pleading †1 original to file with proof of service (after order is made) 

1 to attach to original notice 1 to attach to copy of notice which is served 

1 to serve (after order is made) 

 (b) Notice of Motion for Leave to File Supplemental Complaint (Answer). (must state time and place of hearing as well as ground and basis of motion) †1 original to attach to original affidavit and copy of amended pleading 

1 copy to serve 

1 office copy (c) Affidavit on Motion for Leave to File Supplemental Complaint (Answer) †1 original to file with proof of service 

1 copy to serve 

1 office copy

In assembling-attach copy of supplemental pleading and original affidavit to original notice, the notice being on top and the cover or top paper where no covers are used, must show the nature or title of each of the attached documents 

Attach copies of same for service 

2. Serve copies on opposing counsel at least 5 days before hearing (see CCP 1010, 1011, et seq.) 

3. File originals of notice and affidavit and COPY of supplemental pleading (under one cover), with proof of service 

4. At hearing see that minute order or written order granting leave to file supplemental complaint (answer) is entered 5. If not accomplished at hearing, serve copy and file original supplemental pleading in accordance with court order Forms

IV 

Explanation

ATTACHMENTS 

(CCP 537-561) Attachment is a process by which real or personal property of a party to an action is seized and held in the custody of the law as security for satisfaction of any judgment that may be obtained in the pending litigation. The instances in which an attachment may issue are enumerated in CCP 537; the cases most commonly occurring are: (1) Actions based upon unsecured contracts, express or implied, made or payable in this state calling for the direct payment of money. (Liability imposed by law upon a relative is deemed to have arisen upon an implied contract) (2) An action upon a contract, express or implied, or to recover damages for injury to property in this state, against a person not residing in this state or who cannot after due diligence be found within it (3) In unlawful detainer proceedings wherein recovery of past due unsecured rent is sought (4) In actions by the state of California for the collection of taxes or obligations imposed by law The writ of attachment may issue at the time of issuing the summons or at any time thereafter prior to judgment. (Alias writ may issue if original lost. CCP 

5592.) No attachment will be issued where the amount sought, exclusive of interest, is less than $15.00. (CCP 538)

Note: Levy of the attachment may be avoided by giving a bond or undertaking in an amount approved by the court, or depositing cash with the levying officer in an amount sufficient to satisfy plaintiff's demand, with costs, or in an amount equal to the value of the property about to be attached. (CCP 540)

Defendant may have property released from attachment by posting bond as provided in CCP 554-5. (For de tails see RELEASE OF ATTACHMENT, pp 85-87) Procedure in Attachment Action 

1. Prepare or obtain (a) Complaint and Summons (See COM- PLAINT, generally, pp 26-34, or index for specific type, AND SUMMONS, p 34) (b) Affidavit for Attachment (printed form for CCP 537 (1)) +1 original 

Must show under CCP 538: (1) Statement that action is for money due on contract, express or implied (CCP 537 (1)), or facts appropriate to the particular subdivision under which the remedy is sought (2) Amount of indebtedness claimed over and above all legal set offs or counterclaims (CCP 538 (2)) (3) That attachment is not sought, and action is not prosecuted to hinder, delay, or defraud any creditor of defendant (CCP 538 (3))

(4) In cases where earnings of judgment debtor which would be exempt under provisions of CCP 690.11 are sought to be attached on ground that action is brought to collect a debt incurred for common necessaries of life, or for personal services rendered by employee or former employee, affidavit must contain a statement to that effect and must specify the character or nature of the items constituting the alleged necessaries or services rendered (c) Writ of Attachment (CCP 540) †1 original 1 copy for each levy to be made, for levying officer 

 if real estate is attached 1 copy for each piece of real estate and an additional copy to be recorded if real property sought to be attached is registered under Torrens Title Act, an additional copy of the writ with a description of the Torrens Title Certi ficate, a description of the property, and a notice that it is attached shall be filed with Registrar of Titles of county (d) Undertaking, or Bond for Defendant on At- . tachment in an amount fixed by clerk, judge or justice (CCP 539, 1041, 1054 a, 1057) (1) By surety company bond (CCP 1041, 1056, 1057, 1057 a) or

(2) By 2 or more property owners (printed form) (CCP 1041, 1057) 

Where amount is in excess of $2000, the affidavit on bond must set out sureties' residences, description of their property, etc., as provided in CCP 1057 or (3) Lawful money (CCP 1054a) (4) Not less than $50.00 nor more than amount demanded in complaint or amount fixed by court order (CCP 539) (e) Special undertaking if levy on bank deposit or safe deposit box not wholly in name of defendant (CCP 539a)

If attachment is to be levied on bank deposit or property in safe deposit box not wholly in name of defendant, separate bond in twice amount of claim must be provided and delivered to bank, trust company or safe deposit corporation, concurrently with levy of writ, otherwise attachment is ineffective for any purpose (f) Instructions to Marshal, Sheriff or other levying officer (CCP 542, 543) †1 original 

1 copy for levying officer 

 (1) If real estate is to be attached include legal description of property and street address, and (2) State whether or not it is registered under Torrens Title

(3) If personal property, describe the same, give street address where located, the name of person in possession, and state whether capable of manual delivery (4) If levy to be on bank deposit or safe deposit box not wholly in name of defendant, provide the special bond and direct levying officer to deliver concurrently with levy 

2. Give to Clerk †Original (a) Complaint and summons (b) Affidavit for attachment (c) Writ of attachment (d) Undertaking for defendant on attachment, bond, or cash; and filing fee for complaint (see COMPLAINT, pp 26-34) 

3. Have Clerk issue (a) Original summons (b) Original writ of attachment (several writs may be issued to different counties CCP 540) 

4. Give to Marshal, Sheriff or levying officer (a) Original and at least one copy of writ of attachment for each levy to be made (see 

Writ of Attachment p 81) (b) If levy to be on bank deposit or safe deposit box not wholly in name of defendant, bond to garnishee in double amount of claim (CCP 539a)

(c) Original instructions (d) Check to cover costs

FEES: Consult levying officer, or Pol Code 4300 (b) (d) (1) (B & PC 6322) (e) Original summons and copies of summons and complaint if service thereof is to be made at same time 

Exception to Sureties

If undertaking is by personal sureties, it is frequently advisable (and sometimes if surety is a corporation) from the defendant's standpoint for him to require them to come into court and prove that they are financially responsible. This must be done within 5 days after learning of attachment by filing Notice of Exception to Sureties, and serving copy on opposing counsel. (CCP 

539) (for justification of corporate securities see CCP 1057a) If defendant fails so to do he is deemed to have waived all objections to the sureties.

When excepted to, plaintiff's sureties must justify within 5 days from service of notice of exception, and such justification must be upon notice to defendant of not less than 2 nor more than 5 days. 

CAUTION: Upon failure so to justify within such time and on such notice, the attachment will be dissolved. 

NOTE: Exceptions to sufficiency of sureties on garnishee's bond may be taken by person claiming to be rightful owner and sureties must justify as provided in CCP 539.

DATED: 

19....... Garnishment 

Attorney for Defendant

An attachment whereby money, property or credits of debtor in possession of, or owed by, another may be subjected to payment of creditor's claims. Procedure same as other attachments (CCP 541, 542 (6)) except for special bond as required by CCP 539a where levy is on bank deposit or safe deposit box as noted above under procedure.

RELEASE OR DISCHARGE OF ATTACHMENT 

An attachment may be released or discharged by one of the following procedures: 

1. On order of plaintiff upon satisfaction of claim by defendant (or other settlement) (a) Prepare †(original and office copy) Order to Sheriff or Marshal to Release (b) Give original to marshal or sheriff, or to defendant who will give to levying officer (c) Pay levying officer's fee

2. By giving an undertaking for the release of attachment in whole or in part (CCP 554, 555) Prepare 

Notice of motion for discharge of attachment in whole or in part, Affidavit in support thereof Undertaking for release of attachment Serve

Copy of notice and affidavit on plaintiff File

Original notice and affidavit with proof of service and undertaking

Plaintiff in such case may require justification of the defendant's sureties. The undertaking must be given in an amount fixed by the court upon making of the order for release of the property and will be in an amount appropriate to the value of the property to be released. 

3. On order of court after motion by defendant for discharge of writ for defect in attachment proceedings (CCP 556, 558) 

Prepare, serve and file notice of motion and affidavit in support thereof in same manner as papers for other motions (see MOTIONS- 

NOTICES, pp 455-460) 

4. On Third Party Claim (a) Prepare affidavit of third party claimant 

(see THIRD PARTY CLAIM, pp 87-92) 

(b) Give to levying officer who attached property

5. By levying officer after claim of conditional sale vendor and on failure to make tender or give bond as provided in CCP 689b 

6. Claim of Exemption 

(a) Prepare +1 original 

 (b) Give to levying officer who attached

Explanation property 

THIRD PARTY CLAIM 

(CCP 519, 549, 689)

If personal property levied on is claimed by a third person as his property, he may file with the levying officer a verified claim signed by himself or agent. 

CAUTION: CCP section 689a, 689b and 689c were added in 1921 and are not amendatory of 689. Proceedings under said sections are not to be confused with third party claim proceedings under 689. Procedure 1. Prepare 

Affidavit of Third Party Claim

This affidavit constitutes the pleading upon which the claim may be tried out, if necessary, it being the duty of the officer to file it with the court if the property is not released.

The claim must describe or identify the property attached, state its reasonable value and set out in detail facts showing the claimant's title and right to possession. Claim must be verified by owner or agent. †1 original to give levying officer 

1 copy to give levying officer 

1 office copy

2. Levying officer notifies plaintiff's attorney of third party claim. 

3. Within 5 days after notice of the filing of the claim and demand by the levying officer, plaintiff's attorney must file with levying officer a bond (CCP 689) in double the value of property levied on, if he wishes to keep same under attachment. Consult levying officer as to bond requirements. 

Within 5 days after receipt of notice of the filing of the undertaking, EXCEPTIONS to the sufficiency of the sureties may be filed and their justification required in the same manner as upon an undertaking on attachment. (See ATTACHMENTS, p 79)

If plaintiff fails to furnish or maintain sufficient bond, levying officer shall deliver property to defendant, or if defendant cannot be found after 10 days' notice to last known address property may be delivered to third party claimant. (CCP 689.5) 

4. The petition of plaintiff or third party claimant for hearing to determine ownership of the property must be served on levying officer and filed within 10 days after delivery of the claim to levying officer and hearing MUST BE HELD WITHIN 20 

DAYS after filing of such petition, unless continued by court. 

10 days notice of hearing must be given the officer and adverse counsel and third party claimant. 

On the hearing the third party claimant has the burden of proof.

The court upon the filing of petition may forbid the transfer of the property until the hearing to determine title is terminated and may require a bond as a condition of such order.

Consult court rules as to setting matters of this nature for hearing.

Forms

Explanation CLAIM AND DELIVERY 

(CCP 509-521) Claim and delivery is an optional proceeding. Plaintiff in an action to recover the possession of personal property may, at the time of issuance of summons, or at any time before answer, claim the delivery of such property to him.

Procedure 

1. Prepare (a) Complaint in Claim and Delivery, and Sum mons (see COMPLAINT and SUMMONS, pp 26-37) +1 original 

1 copy for service on each defendant 

 (b) Affidavit showing the facts required by CCP 

510, 511 †1 original 

1 copy for service on each defendant 

1 office copy 

1 copy for posting (if required) (c) Undertaking on Claim and Delivery (CCP 

512) or obtain surety or personal bond or deposit money in lieu of undertaking (CCP 1054a) 

(Printed Form) 

†1 original 

1 copy for service on each defendant 

1 office copy 

1 copy for posting if keeper ordered 

2. File Original Complaint and have summons issued

3. File with levying officer (a) Affidavit (b) Undertaking

Copy of each for each defendant, together with complaint and summons if same are to be served by such officer. 

FEE: Consult levying officer for approximate cost.

The levying officer seizes the property and defendant has 5 days to reclaim and get the property by posting bond for double its value. If he does not post such bond the property is delivered to plaintiff by levying officer at the end of 5-day period.

If a third party claims title to property plaintiff must give officer an additional bond to protect him against the third party claim. Otherwise, property will be released. If plaintiff puts up this additional bond and officer keeps the property, the third party can get it immediately only by bringing claim and delivery suit against officer. Sometimes it is practically as quick and more desirable to intervene in the original action rather than file an independent action.

Forms 

Affidavit on Claim and Delivery-printed 

Undertaking on Claim and Delivery—printed Explanation

INJUNCTIONS 

An injunction is an order requiring a person to refrain from a particular act. When may or may not be granted-see CCP 526, 526a, 526b, and CC 3369, 3422, 3423. 

An injunction may be granted in a pending action at any time before judgment upon a verified complaint or upon affidavits.

Procedure 

1. Prepare (a) Complaint (see COMPLAINT and SUM- 

MONS, pp 26-37) (b) Affidavits, when necessary (c) (1) Temporary restraining order and order to show cause, or (2) Notice (if temporary restraining order is not sought notice is sufficient) (d) Points and authorities (if short, may follow immediately after order to show cause or notice; if lengthy, in separate document) †1 original of each of above to file 1 copy for service on each adverse party 

2. Undertaking, Surety Co. Bond, Personal Bond or Cash 

Temporary restraining order may be granted with or without undertaking at discretion of judge 

Preliminary injunction cannot be granted without undertaking in amount fixed by court Not required in action by one spouse against the other in action for divorce or separate maintenance or in action by state or county or municipal corporation (CCP 529) 

3. File original complaint and have summons issued. 

4. If notice of application for preliminary injunction is used file original with proof of service at least 1 day before date of hearing

5. If temporary restraining order is sought present original complaint, affidavits when required, temporary restraining order, order to show cause and points and authorities to judge

If order is signed by judge he will retain all original documents for filing

Date of hearing must be within 10 days after issuance of order. 

6. Serve copy of each on each party who is sought to be restrained at least 5 days before hearing, unless shorter time is fixed in the order 

7. File proof of service 

8. If points and authorities are not served 2 days before date fixed for hearing court must dissolve temporary restraining order 

9. Hearing (a) If restraining order is in force party procuring same must be ready to proceed on date fixed for hearing. Adverse party is entitled to 1 continuance as of course (b) Adverse party may make return by verified answer or by affidavits, and may demur to complaint (c) If adverse party serves affidavits less than 

2 days before date of hearing, party procuring restraining order is entitled to 1 continuance for the purpose of preparing affidavits in reply (d) Hearing may be had on affidavits or on evidence produced in court. Usually affidavits are preferred by court

Preliminary Injunction

At the time of hearing, if preliminary injunction is granted, court will fix amount of undertaking and should fix the time for filing same and make order that restraining order remain in effect during such period Explanation

RECEIVERS (CCP 564-570) 

When may be appointed-see CCP 564, 565 and CC 140. A receiver may be appointed upon application of any party to the action upon a verified complaint, pleading, or upon affidavits. Procedure 

1. Prepare (a) Complaint, answer, or cross complaint, as case may require and have same verified (b) Affidavits, when necessary (c) Order appointing receiver, setting out fully his powers and duties, if receiver is appointed on ex parte application. In such case the order, in counties where rules so require should contain an order to show cause returnable within 10 days directing adverse party to show cause why the order appointing receiver should not be confirmed. (If ex parte order is not desired order appointing receiver is not prepared until after hearing in court.) (d) Points and authorities †1 original of each of above 1 copy for service on each adverse party

2. Present original pleading, affidavits, order and points and authorities to judge 3. If he issues order he will sign and file same (in counties where rules require order to show cause, judge will fix date of hearing) 4. If receiver is appointed on ex parte application, court must require an undertaking from the applicant in an amount fixed by the court 

5. Receiver's oath and undertaking

Before entering upon his duties, the receiver must sign and file his oath and execute an undertaking in the amount fixed by the court (CCP 567) 6. In counties where rules require order to show cause adverse party may make return as in case of injunctions (see INJUNCTIONS, p 94) 

7. Receiver's Sale

Not final until confirmed by court (CCP 568.5) 

8. Receiver's final report

Receiver should file his final account and petition for discharge as soon as possible after his duties have terminated by final judgment in the action or otherwise.

V 

A. Uncontested Cases Explanation

JUDGMENT BY DEFAULT 

(CCP 585, 586)

Default may be entered and a judgment by default obtained in the following instances: 

1. When defendant fails to answer or demur to complaint within prescribed statutory time: (a) 10 days from date of service in county (CCP 407) (b) 30 days from date of service in state and outside of county (CCP 407) (c) 30 days after serving corporation by serving Secretary of State (CC 373) (d) 3 days from date of service in unlawful detainer or forcible entry action (CCP 1167) (e) 30 days from date of last publication (CCP 413) (f) 90 days from date of service, if publication period is two service, (if months), if served personally out of state (CCP 413) (Order for publication must be obtained before personal service outside of state) 2. When defendant fails to answer amended complaint within time allowed by court (CCP 586) 3. When demurrer is overruled and defendant fails to answer within time allowed by court (CCP 

472b, 586) 4. When demurrer to answer is sustained and defendant fails to amend answer within time allowed by court (CCP 472-b, 586)

When demurrer is sustained and complaint is not amended within time allowed by court, case may be dismissed (see DISMISSALS, p 116)

Note: An order sustaining a demurrer is not appealable; time to appeal starts only when judgment of dismissal based on the order sustaining the demurrer is entered and notice given. Procedure 

Time to answer or amend starts to run only from service of notice of ruling or waiver thereof in minutes. (See generally 

CCP 1014, 432, 953d) 

1. Personal Service within state 

File original (a) Request for entry of default (Superior) Order to enter default (Municipal) (b) Summons with affidavit of service. (c) Memorandum of costs (d) Judgment. by default by clerk

Used where the amount demanded is based on contract or note (except in actions providing for reasonable attorney's fees) (or). (d) Judgment by default by court Used in other actions where the amount of money or nature of relief is determined by court (e) Note or contract-at time judgment is entered CIVIL (f) Military affidavit when required (See

SOLDIERS' AND SAILORS RELIEF ACT) 

Default Hearing-ex parte in open court (see 

Court Rules) 

2. Publication or personal service outside of state

Defendant's default is entered by court instead of clerk. 

Follow same procedure as for personal service.

Forms 

Except that if summons has been published, the affidavit of publication must be presented to the court, and if the order for publication directed mailing, an affidavit showing complete compliance with such order must be presented. If the summons was sent by registered mail and a return receipt has been received, it should be attached to the affidavit of mailing.

If the summons has been served personally on the defendant in another state or country, the proof of service should be by affidavit rather than certificate and should bear the seal of the officer administering the oath. (CCP 1875, 2013)

If judgment is to be enforced against real estate the same must be described in complaint or jurisdiction acquired by attachment (see ATTACH- 

MENTS p 79)

SETTING FOR TRIAL 

At any time after the answer to complaint (and answer to cross complaint if a cross complaint is filed) has been served and filed either party to an action may have the case set for trial. Procedure 

1. Prepare and serve

Memorandum for Setting for Trial (printed form)-(Superior Court) (Judicial Council Rule 

1) †1 original to be filed with proof of service 

1 copy to serve on opposing counsel

Request for Setting-(Municipal Court) 

†1 original for filing 

 (Service unnecessary) Forms generally require the following information (See Judicial Council Rule 1) Jury Fee (a) Nature of action and that it is at issue (b) Estimated time necessary for trial (c) Statement as to whether jury trial is requested 

If jury trial is requested, first day's jury fees must be posted with clerk 10 days prior to date set for trial (2 days in 

Justice's Court) (CCP 631) 

Note: FEE $24 or $36 per day as required by particular county. The mileage fees, if any, will be estimated by clerk and later collected from the attorney.

These are allowable costs for cost bill. (d) Dates not acceptable (in some counties) (e) Names, addresses and telephone numbers of attorneys, and signature of attorney making request 

Notice as to date set

When trial date is set, give notice to opposing counsel AT LEAST 5 DAYS BEFORE 

TRIAL. (See NOTICE OF TRIAL, p 105)

This is essential or trial cannot proceed except by stipulation. (Service of notice not necessary where waived and waiver is entered in clerk's minutes at hearing for purpose of setting date.)

2. A trial date may be set by stipulation, subject to approval by court Prepare (a) Stipulation †1 original to file after signed by all attorneys 

1 copy for opposing counsel for each adverse party
