Explanation SUBPOENAS (CCP 1985-1997) 

A subpoena is the process used to compel the attendance of a witness for the purpose of giving testimony at a trial, deposition, or other proceeding.

A subpoena duces tecum is a process by which a witness is compelled to bring with him specified books, documents or other things under his control. 

A witness cannot be compelled to appear out of the county in which he resides unless the distance be less than 100 miles from his residence to the place of trial. (CCP 1989) Procedure

Subpoena 

1. Fill out printed forms †1 original, including names of all witnesses 1 copy, or ticket, for each witness to be served 

2. Serve witness by (CCP 1987) 

Exhibiting original and

Handing him copy or ticket containing substance and 

Giving witness fee of $2.00 plus mileage, if demanded 

Note: Any person, including party to suit, may serve subpoena. 

3. File original with proof of service

Subpoena Duces Tecum (CCP 1985) 

The facts required by law must be fully and specifically stated or the subpoena may be disregarded. (See 

Shell Oil Company v. Superior Court, 1930, 109 CA 75) 1. Fill out printed form of affidavit, specifying and fully describing exact documents or things desired to be produced and setting forth in full detail the materiality thereof to the issues involved in the case, and stating that the witness has the desired documents or things in his possession or under his control. †1 original 

If for use on deposition, prepare extra copy as prescribed under subpoena re deposition, p 109; see form p 112. 

Note: Affidavit not necessary if subpoena is requested during trial after evidence has supplied the facts usually set forth in the affidavit. 

2. Fill out printed form of subpoena duces tecum which must specify and fully describe the exact documents or things desired to be produced as set forth in affidavit †1 original 1 copy for each party to be served 

1 office copy 3. File original affidavit and have original subpoena issued 

4. Service-same as subpoena 

5. File original with proof of service

Subpoena re Deposition (CCP 1985.5; re depositions see 2019 to 2038) 1. Fill out printed form of subpoena re deposition (printed in most counties) +1 original 

1 copy for service 

2. Prepare affidavit (see form p 112) usually to be signed by attorney of record †1 original 1 copy to be attached to copy of notice for service 

1 copy to attach to original notice 

1 office copy 

3. Prepare notice of taking deposition (CCP 2031) (See form p 114) †1 original 1 copy (with copy of affidavit attached) for service on opposing counsel; if party has not appeared, serve on party 

1 office copy 4. File original affidavit and have original subpoena re deposition issued by clerk 5. Serve copy of each of above documents on party if party has not appeared. If appearance has been made by attorney serve subpoena on party, and copy of notice and affidavit on opposing counsel. Notice must be served at least 5 days-subpoena a "reasonable time"-prior to taking deposition.

6. Deliver original subpoena, and notice with copy of affidavit with proof of service to notary to be attached to deposition (or file). 

Note: Upon showing by affidavit of reasonable ground therefor, the court may make order prescribing shorter time than 5 days. Besides affidavit prepare order shortening time. †1 original 

1 copy for service 

Have order signed by judge ex parte. Serve copy with other documents mentioned above on opposing counsel, or party who has not appeared, and also deliver original, with proof of service, to notary with other documents (or file). 

Note: Depositions are frequently taken by Stipulation, which obviates in most instances the preparation of the above mentioned papers.

Form (Caption same as complaint)

(Said testimony may be taken pursuant to the provisions of Section 2055 of the Code of Civil Pro cedure of the State of California.)

Explanation

DISMISSALS-NONSUITS 

(CCP 581-583)

Dismissal is the voluntary or involuntary discontinuance of an action otherwise than by judgment on the merits. Dismissals are sometimes spoken of as being "without prejudice" or "with prejudice." "Without prejudice" means that the party is free to file another action litigating on their merits the questions involved in the old action. An action dismissed generally renders nothing res judicata and therefore is no bar to further actions. To merely say that an action is dismissed "with prejudice" cannot bar further action, but may mean that an accord and satisfaction have been reached, or that a release has been executed. The circumstances under which an action may be or must be dismissed are enumerated in CCP 581-583.

When affirmative relief is sought in an action by counterclaim or cross complaint voluntary dismissal is barred unless same be consented to in writing, signed by attorney for defendant or defendants seeking the affirmative relief.

Nonsuit ordinarily means dismissal by the court at defendant's request when plaintiff's evidence, construed most favorably to him, fails to prove a prima facie case at the trial.

Voluntary Dismissal by Party 

1. By plaintiff at any time before trial (before submission, see Franks v. Cesena (1923), 192 Cal 1; 

9 Cal Jur 510) subject to payment of costs, provided defendant has not sought affirmative relief 2. By either party at any time with the written consent of the other party

Dismissal by Court 1. By court on motion of defendant when demurrer is sustained and complaint is not amended within time allowed by court 2. By court on motion of defendant when demurrer is sustained without leave to amend 

Note: An order sustaining a demurrer with or without leave to amend does not terminate the action and is not an appealable order; the action continues pending until the entry of a judgment of dismissal. (21 Cal Jur 126, 127; De la 

Beckwith v. Superior Court, 146 Cal 

496, 500.) 

Discretionary Dismissal (CCP 583),

The court may in its discretion dismiss upon motion of defendant after notice to plaintiff when plaintiff has failed to bring action to trial within 2 years after filing. (See also CCP 1190)

Mandatory Dismissal 

1. The court must dismiss upon motion of defendant, after notice to plaintiff (or on court's own motion) if case is not brought to trial within 5 years after filing of action, except where time has been extended by written stipulation (CCP 583) 2. The court must dismiss when, after judgment, new trial has been granted, if no appeal has been taken, upon motion of defendant, after notice to plaintiff, (or on its own motion) if case is not brought to trial within 3 years after entry of order granting new trial, unless time has been extended by written stipulation (CCP 583)

3. The court must dismiss when appeal has been taken and judgment reversed and cause remanded for new trial (or if the appeal is from order granting new trial and such order is affirmed on appeal) upon motion of defendant after notice to plaintiff (or on its own motion) unless brought to trial within 3 years from filing of remittitur in trial court 4. On court's motion or motion of any interested party when summons has not been issued within 

1 year after filing of complaint (if not dismissed, and defendant appears within 3 year period, case may be tried as if summons served (CCP 581a) 5. On court's motion or motion of any interested party if summons not served and returned within 

3 years after filing complaint (CCP 581a) 

6. On court's motion or motion of any party if summons served and no answer filed and plaintiff fails to have judgment entered within 3 years after service of summons (CCP 581a; but see Cook v. 

Justice's Court, 16 CA (2d) 745, for rule if clerk fails to perform ministerial act) 

7. On court's motion or motion of any interested party where action ordered transferred because not originally commenced in proper county where costs and fees of transmission or filing anew have not been paid for 1 year after entry of order of transfer (CCP 581b) 8. In actions within subject matter jurisdiction of 

Class B Justices' Courts whether commenced in

Justice's or Municipal Court and where facts showing proper court for trial of such action are not alleged in verified complaint or affidavit and defendant does not consent in writing or in open court by minute entry (but court may allow an amended complaint or affidavit to be filed after the filing of the complaint) (CCP 396a)

Procedure 

Voluntary Dismissal 

1. Where affirmative relief not demanded 

Fill out printed form "Dismissal" †1 original to file 

1 for opposing counsel (optional) 

2. Where affirmative relief has been demanded by answer or cross complaint 

Same as 1 except add written consent to dismissal to be signed by attorney for adverse party or accompany dismissal with a stipulation signed by both parties consenting to the dismissal

Dismissals on Motion-for forms of notice pp 455-6 Explanation

INSTRUCTIONS TO JURY 

(CCP 607a, 608, 609, 2061)

In all cases tried before the court with a jury, before the first witness is sworn, counsel for the respective parties must deliver to trial judge and serve upon opposing counsel all proposed instructions to the jury covering the law as disclosed by the pleadings. Thereafter, and before the court has commenced instructing, and before commencement of the argument, counsel may deliver to the judge, and serve upon opposing counsel, additional proposed instructions to the jury upon questions of law developed by the evidence and not disclosed by the pleadings.

Procedure As to criminal case instructions see Penal Code 1096, Form 

1096a, 1097, 1127, 1127b, 1127c Prepare †1 original for judge 

1 copy for opposing counsel 

1. Type each proposed instruction on a separate sheet of legal paper. 

2. Number each instruction. The numbering is necessary so that counsel, the trial court and appeal court, if an appeal is taken, may readily identify each instruction given and refused, by its number.

It is immaterial whether the instructions as proposed be numbered consecutively, the important thing being that each proposed instruction have its own identifying number. In offices maintaining a stock of current form instructions it is customary to identify such stock instructions with numbers and in such cases the stock instructions always bear the same numbers. 

In requesting instructions contained in the California Jury Instructions it is necessary only to request instructions by the numbers by which they are identified in such book. 

3. Citation In requesting an instruction which has been approved by a court of last resort, it is an approved practice to place at the bottom of instruction a citation of the case with exact page at which it is approved.

When instruction is copied from California Jury Instructions (Civil) in substance but is modified as applicable to the particular case, it is proper to put at bottom of instruction "BAJI #- as modified." 

4. Identify the party proposing the instruction with "Plaintiff's (defendant's) proposed instruction "This is very important in an appeal.

No..... No. -------- 

5. Do not repeat on each instruction "You are hereby instructed" or the equivalent thereof; it is unnecessary and purely cumbersome to do so. 

6. Instructions are uncaptioned (title page unnecessary) and unbound. For convenience instructions, which are designed for the judge to read to the jury, may be clasped (not stapled) together under a cover back and submitted with written or oral request that they be read to the jury.

Example: 

This may appear at top or bottom Plaintiff's Defendant's

Instruction No. 1 

(Unnecessary and cumbersome to have each instruction recite "You are instructed" or the equivalent thereof.) 

Judges find this form most convenient. They can indicate on appropriate line the exact disposition. (Penal Code 1127, CCP 609). (May be typed or more conveniently (See 13 on page 120) Given... 

Given as modified..........

Refused.......... stamped, on pro- Refused, except as covered by instrucposed instruc- tion) tions given.....

FINDINGS OF FACT AND CONCLUSIONS OF 

Explanation

LAW 

(CCP 632, 634)

In municipal courts written findings of fact and conclusions of law shall be deemed waived unless expressly requested by one or more of the parties at the time of the trial; provided, that the court shall not be required to make any written findings of fact and conclusions of law in cases in which the amount of the demand, exclusive of interest and costs, or the value of the property in controversy, does not exceed $300.00.

In superior courts findings are required unless specifically waived in writing or orally in open court, or by failure to appear.

Procedure 

1. Prevailing party (or as directed by court) prepares findings of fact and conclusions of law, within 24 days after the cause is submitted for decision (CCP 632-634) +1 original 

1 copy for each opposing counsel 

Caution: If every material issue in the case is not properly disposed of by the findings of fact the judgment may be reversed 2. Serve copy on opposing counsel (judgment is usually prepared and served at same time) 3. Submit original, with proof of service on opposing counsel, to judge who tried case

4. Opposing counsel has 5 days from date of service within which to file objections to the proposed findings and to submit proposed findings (CCP 634) 5. Hearing to settle findings-occasionally required 

6. Prevailing party (or as directed by court) must then prepare findings as settled by the court and judgment (if not already prepared) in accordance therewith for the court's signature 

7. See that findings and judgment are signed by judge and filed 

8. Clerk makes entry of judgment 

(See NOTICE OF ENTRY OF JUDGMENT, p 144) Form ?

No........... (Caption same

FINDINGS OF FACT 

as complaint) and

CONCLUSIONS OF LAW 

The above entitled cause came on regularly for trial on ment (division) before the Honorable 

19........, in departof the above entitled court, judge presiding, sitting without a jury, a jury having been expressly waived, attorney for plaintiff, and appearing as appearing as attorney for defendant, (and the case having been dismissed against (any unused fictitious names of defendants sued) and oral and documentary evidence having been introduced on behalf of both parties, and the court having considered the same and heard the arguments of counsel and being fully advised, makes the following findings of fact:

The allegations contained in paragraphs I, II and III of plaintiff's complaint are true. II

None of the allegations contained in paragraphs 

I, II, III, IV, or V of defendant's answer is true. III

Each allegation and denial in paragraph III of defendant's answer is untrue, except it is true Caution:

Every material issue raised by the pleadings must be disposed of by a pertinent finding. As precaution, some attorneys make a practice of adding to specific findings an omnibus finding substantially as follows: 

Except as otherwise hereinabove specifically found, all of the allegations of the complaint (answer) are true and none of the allegations (or denials) of the answer (complaint) is true.

JUDGMENT Trial by Jury (CCP 664) 

Judgment on Verdict 

Must be entered by clerk in conformity with verdict within 24 hours after rendition of verdict unless the court orders or grants a stay of proceedings.

Trial by Court 

(a) Superior Court

Unless findings of fact and conclusions of law have been waived (see page 120) the prevailing party (or as directed by the court) prepares findings of fact and conclusions of law, which when signed and filed constitute the decision of the court. Upon the filing of such decision or in the event of the waiver of the filing thereof, the judgment is signed and filed and thereafter entered by the clerk, in the judgment book. A judgment is effectual for no purpose until so entered (CCP 664). When entered, notice of the entry should be given (see NOTICE OF 

ENTRY OF JUDGMENT, p 144) 

A judgment becomes final when the time for appeal has expired and no appeal has been taken or when it has been affirmed on appeal by the court of last resort and the judgment on appeal has become final. As to time for appeals see 

APPEALS, p 160 et seq.

Procedure 

1. Prepare judgment †1 original 

1 service copy 

 and as many other copies as may be desired for certification, delivery to parties, etc. 

2. Serve copy on opposing counsel (ordinarily accompanies proposed findings of fact and conclusions of law) 

3. Submit original with proof of service on opposing counsel, to judge who tried case. 

4. If judgment is not signed when presented, make a note on calendar to ascertain from time to time as may be necessary when judgment has been signed and filed 5. When judgment has been signed and filed it is the duty of the clerk to enter it 

6. Check legal paper for a day or two following the filing to get book and page of entry. If fact of entry does not appear shortly, check records in clerk's office to ascertain that it has been entered or why not. 

7. Serve notice of entry of judgment on opposing counsel (see page 144)

(b) Municipal Court

In Municipal Courts findings of fact are deemed waived unless expressly requested (see page 

120) and the judgment is ordinarily prepared by the clerk of the court. A check should be made to be sure that the judgment is properly prepared and entered. Notice of entry of judgment must be served (see p 144) (c) Justices' Courts Judgment is entered in the docket by the justice or his clerk. It need not be prepared by the attorney (except in cases of judgment by default) but attorney does well to check its form. 

Note: The statute of limitations on judgments is 5 years from date of entry of judgment. A new suit must be instituted on the judgment before the expiration of the 5-year period if the client desires to have it renewed. (CCP 336, 681) 

It is possible that the action may be ruled to have been commenced in time if started within 

5 years after judgment has become final. See 

Stultz v. Bacon (1939) 35 CA (2) 648, 96 P (2d) 369; Feeney v. Hinckley (1901) 134 Cal 467 

BE SURE TO NOTE THIS EXPIRATION DATE ON CALENDAR 

Notwithstanding the elapse of 5 years from the date of its entry, upon motion and good cause shown, and after due notice to the judgment debtor, the court may in its discretion order execution to issue. (CCP 685)

Caution: Be sure judgment determines action on merits; a mere judgment for costs is a nullity.

Summary Judgments 

In preparing affidavits for summary judgments, the affidavits must set out in detail the evidentiary or probative facts sufficient to justify the judgment. Mere conclusions of law are not sufficient and the affidavit must show the competence of the witness to testify to the facts averred. If a document is referred to in such affidavit either the original must be identified by sufficient sworn statements and attached to the affidavit or legal excuse for its nonproduction and for introduction of secondary evidence of its contents must be stated. (see Gardenswartz v. Equitable etc. 

Society, 23 CA (2d) 745; Eagle Oil & Refining Co. v. Prentice (1942) 19 A C 602)

MEMORANDUM OF COSTS AND DISBURSE- MENTS 

Explanation (CCP 1021-1034) Such expenses incurred by parties in prosecuting or defending a suit as are legally chargeable to the adverse party are termed costs. The court may order the losing party to pay the successful party his allowable costs. For allowable costs see excerpts herein from BRIEF MANUAL OF COST BILLS, com piled by Senator Robert W. Kenny, former Los Angeles County Superior Court Judge. 

The cost bill must be verified. (CCP 1033) A failure to attach the jurat is fatal. (Snodgrass v. Snodgrass, 103 CA 412)

Service of the cost bill must be made on opposing counsel within 5 days after verdict, or notice of entry of judgment, where trial without jury.

Procedure 

1. Prepare

Memo of Costs and Disbursements (printed form) (a) If trial by jury, within 5 days after verdict (CCP 1033) (b) If trial without a jury, within 5 days after notice of entry of judgment (CCP 1033) †1 original, verified for filing with proof of service 

1 copy for service on each opposing counsel 

 (c) In case of Default 

Cost bill must be presented at time of application for judgment (CCP 10332) 

(See JUDGMENT BY DEFAULT, p 99) Prepare †1 original 

1 office copy (d) Costs on Appeal

Cost bill must be served on opposing counsel and filed within 30 days after the remittitur is filed with clerk of lower court. (CCP 1034) 

2. Objections (a) A party dissatisfied with the costs claimed may, within 5 days after service of a copy of the cost bill, file a motion to have the same taxed by the court in which the judgment was rendered, or by the judge or justice thereof at chambers. 

(CCP 1033) (See MOTIONS—NO- 

TICES, pp 455-457)

(b) FORM-Motion to tax costs

as Complaint)

This motion is made severally as to each of said items and is based on the attached affidavit, on the memorandum of costs and disbursements and on all the other papers, proceedings and records herein. Dated: 19....... 

Attorney for

(see MOTIONS — NOTICES, pp 455-457)

Form Memorandum of Costs and Disbursements- Printed Form EXCERPTS FROM BRIEF MANUAL OF COST BILLS (By Senator Robert W. Kenny of Los Angeles) Particular Items A. Trial Costs 1. Attorneys' Fees 

Where provided by statute same may either be claimed by cost bill or be fixed by court and included in judgment without filing a cost bill

Sunset Lumber Co. v. Smith, 95 C A 307 

2. Claim and Delivery

Cost of feeding cattle taken on claim and delivery during pendency of action not recoverable 

Meyer v. Perkins, 20 C A 661 

3. Clerk's Fees

If paid after dismissal of action-not recoverable 

Caffee v. Mann, 3 C A 124 

4. Certified Copies

Copies of deeds and records from another county are allowable 

Whitaker v. Moran, 23 CA 678

A court rule that copies of public records of the county where the court was located are not chargeable as costs, has been approved. Obviously they are not necessary where the originals are immediately available 

Senior v. Anderson, 130 Cal 290

Copies of papers offered but refused admission into evidence not allowable in the absence of showing of necessity 

Senior v. Anderson, 130 Cal 290 

5. Depositions (CCP 1032a)

Even if deposition is not used it may still be charged for as costs if it was necessary for the protection of the party taking same 

Welch v. Abbott, 178 Cal 530 

Deposition of adverse party may be allowed as costs if it appears to have been necessary 

Lomita Land & Water Co. v. Robinson, 154 

Cal 36 

Eades v. Los Angeles Ry. Corp., 43 C A 259

Cost of copy may be allowed other side (CCP 1032a) 

If deposition taken before answer filed, costs may nevertheless be collected 

California Farm & Fruit Co. v. Schiappa-Pietra, 151 Cal 732

Above rule does not apply to special proceedings where deposition cannot be taken until issue of fact arises 

Kummeth v. Atkisson, 23 C A 401

Cost of deposition may not be collected from a party who was not given notice of taking the same 

Eades v. Los Angeles Ry. Corp., 43 C A 259 

Plaintiff's deposition taken by defendant before demurrer sustained without leave may be collected even if deposition not yet signed 

6. Experts 

Hughes v. Hughes, 49 C A 217 

Simpson v. Gillis, 1 Cal (2d) 42 

Fees for expert witnesses in eminent domain not allowed 

City of Los Angeles v. Vickers, 81 C A 737

Fee of accountant not appointed by the court not allowable 

Faulkner v. Hendy, 79 Cal 265

Time spent in court as expert advisers to counsel and not waiting to testify as witnesses not chargeable 

Corona Foothill Lemon Co. v. Lillibridge, 12 CA (2d) 549 

7. Investigation

Expense of obtaining a description of property for attachment not allowable 

Murphy v. F. D. Cornell Co., 110 C A 452 

Cost of investigation prior to trial not allowable 

8. Juries

People v Feraud, 45 C A 765 

Board, lodging and other reasonable necessities of jurors during trial or deliberations are allowable (CCP 1032.5)

Fees on mistrial may nevertheless be collected as costs by party paying them if he ultimately prevails

Statutes 1869-70, page 148, as amended in 1917 

Deering's Act No. 2498, Section 28

9. Maps 

If not ordered by court, maps are not allowable as costs 

Miller v. Highland Ditch Co., 91 Cal 103 

Crabtree v. Houghton, 191 Cal 33 

10. Premium 

Surety bond premium for replevin not allowable 

Williams v. A. T. & S. F. Ry Co., 156 Cal 140 

11. Previous Trials 

Costs of previous trials are chargeable against the party ultimately defeated 

Senior v. Anderson, 130 Cal 290

Costs of mandate proceeding made necessary to compel trial court to settle bill of exceptions cannot be recoverable as costs in principal action 

Levinson v. Silverman, 110 C A 467

Even if an item was properly incurred but not taxed in a previous but different action between same parties, it may not be collected for in a subsequent action 

Carlson v. Lantz, 208 Cal 134 

12. Referees

The fee of a referee where appointed by the court is allowable as costs 

Livingston v. Bank of America, 56 Pac. (2d) 276; opinion modified 46 Pac. (2d) 195. 

13. Reporters

The court cannot compel a case to be reported, and it cannot compel an unwilling party to deposit one-half of the reporter's fees. Under such circumstances, if the case is to be reported, the other side must put up all of the fees and rely on his

chances of collecting them if he prevails at the time of judgment

Meachem v. Bear Valley Irrigation Co., 145 Cal 

606 

Gue v. Superior Court, 1 CA (2d) 91 

Fees of a second reporter used in taking a daily transcript ordered by the court not allowable 

Corona Foothill Lemon Co. v. Lillibridge, 12 CA (2d) 549

A daily transcript ordered by the court is an allowable cost item CCP 274 

Barkly v. Copeland, 86 Cal 493

This applies to partial transcripts as well, if ordered by the court 

Welch v. Alcott, 178 Cal 530

An item for a transcript appearing in a verified cost bill will be presumed to have been ordered by the court unless something is shown to the contrary 

Meyer v. San Diego, 132 Cal 35

Transcripts not ordered by court are not allowable 

Blair v. Brownstone Oil & Refining Co., 20 C A 316 A verbal order of the court for a transcript is sufficient to make the item allowable as costs 

Taylor v. McConigle, 120 Cal 123 

See Sunnyslope Water Co. v. Pasadena, 1 Cal (2d) 87 Copies

The mere fact that the parties order copies of a daily transcript after an original is ordered by the court cannot serve to diminish the prevailing party's right to

collect the full cost of the original at the folio rate for the single original copy. 

Mt. Shasta Power Corp. v. McArthur, 111 CA 640 

Traveling expenses of a reporter not allowable 

Irrgang v. Ott, 9 C A 440 

14. Service Fees

The only code provision for fees for serving papers is found in 4300b Political Code. This provides 50 cents for serving a summons and 25 cents for subpoening a witness, with mileage in both cases of 15 cents for each mile actually and necessarily traveled, when such traveling can be made by rail. If the travel cannot be made by rail, e. g., to Catalina Island, then the mileage is 25 cents. Since there is no code provision for fees for service of any other papers than subpoenas and summonses, no fee may be collected for service of inter-office process between attorneys. 

Since this is true, a fee for an affidavit of mailing of such inter-office process cannot be collected either

Service fees may be collected as costs even if the party making the service is not a peace officer 

Statutes 1891, page 56 

Deering's Act 2499 

Fay v. Fay, 165 Cal 540 

15. Sheriff's Fees

Costs of attachment are collectible 

Bakersfield Sandstone Brick Co. v. Cascade Oil 

Co., 132 C A 663

Costs on attachment must be included in cost bill. They cannot be collected later as part of the costs on execution 

Hitchkiss v. Smith, 108 Cal 285

Costs of preservation of perishable property under attachment are allowable 

Puppo v. Larosa, 194 Cal 721

Insurance premiums expended by sheriff to insure attached property not allowable. 

Galindo v. Roach, 130 Cal 389

Keeper's fee where keeper is necessary is allowable 

Nisbet v. Clio Mining Co., 2 C A 436

A keeper's fee may be included in cost bill even if not yet fixed by court as required by 4300b Political Code, provided the court fixes the fees at the time the motion to retax comes on for hearing 

Unwin v. San Antonio Oil Co., 36 C A 508 

16. Void Order 

Costs incurred under a void order while it was still believed to be valid are not necessarily lost to the party incurring the expenditure 

Meyer v. City of San Diego, 132 Cal 35 

17. Witnesses 

Mileage. The practice in some counties is to apply to witnesses' mileage the same rule as that applied to jurors, and to permit mileage only to be charged for nonconsecutive days. This is the requirement of the affidavit on the official form furnished by the County Clerk and is supported by the rule expressed in Howes v. 

Abbott, 78 Cal 270

Where a continuance is ordered to a nonconsecutive day, a second proper second mileage is 

Richards v. Silveria, 97 C A 166

The court has no right to cut down arbitrarily the actual mileage traveled 

Engel v. Ehret, 21 C A 112

Mileage is not collectible at all where the witness resides more than 100 miles from the county seat and therefore cannot be legally required to attend. Such a witness is entitled to a per diem, however 

Naylor v. Adams, 15 C A 353

Fees of witness are allowable even if he is not sworn, provided he is called in good faith 

Pritchard v. Southern Pacific, 9 C A (2d) 704 

Randall v. Falkner, 41 Cal 242

Materiality of witnesses' testimony as justifying their fees as costs is a question for the discretion of the trial court 

Barnhart v Kron, 88 Cal 447

Fees are allowable for time waiting in court to be called to testify 

Corona Foothill Lemon Co. v. Lillibridge, 12 C A (2d) 549

While witness fees may not be charged for a party, even an adverse party, who is not subpoenaed, still if an adverse party is subpoenaed, witness fees may be collected for him 

Beal v. Stephens, 72 Cal 451

Fees are a chargeable cost item even if (1) witness did not demand fees at the time of the service of the subpoena, or

(2), the fees have not yet been paid, so long as the party claiming them incurred liability for same 

Ray v. Clark, 57 Cal 467

It is not necessary to subpoena a witness to make his fees collectible as costs-a mere request to attend makes the fee an allowable item 

Linforth v. S. F. Gas & Electric Co., 9 C A 434

The fees of a witness subpoenaed on a blank form filled out by the attorney and not the clerk are an allowable cost item 

Pritchard v. Southern Pacific, 9 C A (2d) 704

If a witness refuses to accept fees then the item is not collectible 

Lindforth v. S. F. Gas & Electric Co., 9 CA 434

B. Appeal Costs 

1. Attachment

Cost of keeping an automobile under attachment during the pendency of the appeal is not recoverable 

Arrow Garage Co. v. Kikugawa, 69 Cal 509 

2. Attorneys Traveling Expenses 

Cost of attorneys for travel to argue matter before appellate court not recoverable 

Sime v. Hunter, 55 C A 157 

3. Bond Premium

A premium paid to the surety company for a stay bond is not an allowable item 

Christensen v. Cudahy Packing Co., 84 C A 237

4. Briefs 

The total cost of printing briefs may not exceed $100 (CCP 1034).

In the absence of this statute the cost of printing briefs has been held not to be allowable and therefore the section has been strictly construed 

Bond v. United Railroad Co., 20 C A 124

The supplement required to be printed in a brief by CCP 953a is a part of the brief and included within the $100 limitation 

Central Sav. Bank of Oakland v. Lake, 201 Cal 438 Expressage on briefs has been held not to be recoverable under the rule of deminimus 

Sime v. Hunter, 55 C A 157 

5. Transcripts (a) Bill of Exceptions Method 

In an order for a transcript used for preparation of a bill of exceptions to be chargeable as costs, it must have been ordered prepared by the court under CCP 274 

Estate of Gallo, 71 C A 352, as explained by Newberry v. Evans, 86 C A, at 110 

Where respondents obtained transcript to be used in preparing amendments to a bill of exceptions, they cannot recover the cost of this item as a cost of appeal, in the absence of a court order under CCP 274 

Countryman v. Calif. Trona Co., 39 C 

Eaton v. So. Pac. Co., 31 C A 379

A 753

Where an appellant sought to prepare a bill of exceptions from his own stenographic notes, but the respondent obtained a court order for a transcript under CCP 274, in order to prepare amendments thereto, the respondent was allowed to recover this item after he was successful on appeal 

Kessler v. Young, 86 C A 657 

(b) The Alternative Method 

Costs of an original transcript prepared under CCP 953a are recoverable even if not ordered by the court 

Stafford v. Hill, 63 C A 15 

Newberry v. Evans, 3 C A 106 

A copy of the transcript ordered by the prevailing party on appeal is an allowable item (see CCP 1034) 

Where the transcript furnished under CCP 953a was actually a daily prepared under the course of the trial, and the appellant incurred no additional costs for using it on appeal, it is not an allowable item of costs on appeal 

Turner v. Eastside Canal & Irrigation 

Co., 177 Cal 570

Where the parties stipulate that the daily transcript may be treated as though made under CCP 953a, then its cost is an allowable item 

By-Products Fuel Machine Co. v. Daw son, 218 Cal 421

Payment of Items 

If the item was never paid for or incurred at all, then, of course, it is not allowable 

Freshour v. Hihn, 99 Cal 443 

City Investment Co. v. Pringle, 49 C A 353

But cost items need not have been actually paid out, it is merely essential that the liability for them has been incurred by the party claiming them 

Stafford v. Hill, 63 C A 15 

Ray v. Clark, 57 C A 467

It is immaterial whether the party claiming costs paid the same personally or procured the payment thereof by a third party under an agreement for reimbursement. This is a question that concerns them alone and cannot be appropriately raised by the losing party to the litigation in a motion to tax costs 

Danley v. Merced Irrigation Dist., 76 C A 52

Overruling an objection that a successful defendant had a contract of insurance and was not under obligation to pay costs does not show an abuse of discretion 

Squiar v. McLean, 3 C A (2d) 429

NOTICE OF ENTRY OF JUDGMENT 

(CCP 953d) Explanation

Notice of entry of judgment should be served on opposing counsel immediately after judgment has been entered. This is highly important to limit the time within which proceedings for a new trial may be taken or an appeal may be perfected. Procedure 

1. Prepare Notice of Entry of Judgment 

†1 original 

1 copy for service on each opposing counsel 

2. Serve copy on each opposing counsel (Memorandum of costs is usually served at same time) 3. File original notice with proof of service

ABSTRACT OF JUDGMENT 

(CCP 674) Explanation

An abstract of judgment is a brief transcript of the essentials of an entered judgment. By recording such abstract of judgment a lien upon real property of judgment debtor is created in the county where recorded. Procedure 

1. Prepare printed form of Abstract of Judgment 

†1 original 

2. Have clerk issue abstract 

3. Fee-usually 50¢ to $1.00 

4. Send abstract of judgment for recordation to the 

County Recorder of any county wherein real property of the judgment debtor is located. If the judgment debtor owns property in more than one county, abstract may be recorded in each county where his property is located. 

5. Recordation fee-consult the County Recorder for the exact amount (usually $1.00) 

Note: The abstract of judgment, when recorded, becomes a lien on the property of the judgment debtor which lien continues for 5 years from the date of the entry of the judgment (not from recording of abstract)

BE SURE TO NOTE THIS EXPI- RATION DATE ON YOUR CAL- ENDAR!

Forms The statute of limitations on judgments is 5 years from date of entry of judgment in the judgment book. A new suit must be instituted on the judgment before the expiration of the 5 year period if the party desires to have it renewed (although court may on proper showing, after notice to defendant, order execution to issue after 5 years-see 

JUDGMENT, note p 127) (CCP 336, 681, 685) It is possible that the action may be ruled to have been commenced in time if started within 5 years after judgment has become final. See Stulz v. Bacon, 96 P (2d) 369; Feeney v. 

Hinckley, 134 Cal 467.

Printed form usually filled out by clerk Explanation

NEW TRIAL 

(CCP 656-663a)

A new trial is a reexamination of the facts by the same court after a trial and decision by a jury, court or referee.

Grounds Upon Which New Trial May Be Ordered 

1. Irregularity in the proceedings (by court, jury or adverse party) 

2. Misconduct of the jury 

3. Accident or surprise 

4. Newly discovered evidence 

5. Excessive damages, appearing to have been given under passion or prejudice 6. Insufficiency of the evidence to justify the verdict, or decision, or that it is against law 

7. Error in law

Manner of Applying for New Trial 1. Notice of Intention to Move for New Trial desig nating grounds must be served upon opposing counsel and filed with the clerk either before entry of judgment, or within 10 days after service of written notice of entry of judgment. Time cannot be extended by order or stipulation. 

2. Stipulation (see Form) 

Affidavits Supporting Motion 

When motion is made on grounds 1, 2, 3, or 4 above, it must be supported by affidavits; otherwise it is made on minutes of court (a) Affidavits must be served on opposing counsel within 10 days after service of notice of intention to move for new trial (b) Adverse party may serve and file counteraffidavits within 10 days thereafter (c) Either of the above 10 day periods, prior to expiration, may be extended by court for an additional period not to exceed 20 days, each.

Such extension may be made on affidavit or stipulation of the parties (d) Upon expiration of time to file counter-affidavits, judge sets time for hearing, not later than 10 days before expiration of time within which court has power to pass on same (CCP 661) (e) Clerk gives parties 5 days' notice by mail (CCP 661)

(f) Court has power to pass on motion for new trial until 60 days after service of notice of entry of judgment; if such notice has not been served, then 60 days after the filing of notice of intention to move for new trial. 

If not determined within such time, said motions shall be deemed denied without further order of court. (CCP 660) (g) At hearing court may vacate judgment, modify it or grant a new trial (CCP 657) Note: For time within which to appeal and initiate proceedings for record under CCP 953a, see p 161. Procedure 

1. Prepare Notice of Intention to Move for New Trial 

†1 original 

2. Serve 

1 copy for service on each opposing counsel 

3. File original with proof of service 

4. Fee $2.00 

5. If motion is made under sections 1, 2, 3, 4, prepare and serve necessary affidavits within 10 days. Adverse party has 10 days after service of moving parties' affidavits in which to serve and file the counter affidavits. 

6. Notice of date of hearing given by clerk (CCP 661) 

7. Notice of ruling on motion

File notice of ruling on motion with proof of service

Forms Notice of Intention to Move for New Trial

that:

Defendant (Plaintiff) intends to and will move the above entitled court in department (division) thereof, to vacate and set aside the verdict (decision) and judgment in the above entitled action, and to grant a new trial.

Said motion will be based upon this notice, upon all of the files, papers, pleadings and proceedings herein, upon the minutes of the court, and affidavits to be filed, and will be made upon each of the following grounds: (1) Irregularity in proceedings of the court (jury) (adverse party), by which defendant (plaintiff) was prevented from having a fair trial. (2) Abuse of discretion of the court during the trial, by which defendant (plaintiff) was prevented from having a fair trial. (3) Misconduct of the jury. (4) Accident and surprise, which ordinary prudence could not have guarded against.

(5) Newly discovered evidence for the defendant (plaintiff) which he could not with reasonable diligence have discovered and produced at the trial. (6) Excessive damages appearing to have been given under the influence of passion or prejudice. (7) Insufficiency of evidence to justify the verdict (decision) and judgment. (8) Said judgment is against law. (9) Error in law occurring at the trial and excepted to by defendant (plaintiff).

VI 

EXECUTION OF JUDGMENT IN CIVIL 

Explanation

ACTIONS EXECUTION 

(CCP 681-688) An execution is a writ authorizing the seizure and appropriation of the property of the losing party for the satisfaction of a judgment against him. 

When to Issue (CCP 681) 

The party in whose favor judgment is given may at any time within 5 years after the entry of such judgment have a writ of execution issued. Several executions may be issued to different counties. 

Note: In discretion of court on sufficient showing after notice to adverse party, may issue after 5 years (CCP 685)

Stay of Execution (CCP 681a) 

Without the consent of the adverse party the court may stay the issuance of the execution for not longer than 

10 days in Justices' Courts or 30 days in other courts, and for a longer period with consent of adverse party. 

If motion for new trial is pending, execution may be stayed until 10 days after the determination thereof.

Execution may be stayed pending appeal only upon posting a stay bond, or in certain instances upon the granting of a writ of supersedeas.

Who May Issue (CCP 682) 

Issued by Clerk of Court 

To Whom Issued (see CCP 687).

When Made Returnable (CCP 683) 

At any time not less than 10 days nor more than 60 days after its receipt by the officer to whom it is directed. 

When returned unsatisfied, another execution may be issued. 

Levy on Bank Account or Property in Safe Deposit

Vault Not Wholly in Name of Defendant (CCP 682a) 

If execution is to be levied on bank deposit or property in safe deposit box not wholly in name of defendant, a bond in twice amount of judgment must be provided and delivered to bank, trust company or safe deposit corporation concurrently with levy of writ, otherwise such levy is ineffective for any purpose. 

Upon delivery of bond the garnishee must immediately notify persons concerned other than judgment debtor and hold property for 15 days (unless sooner released) after which it may comply with levy. Fee $1.00 required by clerk for issuing executions in Superior and Municipal Court but no fee in Justices' Courts (Pol C 4300 a, 1, e)

Execution Can Issue After 5 Years (CCP 685) 

By leave of court upon motion and sufficient showing after due notice to judgment debtor

Property Liable to Execution (CCP 688) 

(For property exempt from execution see CCP 690 to 

690.25, also CCP 688)

Lien on Judgment or Cause of Action (CCP 688.1) 

No cause of action or judgment shall be subject to levy (CCP 688), but upon motion court may give judgment creditor lien upon cause of action or judgment and permit intervention.

EXECUTION OF JUDGMENT 

155 Procedure 

1. Prepare (a) Execution (printed form) †1 original and 

1 copy for levying officer 

1 copy for service on adverse party 1 copy for each item to be levied upon 

 (b) Instructions to levying officer (printed form) †1 original and 

1 copy for levying office 

1 office copy (c) If levy to be on bank deposit or safe deposit box not wholly in name of defendant, provide the special bond and direct levying officer to deliver concurrently with levy 

Fee Consult levying office or see appropriate subdivision of 4300 Political Code 

2. Have clerk issue original execution 

3. Deliver original and appropriate number of copies to levying officer with original instructions and required fee (together with bond required by CCP 682a if levy to be made on bank deposit or safe deposit box not wholly in name of defendant.) (Executions may be issued to different counties) (CCP 687)

Note: See WRIT OF ATTACHMENT, 

Pp 79-87

NULLA BONA RETURN 

(CCP 714) Nulla Bona Return is the name of the return made by the officer to a writ of execution when no goods of defendant have been found within jurisdiction upon which to levy. 

To lay the foundation to bring the judgment debtor into court for supplementary examination as to his assets, follow this procedure: 

1. After original execution is issued by clerk, deliver to levying officer who "clocks" it as to date and time of receipt 

2. The execution is then held for 10 days, after which time the officer will return the execution unsatisfied 

3. File the original execution with clerk 

(See SUPPLEMENTARY PROCEEDINGS, pp 157-159)

APPEALS 

161 

SUPREME COURT AND DISTRICT COURTS OF APPEAL 

(Where Rule is mentioned in the following Section it refers to the Rules of the Supreme Court and District Courts of Appeal, adopted by the 

Judicial Council of California; 213 Cal. XXXV)

Party Aggrieved May Appeal. Designation of parties 

(CCP 938) Any party aggrieved may appeal. Party appealing is known as appellant and the adverse party as the respondent.

Time for Taking Appeal (CCP 939) 

(New trial proceedings controlled by CCP 656 et seq.)

NOTE: Judicial Council rules for practice and procedure on appeal effective 1943

The 1941 session of the Legislature added CCP 961 giving the Judicial Council power to prescribe rules for practice and procedure on appeal and for the time and manner in which records may be made up and filed in civil actions. Such rules are to be reported by the Council to the 1943 Legislature and become effective 90 days after such Legislature convenes, all laws in conflict therewith being thereupon repealed. 

Under present law appeal may be taken within 60 days from entry of judgment or order, or if motion for new trial is pending, within 30 days after entry of order determining such motion or other termination in trial court of the new trial proceedings (CCP 939); but notice to prepare transcript under CCP 953a must be filed within 10 days after service of notice of entry of judgment, or if proceeding on motion for new trial is pending, within 10 days after notice of decision denying such motion or other termination thereof.

Supreme Court-Jurisdiction (Art. VI, 4, Constitution, see p 1) 

1. Has appellate jurisdiction on appeal from the Superior Court in all cases in equity, except such as arise in Municipal or Justices' Courts; in cases at law which involve the title or possession of real estate, or the legality of any tax, impost, assessment, toll or municipal fine; on questions of law alone in all criminal cases where judgment of death has been rendered; also in all such probate matters as may be provided by law. 

2. In matters pending before a District Court of Appeal which shall be ordered by the Supreme Court to be transferred to itself for hearing 

3. Has power to issue writs of mandamus, certiorari, prohibition and habeas corpus, and all other writs necessary or proper to the complete exercise of its appellate jurisdiction

District Courts of Appeal-Division 

State divided into four districts with a court in each district. See any recent volume of California Appellate

Reports for list of the several counties in the respective districts.

District Courts of Appeal-Jurisdiction (Art VI, 4b, 

Constitution, see p 2) 

1. Have appellate jurisdiction on appeal from the Superior Courts (except where appellate jurisdiction is given to the Supreme Court) (a) in cases at law in which the Superior Courts are given original jurisdiction (b) in cases of forcible or unlawful entry or detainer (except such as arise in Municipal or 

Justices' Courts) (c) in proceedings in insolvency

APPEALS 

163 (d) in actions to prevent or abate a nuisance (e) in proceedings of mandamus, certiorari, prohibition, usurpation of office, removal from office, contesting elections, eminent domain. and in such other special proceedings as may be provided by law (f) on questions of law alone in criminal cases where judgment of death has not been rendered 2. In matters or proceedings before the Supreme Court which it shall order to be transferred to a District Court 

3. Same as Section 3 SUPREME COURT-Jurisdic tion p 162

Appeal to Wrong Court (Art. VI, 4a, Constitution) 

No appeal taken to the Supreme Court or a District Court shall be dismissed for the reason that the same was not taken to the proper court, but the case shall be transferred to the proper court upon such terms as to costs or otherwise as may be just. (Payment of new filing fee, unless otherwise specified in the order of transfer. Sec. 3, Rule XXXII) 

Powers of Courts in Appealed Cases (CCP 53, 956a) 1. Appellate courts may affirm, reverse or modify any judgment or order appealed from, and may direct the proper judgment or order to be entered, or direct a new trial 2. Decision of court given in writing. Its judgment is remitted to the court from which appeal was taken. 

3. In nonjury cases may take evidence and make findings.

From What an Appeal May Be Taken (CCP 963) 

1. From a final judgment entered in an action or special proceeding commenced in a Superior Court, or brought into a Superior Court from another court otherwise than on appeal 

2. From an order granting a new trial or denying a motion for judgment notwithstanding the verdict in an action or proceeding where a trial by jury is a matter of right, etc. 

3. From such probate orders and decrees as are made appealable by the provisions of the Probate Code 

Probate and Election Cases Given Preference 

Appeals in probate proceedings and in contested election cases given preference (CCP 57); also in criminal cases

Penal Code 1252 Two Methods of Preparing Record of Testimony Upon Appeal 

1. Printed authenticated bill of exceptions prepared under CCP 646 et seq. 

2. Reporter's transcript of testimony certified by judge under CCP 953a et seq. and clerk's transcript of judgment roll, certified by clerk only 

Exception Defined (CCP 646)

An exception is an objection upon a matter of law to a decision made, either before or after judgment, in an action or proceeding. When Taken. Certain decisions are deemed excepted to. (CCP 647) Must be taken at time decision is made except when deemed excepted to as provided in CCP 647.

APPEALS 

165 

Bill of Exceptions (CCP 650) 

1. Where a party desires to have exceptions taken at a trial settled (i. e., stated in a document to be certified to a higher court as the record on which the appeal is to be taken), he may, within 20 days after service of written notice of entry of judgment, prepare and file with the clerk the draft of a bill; or 

2. If proceedings on motion for a new trial are pending, within 10 days after notice of the ruling on said motion 3. Proof must be made of service of a copy thereof on adverse party 4. Such draft must contain all exceptions and proceedings taken upon which the party relies 5. Within 10 days after such service adverse party may propose, serve and file amendments thereto 6. At a time designated therefor the judge must settle the bill, which must thereupon be engrossed 7. Within 10 days after settlement the party who filed the bill must present it duly engrossed to the judge to be certified

Note: An exception to a decision taken on an occasion other than a trial must be preserved in a bill presented within 10 days after written notice of the decision. (CCP 649)

Appeals From Court of Record. Reporter's transcript in lieu of bill of exceptions (CCP 953a) 

1. Person desiring to appeal may file with the clerk of the Court from whose judgment, order or decree the appeal is taken a notice that he intends to appeal and requesting a transcript of the testimony be prepared 2. Such notice must be filed within 10 days after filing of the notice of appeal

3. Within 20 days after notice to him reporter must file transcript, time for which may be extended as provided in CCP 953a 4. Clerk shall set a time for presenting the transcript to the judge for approval and give notice to the attorney not less than 5 days before time set 

5. When the judge certifies to the truth and correctness of the transcript it becomes a portion of the judgment roll and is considered in lieu of bill of exceptions

Dismissal of Appeal 

1. On failure to furnish requisite papers (CCP 954) or to file transcript or briefs within rule time (Rules 

V and VI) 

2. Voluntary withdrawal before filing of record (CCP 954a)

Time Table of Filings CIVIL APPEALS 

Transcript (containing bill of exceptions or testimony under CCP 953a) (Rule 1)-40 days after certification of record. Appeal on judgment roll— 

40 days after filing notice of appeal. 

Briefs (Rule 1) (a) Appellant's-30 days after transcript (b) Respondent's-30 days after service of appellant's brief (c) Appellant's closing-20 days after service of respondent's brief

Petition for rehearing in same court (Rule XXX) -20 days after date of decision

Answers to such petition-within 28 days after date of decision

167 Petition, after D. C. A. decision, for hearing in the 

Supreme Court-40 days after date of decision 

Answer to such petition-50 days after date of decision

CRIMINAL APPEALS

Reporter's transcript-"When transcript has been certified and delivered"

Extension of time for transcribing (Rule II, Sec. 

11)-may not exceed 60 days Briefs (a) Appellant's-10 days after filing of last transcript (b) Respondent's-10 days after service of appellant's brief (c) Appellant's closing-5 days after service of respondent's brief 

Petition for Rehearing

In Supreme Court-after judgment therein-20 days after date of decision (Rule XXX, Sec. 1) 

In District Court of Appeal-10 days after date of decision (Rule XXX, Sec. 1) 

Answer to Petition for Rehearing 

In Supreme Court-28 days after date of decision

In District Court of Appeal-13 days after judgment 

Petition for hearing in Supreme Court-22 days after judgment in District Court of Appeal 

Answer to Petition-26 days after judgment in 

District Court of Appeal

Copies to Be Filed

In SUPREME COURT 

Original and 20 copies of all printed documents, except petitions for hearing and answer (Rule I, Sec. 6)

Original and 10 clear copies of typewritten petitions, with points and authorities supporting the same, and answers or other documents in response thereto, and all typewritten briefs (Typed documents should be double spaced) Original and 3 copies of all other typewritten documents tendered for filing, except typewritten transcripts Original and 17 copies of petitions for hearing or answers thereto. Also 17 copies of all printed documents filed in D. C. A. 

In DISTRICT COURTS OF APPEAL- 

Original and 3 copies of all printed documents, and of all typewritten documents in cases pending therein, and 3 copies of petitions for hearing and answers thereto, in causes sought to be removed to the 

Supreme Court for hearing 

Typewritten petitions, motions, briefs, etc., should be upon paper 10 inches in length and 8 inches in width and bound on the left side. Typewritten transcripts should be provided with stiff board covers.

Indistinct, carbon copies will not be accepted for filing

APPEALS 

169 

Service of Briefs (a) Civil Cases 

Serve 1 copy on attorney for each party who has appeared separately in Superior Court 

1 copy on County Clerk for trial judge

Criminal Cases 

Appellant must serve 1 copy on Attorney 

General and 

1 copy on District Attorney (b) Show proof of service on original, either by acknowledgment of opposing attorney or by affidavit 

Form of Documents (See also p 438)

On cover there should appear the correct appeal number of a cause with its title, with proper designation of the parties, the name of the county and of the judge of the trial court and name and address of each attorney. (For example of how to set up the brief see BRIEFS, p 440) Covers

All briefs, petitions, etc., and other typewritten papers should have manuscript covers Index and Arrangement of Briefs, Petitions for Hearings, etc. (Rule VIII) 

1. A statement of question involved should appear on first page of appellant's brief without any other matter appearing thereon. 

2. Statement must be in the briefest terms without names, dates, amounts or particulars; should not exceed 20 lines and never one page 3. If respondent disagrees with appellant's statement he may frame his own in like manner

4. When a printed document contains 30 pages or more it must be prefaced with a topical index and a table of cases and authorities cited showing the pages on which each is cited 5. The brief or petition must present each point separately under an appropriate heading Printing 

1. Penal Code 1247e provides: "No printing of any record on appeal or brief in a criminal case shall be required or ordered" 2. Transcripts in civil cases should be printed except when alternative method has been adopted, and briefs in all appeals except when otherwise permitted by special court order, or rule 

Industrial Accident Commission Cases (Rule XXVI, 

Sec. 4) 

1. Application for a writ of certiorari to review an order or award of the Commission must be accompanied by proof of service upon the Commission 2. Commission may within 10 days after such service serve and file an answer 3. Applicant may serve and file a reply to such answer within 5 days thereafter

Railroad Commission Cases (Rule XXVI, Sec. 5) 

1. Application for a writ of certiorari must be accompanied by proof of service upon the Commission 

2. Commission may within 10 days after such service serve and file an answer 3. Applicant may serve and file a reply to such answer within 5 days thereafter

Extension of Time (Rule I, Sec. 5) 

Time limit for filing of briefs may be extended by stipulation or by order of Court upon affidavit (2nd 

Dist. Court of Appeal requires affidavits)

APPEALS 

171 

Finality of Judgment (Art VI, 4c, Constitution) 

1. Judgment in criminal cases becomes final upon the expiration of 15 days (except in Supreme Court- 

30 days (Rule XXX, Sec. 1)) 

2. In civil cases becomes final 30 days after being pronounced but remittiturs do not issue until the time has expired within which a hearing in the Supreme 

Court may be granted 

Schedule of Fees (Pol C 752, 759) 

Filing transcript on appeal, civil cases.. $10.00 

Refiling transcript after transfer for improper appeal 10.00

Time for Appeal 

A. Civil (CCP 983a) 

1. After rendition of judgment or order and within 30 days after notice of entry of same 

2. Regardless of service of notice of entry not beyond 60 days after entry except when motion for new trial is pending, in which event time to appeal expires 15 days after determination of motion

B. Criminal (Penal Code 1467) 

1. Within 5 days after rendition of judgment or order Procedure 

A. Civil 1. 

Notice of Appeal (CCP 940) 

Must state who appeals, the specific judgment, order or part thereof appealed from and the court to which the appeal is taken

Prepare 

1 original to file with clerk (proof of service unnecessary) 

1 copy for opposing counsel-not required, just a matter of courtesy File Pay Fee of $8.00, includes fee to file appeal in Superior Court. Notice of

Appeal ineffectual unless fee paid at time notice is filed. (CCP 988)

2. Stay of Execution Pending Appeal (CCP 

985) (a) Defendant can stay execution by filing bond in twice the amount of the judgment including costs. (CCP 

1054a deposit of money in lieu of undertaking)

Note The bond is retained by the clerk of the Municipal 

Court; it is not sent to the Superior Court with the other records. (b) Time for Filing Bond (CCP 986) 

Must be filed within 5 days after the filing of the Notice of Appeal, and Notice of the filing must be given to respondent. 

Exceptions by adverse party may be made within 5 days after receipt of such notice. 

(c) Effect of Filing Bond (CCP 987) 

If execution has been issued it must be recalled, the judge being required to make an order for ing proceedings thereon. 

3. Bill of Exceptions (CCP 650) 

(a) Proposed Bill of Exceptions 

Within 20 days after service of written notice of entry of judgment or within 10 days after notice of order denying motion for new trial, etc., or within such time as the court may allow.

APPEALS 

175 

Prepare Proposed Bill of Excep tions (or transcript on appeal- 

See Reporter's Transcript page 177) 

1 original to file with clerk, together with proof of service 

1 copy for opposing counsel (b) Amendments 

Within 10 days after service of proposed bill, adverse counsel may prepare, serve and file with proof of service, proposed amendments. 

(c) Engrossed Bill of Exceptions 

Immediately upon receipt of amendments, or, upon the expiration of the time for filing the proposed amendments if none are filed, the clerk delivers the draft (proposed bill) and the amendments, if any, to the judge for settlement. 

The judge designates the time at which he will settle the bill, clerk notifies parties immediately of the time so designated. At said time the judge must settle the bill. 

Within 10 days after settlement, if amendments to proposed bill of exceptions have been allowed, prepare an engrossed bill of exceptions, which is the final form thereof, and present to judge for certification. It is courteous practice to send copy of engrossed bill to opposing counsel.

*CAUTIONS 

(a) When the exception is to the verdict or decision upon the ground of the insufficiency of the evidence to justify it, the objection MUST

SPECIFY THE PARTICULARS 

in which such evidence is alleged to be insufficient. Otherwise the alleged insufficiency will be disregarded on appeal. Live Oak Cemetery Assn v Adamson (1930) 106 

CA 783; Loewenberg v Schneider (1939) 14 Cal (2d) 305, 307 (b) The provisions of CCP 648, 649, 650 and 953a apply to appeals from the Municipal Court but papers ON

FILE IN THE MUNICIPAL 

COURT need not be copied into such record if the same are referred to and indexed in the bill of exceptions or transcript by a designation sufficient to identify the same. (988a) (c) Except for the judgment roll (see 

CCP 988b) the bill of exceptions or transcript must contain everything necessary to enable the appellate court to rule on the questions to be argued. Generally speaking, if any evidence appears to have been omitted the appellate court must

APPEALS 

177 presume that it was sufficient to support the judgment. Likewise if an error in giving instructions to a jury is complained of, the record must contain all instructions given as well as those refused; a mere statement that the evidence is insufficient to support the judgment is not a specification of particulars as required by CCP 648. (This is the downfall of many appeals.) (d) Within 5 days after certification of record by judge or expiration of time for proposing bill of exceptions or transcript, clerk must transmit record on appeal to Superior Court. Thereafter and before argument or preparation of points and authorities attorneys should examine the record as filed, because it may have been amended by the judge and differ from their copies. 

Points and authorities must be prepared, served and filed as prescribed by Rule 5 of Rules of Judicial Council for Appellate Departments.

OR alternative method of preparing record 

3. Reporter's Transcript (CCP 953a) 

(a) Appellant must file with the clerk of the court a notice stating that he desires or intends to appeal or has appealed, and requesting that a transcript of the testimony offered or taken, etc., be prepared (within

10 days after the notice of appeal itself is filed, said notice must be filed). Clerk will give immediate notice to reporter. (b) Appellant must either put up bond to cover cost of reporter's transcript or, as is usually done, make satisfactory arrangements with the reporter direct. (c) Stenographic reporter has 20 days from date of receipt of notice or such additional time as may be allowed by court within which to prepare transcript and file (d) When transcript is filed with clerk, clerk sets time (not more than 10 days after the filing of the transcript) for presenting to the judge for approval and gives notice of the setting to the attorneys of record (e) Judge to certify as to correctness, etc. *See Cautions on page 176 (however, specifications of particulars of insufficiency of evidence are not required where the record is presented by reporter's transcript)

APPEALS 

179 

B. Criminal 1. Notice of Appeal (Penal Code 1467) 

Within 5 days after rendition of judgment or order prepare and file in Municipal Court written notice of appeal stating by whom, from what, and to which court, appeal is taken. 

2. Statement on Appeal 

Prepare, serve on respondent's attorney and file in Municipal Court a proposed statement on appeal. Such statement must specify the grounds of appeal and set forth all evidence and proceedings necessary for determination of questions to be presented. If insufficiency of evidence is a ground of appeal, the particulars wherein the evidence is insufficient must be specified. In any event, consult rules governing criminal appeals to the Superior Court as set out by 

Judicial Council. (The statement on appeal is similar to the bill of exceptions in civil cases)

Civil

POINTS AND AUTHORITIES 

Appellate Department 

Superior Court (Rule 5) 

1. Contents and Arrangement 

(a) The rule permits points and authorities not exceeding 10 typewritten pages, double spaced. (b) Each point with supporting argument must be under appropriate heading stating its nature. (c) State concisely the propositions of both law and fact relied on with reference by line and page, if possible, to the record, with appropriate citation of authorities. (d) No quotation of authority or extract from record may exceed 15 lines. 

2. Time of Filing (a) Appellant's points and authorities 

Within 9 days after filing record on appeal (b) Respondent's points and authorities 

Within 14 days after filing record (c) Appellant's reply 

Within 17 days after filing record 

3. Service and filing (a) Serve 1 copy on attorney for each adverse party who appeared separately in lower court (b) File original, with proof of service

Criminal 

Same rule applies in criminal cases except that as to time of filing each party in a criminal case shall serve his points and authorities on or before date of hearing of appeal.

FROM JUSTICES' COURTS 

A. Civil (CCP 973-982) 

Appeals on questions of both law and fact 

Appeals from Justices' Courts may be taken either on questions of law alone, or on questions of both law and fact. The notice of appeal must specifically state whether the appeal is taken from the whole or a part of the judgment, and whether it is taken on questions of law alone, or on questions of both law and fact.

If the appeal is on questions of law alone, in counties in which there is an appellate department of the Superior Court, the appeal goes to such department. If on questions of both law and fact, it goes to a trial department of the Superior Court and the action is tried de novo. Procedure

The procedure is otherwise generally similar to appeals from Municipal Courts and is governed in particulars as to time, etc., by the above mentioned code sections.

B. Criminal Appeals from Justices' Courts or Police Courts in criminal cases are taken in counties where there is an appellate department of the Superior Court to such department, in other counties to a trial department of such court. 

When a new trial is granted by the Superior Court, such new trial is held in the Superior Court, except when the appeal is taken from a Class A Justices' Court. Otherwise, the procedure is generally as prescribed for appeals from Municipal Courts. (See Penal

Code 1466 and rules governing criminal appeals as prescribed by Judicial Council)

GENERAL PROCEDURE IN DIVORCE AND MAINTENANCE ACTIONS 

Explanation (CC 82-148) Actions for divorce are brought in the Superior Court. Plaintiff must have resided in California for one year and in the county in which action is brought for three months, immediately preceding commencement of the action. (CC 128) 

An action for separate maintenance (CCP 137) is also brought in the Superior Court. When one spouse wilfully deserts the other (although the desertion has not continued for the 1 year necessary to constitute a ground for divorce) or when either spouse has any cause of action for divorce, as listed in CC 92, the allegedly innocent spouse may, without applying for a divorce, maintain an action for permanent support of self and maintenance of children, if any.

A separate maintenance action may also be maintained by the wife when the husband wilfully fails 

Note: The residential requirements of CC 128 do not apply to actions for separate maintenance, to cross complainants in divorce cases or to annulment actions.

DOMESTIC RELATIONS 

185 to provide for the wife although such failure to provide may not have continued for the 1 year necessary to constitute a ground for divorce and if father wilfully fails to provide for his children the mother may maintain action solely for support of children. 

The various steps and proceedings in separate or children's maintenance actions are substantially the same as for divorce and the procedure herein outlined for divorce may be followed in such actions. 

The allegations of the complaint are similar so far as pertinent.

Grounds for Divorce (CC 92) 

(1) Adultery 

Action must be commenced within 2 years after discovery of said adultery by the injured party (CC 124) (2) Extreme cruelty (3) Wilful desertion (4) Wilful neglect (5) Habitual intemperance (6) Conviction of felony must continue for 

1 year (CC 107) 

Action must be brought within 2 years after pardon or termination of period of sentence (CC 124) (7) Incurable insanity must continue for 3 years

Neither of the parties shall, subsequent to the date of this agreement, incur any debts, obligations or liabilities, against the other, and each of the parties hereto expressly releases the other from any and all future accounts, alimony and support, and each of the parties agrees that neither shall hereafter be responsible for any debts contracted by the other subsequent the date of separation, except as herein specifically provided. (Each of the parties represents to the other that he has not incurred any obligation subsequent to to 2 for which the other might or will be held responsible.) (or)\\(The parties agree that there are no outstanding obligations of the community at this time, and that if any such obligation shall appear, the party who incurred the same shall be responsible therefor.) VIII

Neither party shall charge or cause or permit to be charged to or against the other, any purchase or purchases which either of them may hereafter make, and shall not hereafter create any engagements or obligations in the name of or against the other, and shall never hereafter secure or attempt to secure, any credit upon or in connection with the other, or his or her name, and each of them will promptly pay all debts and discharge all financial obligations which each may incur for himself or herself, and each of them will hereafter hold the other free and harmless from any and all debts and other obligations which the other may incur.

IX

All property, whether real, personal or mixed, which the parties severally now hold, or may acquire by virtue of or pursuant to this agreement, together with any increment thereon, and all property which either of the parties hereto may hereafter acquire, shall be and remain the separate property and estate of the party so holding, acquiring or to acquire the same, free from any claim or claims of the other. Each of the parties agrees, when and as requested by the other party, to execute and deliver to such other any and all documents, deeds, releases, or conveyances, necessary or convenient to show title to the property of the several parties vested in accordance with this agreement. XI

Each party has read the foregoing agreement and fully understands the contents thereof and accepts the same. There has been no promise, agreement or undertaking of either of the parties to the other, except as above set forth, relied upon by either as a matter of inducement to enter into this agreement, and each party has been independently advised by his or her attorney as to the legal effect of executing this agreement. DATED: 19....... Husband Wife 

Attorney for Husband 

Attorney for Wife (Acknowledgments if desired-and they are highly desirable)

DOMESTIC RELATIONS 

205 

Explanation ANNULMENT 

Divorce and annulment differ in that a divorce dissolves a valid marriage while an annulment merely annuls or declares void a voidable or void marriage. A divorce terminates the marriage as of the time of entry of the final decree-a decree of annulment (there is no interlocutory decree of annulment) is effective as soon as entered and declares the purported marriage void clear back as of the time it was contracted. 

By express provision of CC 84, however, it is declared that a judgment of nullity of marriage does not affect the legitimacy of children conceived or born before the judgment, and CC 85 provides that "The issue of a marriage which is void or annulled or dissolved by divorce is legitimate." 

Marriages are either valid, voidable, or void.

Valid Marriage 

A valid marriage can be dissolved only by death or divorce

Voidable Marriage 

A voidable marriage may be annulled by action brought for that purpose on one of the grounds specified in CC 82 and within the particular time specified in CC 83. If the voidable marriage is not annulled within the time specified in CC 83, it becomes valid and can be dissolved only by divorce 

The following are considered voidable marriages (CC 82): 

1. Where party securing annulment was under age of legal consent and contracted marriage without the consent of parents or guardian, unless such party after attaining age of consent freely cohabited with the other

2. Where former husband or wife turns out to have been living although believed to have been dead 

3. Where either party was of unsound mind, unless such party after restoration to reason freely cohabited with the other 

4. Where consent was obtained by fraud, unless there was cohabitation afterwards with full knowledge of facts 

5. Where consent was obtained by force, unless there was voluntary cohabitation thereafter 6. Physical incapacity existing at time of marriage and proved to be incurable

The periods of limitation covering the above mentioned grounds for annulment are as specified in CC 83. 

Void Marriage 

A void marriage is wholly void from the beginning, never can become valid and confers no rights or obligations. It may, however, be the subject of a court action to declare it void pursuant to CC 80. 

The following are considered void marriages (CC 59): 

1. Marriages between parents and children 

2. Between ancestors and descendants of any degree 3. Between brothers and sisters, of the half as well as the whole blood 

4. Between uncles and nieces 

5. Between aunts and nephews 

6. White persons with negroes, Mongolians,

Malays, or mulattoes (CC 60)

DOMESTIC RELATIONS 

207 7. By any person having an already presently existing valid marriage, unless the spouse of such person is absent and not known to be living for 5 successive years immediately preceding the subsequent marriage or is generally reputed to be dead, in which case the subsequent marriage is considered valid until its nullity is adjudged (CC 61) 

Note: The provisions of CC 128 requiring residence in the state for 1 year and in the county for 3 months preceding the commencement of an action for divorce, do not apply to annulment actions, there being no residential requirements for the latter. The provisions of CC 130 prohibiting the granting of a divorce upon the uncorroborated testimony of the parties do not apply to annulment actions. Procedure

Except as noted, the procedure for annulment is similar to that of divorce (see DIVORCE, p 186 and COM- 

PLAINT, p 26)
