CHILDREN'S CONCILIATION COURT 

Explanation

The Children's Court of Conciliation provided for by the 1939 legislature is not a separate or new court, but is in effect a new procedure provided for in the Superior Court whereby one or more designated departments of that court (in courts having more than one judge) hold such sessions as may be expedient for the purpose of effecting the reconciliation of spouses and the amicable settlement of domestic controversies. Proceedings may be voluntarily instituted in such department by petition, and, if not voluntarily so instituted therein, all cases of divorce, annulment of marriage or separate maintenance in which the welfare of a minor child of a party appears to be affected are required to be transferred to such conciliation court department.

CCP 1769 authorizes the court to "make such orders in respect to the conduct of the spouses and the subject matter of the controversy as the court deems necessary to preserve the marriage but in no event shall such orders be effective for more than 30 days from the filing of the petition."

Procedure 

See CCP 1730 to 1732, 1740 to 1747, and 1760 to 1772, inclusive CCP 1770 provides that "during a period of 30 days after the filing of the petition for concilation, neither spouse shall file any action for divorce, annulment of marriage or separate maintenance"

Petition Forms 

CCP 1764 provides that clerk shall furnish forms

IX

FORECLOSURE OF MORTGAGES AND TRUST DEEDS 

Explanation

A mortgage is a contract by which specific property is hypothecated as security for the performance of an act, without the necessity of a change of possession. (CC 

2920) A mortgage of real property shall not be deemed a conveyance, whatever its terms, so as to enable the owner of the mortgage to recover possession of the real property without a foreclosure and sale. (CCP 744)

A trust deed is a conveyance of property to a person or corporation as trustee, for the purpose of securing a debt or other obligation, with a power of sale in the trustee, upon default, and of applying the proceeds in payment thereof.

The beneficiary or the trustee named in a deed of trust or mortgagee named in a mortgage with power of sale upon real property or any interest therein to secure a debt or other obligation or the successor in interest of any of them, shall have the right to bring suit to foreclose the same in the manner and subject to the provisions, rights, and remedies relating to the foreclosure of a mortgage upon such property. (CCP 725a) 

A trust deed may be foreclosed by the same procedure as a mortgage, except the complaint must state that the trust deed is being foreclosed as a mortgage. 

Note: 

As to notes secured by mortgage or trust deed executed subsequent to September 19, 1939 (except as to public utilities issues and bonds approved by commissioner of corporations), no deficiency judgment can be secured except after sale on foreclosure by court proceeding. (CCP 580d) (originally enacted as 580c, effective Sept. 19, 1939, and reenacted as 580d, effective March 6, 1941)

A mortgage is foreclosed by the mortgagee because of default in the terms of the mortgage, such as nonpayment of principal or interest when due, nonpayment of taxes or insurance or breach of other terms stated in the mortgage as grounds for declaring the debt secured by the mortgage due and collectible. 

Preliminary Step 

Secure a foreclosure report from a reputable title company. This foreclosure report is important, because from it much information necessary in preparing the complaint is obtained. It shows the exact condition of the title; who owns the property and its incumbrances; it specifies all the persons who are necessary or proper parties to the foreclosure action, and discloses the status of the interested individual or corporation, and any legal incapacities. Procedure 

1. Copy Exhibit 

If mortgage or trust deed is to be used as an exhibit prepare one copy of each for each copy of complaint. If forms identical to the mortgage or trust deed upon which suit is brought are available these properly filled in can be used; otherwise the entire mortgage or trust deed, including endorsements of recorder, must be copied. 

2. Complaint (Form p 217) 

Before starting complaint have at hand foreclosure report, note and mortgage or trust deed, and any receipts for taxes, insurance or advances made by plaintiff. Prepare †1 original 

1 copy for service on each defendant

MORTGAGE AND TRUST DEED FORECLOSURES 213 

Contents Caption 

(a) Title of Court 

(b) Parties (1) Plaintiff—mortgagee, beneficiary or assignee (2) Defendant-mortgagor or trustor and others exactly as named in foreclosure report; add a few fictitious names. 

(c) Title of Document 

(Complaint (To Foreclose Mortgage)) (d) Introduction

Body-Allegations 

(a) Status of plaintiff and defendant (b) Fictitious name paragraph (c) Execution of note and set forth note (d) Execution of mortgage or trust deed as security (e) That plaintiff is now the owner and holder of the note (f) and (g) Various defaults in principal and interest, taxes, etc. (h) That by reason of the defaults plaintiff exercises his option to declare all the remainder of the debt to be immediately due and payable (i) Interests and liens of other parties (j) Attorney's fees and cost of title report, taxes, etc.

Prayer Exhibit-Copy of mortgage or trust deed with endorsements of recorder

Verification (see VERIFICATIONS, p 463)

3. Summons 

Prepare original, one copy for each defendant, and office copy if desired. Copies of summons are attached to copies of complaint. Original summons is not attached to original complaint. 

4. Lis Pendens

Prepare original for recording and office copy. (See LIS PENDENS, p 453) 

5. Covers

Prepare sufficient covers in counties where used, for original complaint and service copies; verification, usually on cover, should be filled in; name of plaintiff verifying same, and name of notary should be typed on copies and noted on office copy; original verification to be signed by party and notary, and notary's seal affixed.

Original complaint and have summons issued 

7. Record

Lis Pendens in office of County Recorder 

8. Conform Copies of Summons 

Copies of summons on the service complaints should be filled in to conform with the original summons, as to date, number, and name of deputy signing same. 

9. Service 

Give service copies of summons and complaint to process server; after service has been made on defendants, affidavit of service is made on the original summons, setting forth the date and place where the defendants were served; in making his return the names of the defendants

MORTGAGE AND TRUST DEED FORECLOSURES 

215 served should be stated exactly as given in the summons; fees for service and mileage, together with notary fee should be stated; it is then sworn to before a notary public, or other officer authorized to administer oaths. 

10. Note on calendar the date to enter default 

11. If appearance is made, follow same procedure as general procedure in other civil actions. 

CHAPTER III, p 23) 

12. Default (See If no answer is filed within the statutory time, prepare original and office copy of Request for Entry of Default (a printed form) to be signed by attorney. Also prepare a dismissal of action against the unused fictitious names. File request, dismissal and original summons. Check records in two or three days after filing to see if default has been entered. 

13. Decree of Foreclosure

After default is entered, or after decree of foreclosure is ordered by court, prepare the decree of foreclosure (obtain printed form from court commissioner or county clerk in counties where provided). In default cases the decree should be in the hands of the clerk in the presiding judge's office, with the note, mortgage or trust deed, receipts, cost bill, and the county clerk's file at such time before hearing as required in court rules. 

After the decree of foreclosure is signed by the judge it is entered in the records of the county clerk.

Ascertain date of entry and note on copy (and office copy) of decree.

Send copy to a commissioner of sales, or the sheriff, as provided in the decree and order of sale, with instructions to have notice of sale published in a designated newspaper. 

The sheriff or commissioner sends the attorney copy of published notice of sale. 

Note date of sale on calendar. 

14. Sale 

At the date of sale the attorney usually attends the commissioner's or sheriff's sale to bid in the property. The property is sold to the highest bidder, and the purchaser is furnished with a certificate of sale by the commissioner or sheriff, a duplicate of which is filed for record in the office of the county recorder. This certificate should give the description of the property, price bid and paid, and date of judgment under which the sale was made and must state whether the property is subject to redemption. (CCP 700a, 725a) 

15. Property sold under a mortgage or trust deed foreclosure may be redeemed by the mortgagor or trustor or his successor in interest, or by a creditor having a lien by judgment or mortgage on the property sold subsequent to that on which the property was sold, or by any other junior encumbrancer, within 12 months (3 months in cases provided for in CCP 725a) from date of sale. (CCP 701, 702, 725a) If no redemption is made within 12 months (3 months in cases mentioned in CCP 725a) after the sale, the purchaser or his assignee is entitled to a deed from the sheriff or the commissioner. (CCP 703) If redemption is made by the debtor, he is entitled to a certificate of redemption from the person to whom payment is made, which certificate must be filed and recorded in the office of the county recorder. (CCP 703)

FORECLOSURE THROUGH EXERCISE OF 

Explanation

POWER OF SALE 

(CC 2924) When the deed of trust or mortgage confers upon the trustee, mortgagee or other person power to sell the hypothecated property upon default such power may be exercised without any court proceeding, subject, however, to the provisions of CC 2924, other pertinent provisions of Div 3, Pt 4, Title 14, Ch 2, Art 1 CC 

and CCP 692, 694 and 580d. Notwithstanding a power of sale in the instrument the trustee, beneficiary or mortgagee may elect to proceed by court foreclosure and must do so if he intends to secure a deficiency judgment. CCP 725a, 580d (formerly 580c) 

Procedure (CC 2924)

When a power of sale is conferred, that power may be exercised by substantially the following procedure: (a) The trustee or beneficiary shall first file for record in the office of the County Recorder of the county in which the property is situate, a Notice of Default. (b) Not less than 3 months shall thereafter elapse. (c) After the expiration of said 3 months period the trustee or other person authorized to make the sale shall give notice of sale, setting forth the time and place in the manner and for a time not less than required by law for sale of real property upon execution. (See CCP 692) (d) CCP 694 provides the manner of conducting the sale.

Prepare †1 original for filing 

1 copy for office copies for any person or persons filing a request for copy of notice

Record original notice of default in the office of County Recorder of the county where the property is situate.

Mail copy of notice to persons who have recorded request for special notice. 

If trustor's or mortgagor's address is not set forth in the document to be foreclosed and there is no recorded request of such trustor or mortgagor, for notice, a copy of the notice of default must be published at least once a week, for at least 4 weeks, commencing within 10 days after recordation, or a copy of the notice of default may be personally served on the trustor or mortgagor in lieu of publication, such personal service to be made within 10 days after filing notice of default or before publication is completed.

Procedure (CCP 692) (1) Notice of sale must be published once a week for at least 20 days in newspaper of general circulation, published in the city or township in which the property is situate. (2) Post 1 copy of notice in a public place in city or township in which property is located and 1 copy in conspicuous place on property to be sold, at least 20 days before date of sale. (3) Procure affidavit of publishing and affidavit of posting prior to date of sale.

Explanation

UNLAWFUL DETAINER 

(CCP 1161-1179a, etc.) 

An unlawful detainer suit is a quick action by a landlord against a tenant for possession of real property, i. e., it is a special or summary proceeding of a civil nature by which possession of real property can be quickly recovered in certain cases. It is maintainable 

ONLY against a person in possession and in general, neither counterclaim nor cross complaint is permissible. (Markham v Fralick, 2 Cal (2d) 221; Knight v Black, 19 CA 518; where there ceases to be an issue as to possession see Servais v. Klein, 112 CA 26, 33). It is a specialized form of procedure although similar to other types of actions and takes precedence on the calendar over ordinary civil actions. 

IMMEDIATE POSSESSION upon filing complaint may be had on order of court based on verified complaint or on affidavit stating matters required by CCP 

1166a, and on filing undertaking. 

ATTACHMENT may issue when complaint alleges rent due and unsecured. (CCP 537(4)) 

Brought When

Tenant or subtenant continues in possession 

1. After expiration of term (CCP 1161) 

2. After default in rent (CCP 1161) 

3. After failure to perform conditions of lease (CCP 1161)

4. By tenant to get possession from a subtenant, employee, agent or licensee (CCP 1161) 5. After perfection of title following sale on execution or under foreclosure of mortgage or deed of trust where person so holding over is the original execution or foreclosure debtor or claims under him (CCP 1161a) 6. When property has been sold by occupant or person under whom he claims and title has been perfected 

Notices Necessary Preliminary to 

Unlawful Detainer Action 

1. Tenancy at Will (when tenant not in default) 

Ordinarily at least 30 day notice to quit (CC 

789, 1946; CCP 1162) 

2. Indefinite or Unspecified Term (Term is presumed to be for the length of time adopted for the estimation of the rent. In absence of an agreement regarding the length of time or the rent, the hiring is presumed to be monthly.) (CC 1944) Notice to Quit must be given at least as long before the expiration as the term of the hiring, not exceeding one month-but when term is one month or longer the one month's notice may be given at any time and rent is then payable to and including termination date. The parties may agree at time tenancy is created that a notice of 

7 days or more may be sufficient. (CC 1946)

As to possible change in rate of rent during tenancy see CC 827.

3. Default in Rent 

3 day notice to pay rent or quit; if forfeiture of lease is to be claimed, landlord's election so to do must be stated in the notice. (CCP 1174) 

4. Breach in covenant of lease 

3 day notice to perform (if covenant is still performable) or to quit; see 3-Default in Rent re notice of election to forfeit lease.

ΧΙ 

(CCP 1183-1203) Explanation

A mechanic's lien is a charge imposed upon specific property by which said property is made security for the reasonable value of labor or materials furnished by mechanics, materialmen, contractors, subcontractors and others who contribute labor or skill in the construction of a building, tunnel or other improvement on land, or on a mining claim.

The lien or suit thereon does not impair or affect the right of a person to maintain a personal action on the debt and to attach and execute thereon. (CCP 1197)

The lien is forfeited by false notice of claim or charge for work or materials not performed or furnished. 

A deficiency judgment may be obtained in same manner as in foreclosure of mortgage.

Mechanic's lien procedure is extremely technical. The statute applicable to any particular case must in each instance be strictly complied with and the material on this subject is to be regarded as suggestive only and not complete. 

Priority (CCP 1186)

The mechanic's lien has priority over any lien, mortgage, deed of trust, or incumbrance which may have attached subsequent to the time when the building was commenced, work done, or materials were furnished; also over any lien, mortgage, deed of trust, or other incumbrance of which the lienor had no notice and which was unrecorded at the time when building was commenced, work done, or materials were commenced to be furnished.

Notice of Nonresponsibility (CCP 1192) 

The owner or any person having or claiming any estate in the land may, within ten days after obtaining knowledge of construction, give notice that he will not be responsible for the same by posting a notice on the property and recording a verified copy thereof. Said notice must contain a description of the property with the name and nature of the title or interest of the person giving the same, name of purchaser under contract, if any, or lessee if known. This notice may be verified by anyone having a knowledge of the facts on behalf of the owner or person for whose protection the notice is given.

Notice of Completion (CCP 1187) 

The owner is required to record a verified notice of completion of improvement within 10 days after completion. 

Time Within Which to File Lien If Notice of Completion IS filed 

(a) 60 days after completion of his work by original contractor (b) 30 days after completion of work by any claimant after he has ceased to perform labor or furnish materials (Filing notice of completion held to start time running for filing notice of mechanic's lien. Reinhart Lumber and Planing Mills 

Co. v. Hladek, 259 P 363. But mechanic's lien must be filed within 90 days after completion. Simms Brick Co. v. Hetzel, 236 P 357, 72 C A 1 If Notice of Completion NOT filed (a) Within 90 days after completion of said work for all persons.

Procedure 1. Record lien with County Recorder within required time 

2. If settled without suit record Release of Mechanic's Lien 

3. File verified complaint within 90 days after recording lien (for extension of time by filing notice of credit see CCP 1190; this limitation has no reference to the limitation prescribed in CCP 1184a, which fixes the period within which action must be brought to enforce claim after the "stop notice" provided for in CCP 1184) 

4. Record Lis Pendens (see LIS PENDENS, p 453) 

5. Balance of procedure same as in other civil actions Forms Mechanic's Lien-Obtain printed form from stationery store

Release of Mechanic's Lien-Obtain printed form from stationery store

XII 

, COMMON COUNTS, PROMMISSORY NOTE, AND NEGLIGENCE COMMON COUNTS 

Explanation

The term "Common Counts" refers to the old common law forms of pleading, often fictitiously, certain causes of action. By using the common count a brief conclusion of indebtedness is alleged instead of the specific facts required by code pleading. Although opposed to the code theory, and sometimes characterized as "slovenly," the use of the common counts in California is well established. They are good against both general and special demurrers.

The most generally used common counts are: (with forms for specific allegations) (For other allegations of complaint, caption, verification, signature, etc., see 

COMPLAINT, p 26 et seq.) 

(1) Goods Sold and Delivered (Reasonable value) 

(a) (Within two years last past) (or) On or 

19......, at about 

California, plaintiff sold and delivered to defendant goods, wares and merchandise of the reasonable value of $................, for which said defendant agreed to pay plaintiff. (b) No part of said sum has been paid (except $......... and there is now due, owing and unpaid $--------------).

Goods Sold and Delivered (Agreed amount) 

(a) (Within two years last past) (or) On or about 19......, at 

California, plaintiff sold and delivered to defendant goods, wares and merchandise, for which defendant agreed to pay plaintiff the sum of $.......... (b) No part of said sum has been paid. 

(2) Account Stated 

(a) (Within four years last past) (or) On or about 

19......, at 

California, an account was stated by and between plaintiff and defendant, wherein and whereby it was agreed that defendant was indebted to plaintiff in the sum of $............... (b) No part of said sum has been paid (except $................. and there is now due, owing and unpaid $...-------------) . 

(3) Open Book Account 

(a) (Within four years last past) (or) On or about 

19........, at 

California, plaintiff furnished to defendant at his special instance and request, upon an open book account, goods, wares, and merchandise of the aggregate agreed (reasonable) value of $.......... (b) No part of said sum has been paid (except $........ and there is now due, owing and unpaid $……..…………………………….. ..) 

Frequently 1, 2 and 3, are used in one complaint, 2 and 3 being designated as the second and third causes of action.

(4) Account for Services (Agreed amount) (a) (Within two years last past) (or) On or about 19........, at 

California, defendant became indebted to plaintiff in the agreed sum of $................ on account of work, labor, and services performed by plaintiff as at defend- ant's special instance and request. (b) No part of said sum has been paid (except $........ .). 

(5) Work and Labor (Reasonable value) 

(a) Between 

19........, and 19........, at 

California, plaintiff rendered services to defendant as a

XIII 

SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS General Laws ADOPTION 

(CC 221, et seq.)

Any minor child may be adopted by an adult person. (CC 221) The person adopting a child must be at least ten years older than the person adopted. (CC 222) 

A legitimate child cannot be adopted without the consent of parents, if living.

Consent of both parents must be obtained to the adoption of a legitimate child, except if the mother has been granted custody and the father fails to provide for the child's support, he being able so to do, for a period of one year, then the consent of the mother alone is necessary, but the father must be served with a copy of a citation requiring him to appear at the hearing. (CC 

224) (For requirement where parent is feeble-minded or insane, see CC 224 (4))

An illegitimate child cannot be adopted without the consent of the mother EXCEPT: (a) Where mother has been judicially deprived of the custody and control of the child by order of a Juvenile Court. (b) Where child has been deserted without provision for its identification. (c) Where mother has relinquished the child for adoption. (CC 224) (d) Where mother has been declared feeble-minded or insane, and incapable of supporting or controlling the child. (CC 224 (4)) 

Note: An illegitimate child becomes legitimated by subsequent marriage of parents (CC 215), by public acknowledgment by father (CC 

230) and, for limited purposes, by written acknowledgment signed by the father before a witness as provided in Pr C 255.

SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS 

257 The consent of a child over the age of 12 years is necessary to its adoption. (CC 225)

A father or mother may relinquish a child for adoption by a written statement signed before two subscribing witnesses and acknowledged before a notary, or before a secretary of an organization duly licensed by the Department of Social Welfare to find homes for children and place children for adoption. Certified copy of the relinquishment must be filed with the State Department of Social Welfare. (CC 224m) 

A child, when adopted, may take the family name of the person adopting. After adoption, the two shall sustain towards each other the legal relation of parent and child. (CC 228) 

General Procedure (CC 226) 

1. Prepare petition for adoption in the Superior

Court of the County in which the petitioner resides and file with the County Clerk (No fee- Pol C 4300a) †1 original 

1 copy for State Dept. of Social Welfare 

2. Clerk will notify the State Department of Social 

Welfare at Sacramento in writing 

3. At hearing have prepared (a) Agreement of Adoption (CC 227) (b) Consent to Adoption, where required 

(c) Order of Adoption 

Note: Child and persons desiring to adopt child should be present at hearing.

Order to Show Cause 

Secure printed form from county clerk or legal newspaper.

Explanation PREROGATIVE WRITS 

(CCP 1067-1110b) The most frequently used writs are Mandate, Review, and Prohibition.

A Writ of Mandate issues to any inferior tribunal, corporation, board or person, to compel the performance of an act which the law specially enjoins as a duty resulting from an office, trust, or station, or to compel the admission of a party to a right or office to which he is entitled and from which he is unlawfully precluded by such tribunal, corporation, board, or person. (CCP 1085)

A Writ of Review issues when an inferior tribunal, board or officer exercising judicial functions has exceeded its jurisdiction and there is no appeal, nor any plain, speedy, or adequate remedy at law. (CCP 1068)

A Writ of Prohibition issues to arrest the proceedings of any tribunal, corporation, board or person exercising judicial functions when such proceedings are without or in excess of its jurisdiction. (CCP 1102)

By Whom Granted 

May be issued by Supreme Court, District Court of Appeal, or Superior Court, but not by Municipal Court or by Justices' Courts or other inferior court. Procedure 

1. Prepare verified petition 

2. Alternative writ 

3. Points and authorities *+1 original of each for filing 1 for service upon each adverse party respondent and real party in interest  *Consult rules of Supreme Court and DCA for number of copies when to be filed therein

SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS 

267 

4. File original petition *5. Present petition, alternative writ and points and authorities to judge for signature 

6. If writ is granted judge will fix date of hearing and endorse on the writ "Let the within writ issue Judge" 7. Clerk will issue writ and place seal of court on same 

8. Serve copy of petition, writ and points and authorities upon each adverse party personally 9. Attach proof of service to original writ and file same at least 1 day before date of hearing **10. Return to writ may be by demurrer or by verified answer, or by both at same time Prepare 

1. Demurrer or 

2. Answer or 

3. Both Demurrer and Answer 

4. If Demurrer, accompany same with Points and Authorities +1 original for filing 

1 for service 

11. If answer raises issues of fact evidence is introduced by both parties on return day or at a later date fixed by court 12. At hearing-if court orders peremptory writ to be issued, prepare +1 original 1 copy for service on each adverse party personally 

1 office copy 13. Serve copy of peremptory writ on each adverse party personally 

14. Make proof of service and file same *Unless otherwise ordered application must show proof of service upon respondent and real party in interest, who have 5 days to file points and authorities in opposition. CCP 1107 **Return to writ of review is normally a transcript of the record made up by clerk. Caution: Responsibility is on applicant to see that full record is presented

XIV 

Estates, Joint Tenancy, Guardian and Ward 

Probate Procedure-Estates General Outline Jurisdiction-Superior Court 

Probate Code 301 et seq.

All sections herein mentioned refer to the Probate Code of the State of California unless otherwise specified Intestate 

1. File petition for letters of administration (440) 

2. Notice of hearing (441) 

Contest to petition (442) 

3. Hearing 

(a) Testimony establishing death, residence, etc. (443) (b) Order appointing administrator (c) Bond (541) (d) Issuance of letters (500) 

4. Request for appointment of appraiser or order appointing appraiser (605) Testate 

1. File petition to probate will or Petition for letters of administration with will annexed (326) together with 

Original will if not already filed 

2. Notice of hearing (327) 

Contest of will-before probate (370) 

3. Hearing 

(a) Testimony of subscribing witness and proof of will (329) (b) Order admitting will (c) Bond, if required (541) (d) Issuance of letters (500) 

4. Request for appointment of appraiser, or order appointing appraiser (605)

NOTE: Probate of will may be contested any time within six months after admission of will to probate (380) 

5. Administration of Estate 

(a) Publication of notice to creditors (700) (b) Collection and care of assets (571 et seq.) (c) Inventory and appraisement (600)

Setting apart 

Homestead, exempt 

Property and family allowance (660 to 683) 

Request for special notice (1200)

Any time after issuance of letters (1202) (d) Liquidating assets and raising funds (750 to 900) 764A 771A 842A (e) Payment of debts, expenses of administration, etc. (950) 

Partial distribution (1000) 

Ratable distribution (1010) 

Claims of Creditors (705 et seq.) 

Inheritance Tax (1024) 

Federal and state income tax 

Federal estate tax; Real and

Personal Property Tax 

(f) Elapse of six months from date of first publication of notice to creditors before estate may be closed 

6. Closing Estate (a) Accounts-current and final-petition for distribution (b) Hearing on final account and petition for distribution (c) Filing receipts of distributees (d) Final discharge (1068) 

General Provisions and Suggestions 1. If papers are filed in a branch department of the 

Superior Court (such as Long Beach, Santa Monica, Pasadena, Pomona, etc.) they must be filed in duplicate. 

2. Particular attention should be given to hearing dates and dates of sales, when preparing a petition in order that the dates will conform to those listed in the published notices. 

3. Inspect all papers before filing. The court rejects documents as insufficient in cases where the notary seal is missing, despite the signature of the officer taking the oath. Be sure that all petitions are signed, and verified when necessary. 

4. The attorney's name, address and telephone number must appear on each document filed. Check with the 

Superior Court Rules to see that the papers filed conform to those rules.

5. When papers are filed just prior to a hearing date it should be so indicated to clerk at time of filing in order that the paper will be placed in the case file before the hearing. 

6. When counter petitions are filed and different dates of hearings have been set, the court will continue the earlier and have them heard together. (442) 

7. It is important to correctly state the legal description of real property affected by probate proceedings. Particular care should be given to description of real property in a final account and petition for distribution, in order that decree of distribution will correctly describe the property and distribute it to persons entitled thereto. 

8. The attorney should definitely ascertain and state in any paper filed with reference to real property whether the property is registered under the Torrens system. If it is, appropriate directions to the 

Registrar of Land Titles should be prayed for in any petition affecting it. 

9. A note or mortgage should not be drawn in favor of "The Estate of" a deceased person, minor or incompetent. Instead it should be in favor of "The heirs or devisees of John Smith, deceased," or in favor of "John Smith, a minor," or "John Smith, an incompetent." It should not be drawn in favor of the administrator, executor or guardian. 

10. Likewise, deeds should not be made to guardians, administrators, executors, etc., unless the phrase 'subject to the administration of the estate" is added, but should be made to the minor or to the heirs or devisees of .... deceased, subject to the administration of the estate of said decedent. 

11. In order to facilitate the work of the court attorneys should use the blank forms of petition, etc., furnished by the clerk's office. Otherwise, attorneys should follow the general outline of clerk's office forms.

PETITION FOR LETTERS OF ADMINISTRATION 

When the deceased leaves no will he is said to have died intestate. In such cases the court will appoint an administrator to administer the estate upon petition of an interested person. (422) PROCEDURE for Issuance of Letters 

1. Prepare petition for letters of administration (Printed form available in most counties) †1 original 

 setting forth (440): (a) Jurisdictional facts date and place of death place of residence of deceased whether property of the deceased within jurisdiction of the court (b) Names, ages and post office addresses of heirs so far as known (c) Character and estimated value of estate, including estimated annual income from the property (d) That due search has been made and no will found (e) Right of applicant to letters (f) Petition should be verified to gain benefits of CCP 2009 in uncontested matters 

2. File original petition with county clerk 

Fee $7.00 in some counties there is an additional posting fee of 50¢

3. Clerk sets time and place of hearing (441) 

4. Notice given by clerk by posting and mailing to heirs (441) 

Clerk files his affidavit of mailing and posting 

5. If no contest (a) Arrange for petitioner to be present at hearing or a witness to testify as to fact of death and that decedent died intestate, etc. (443) unless court has accepted verified petition in lieu of oral testimony as permitted by CCP 2009. (b) Arrange for surety or personal bond of administrator (541) 

6. Hearing-have prepared: (a) Order appointing administrator** †1 original 

 (b) Letters of administration (500) +1 original (with oath signed by the administrator and sworn to before 

Notary Public or County Clerk) 

1 office copy 1 copy to be certified for each bank account, safe deposit box, issues of securities, etc. 

7. Order appointing appraisers can be signed at hearing on petition for letters-(In Los Angeles county request for appointment of appraiser should be filed) **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department, unless court instructs attorney to prepare same)

PETITION FOR PROBATE OF WILL 

When the deceased leaves a will he is referred to as having died testate. Any executor, devisee or legatee named in a will, or any other person interested in the estate may, at any time after the death of the testator, petition the court having jurisdiction to have the will proved. (323) 

The custodian of a will must deliver it to the clerk of the Superior Court having jurisdiction or to the executor named therein within 30 days after being informed of the death of the maker thereof. (320, 324) PROCEDURE for Issuance of Letters 

1. Prepare several copies of will (at least 4) 

2. Prepare petition for probate of will (printed form usually available) †1 original  and attach a copy of the will to each 

Contents of Petition 

(a) Jurisdictional facts date and place of death place of residence of deceased whether deceased left property within the jurisdiction of court (b) Whether the person named as executor consents to act or renounces his right to letters testamentary (c) The names, ages and residences of the heirs, devisees and legatees of the decedent, so far as known to petitioner

(d) The character and estimated value of the property of the estate, including the estimated annual income, if any (e) Due execution of will (f) If no executor named, qualifications of petitioner to to act act as administrator with will annexed 

3. File with County Clerk (a) Original will (b) Original petition for probate of will (c) Pay $7.00 filing fee (Designate name of newspaper for publication) 4. The clerk will set time and place of hearing, not less than 10 days nor more than 30 days after filing 

5. Notices (327, 328) (a) Clerk will give notice by publication in newspaper of general circulation (b) Affidavit of publication is made in duplicate by publisher. Original should be filed with the county clerk. Check after time for filing affidavit of publication has arrived to see that affidavit has been made and filed. (327) (c) Copies of notice of hearing of petition must be mailed or personally served upon heirs, legatees and devisees, and executors who are not petitioning, at least 10 days before the hearing, not including the day of hearing. 328) 

NOTE: In Los Angeles County mailing is usually done by newspaper making publication, at request of attorney 

In San Francisco mailing is done by attorney for petitioner 

In some counties clerk is furnished with addressed, stamped envelopes so that he may attend to mailing of notices (Check with County Clerk for procedure on this notice)

(d) Affidavit of mailing is prepared by person who mailed notices, and original filed with County Clerk. 

6. If petitioner is not executor or executrix named in will, secure and file renunciation of right to act by person named in will, and petition may be made for letters of administration with the will annexed. (406) or

If the person named in the will as executor fails to petition the court for probate and letters within 30 days he is held to have renounced his right to letters and the court may appoint any other competent person unless good cause for delay is shown. (324) 

7. Witnesses (329) (a) Arrange for one or more subscribing witnesses to testify at hearing (b) If holographic will, arrange for witness to testify regarding handwriting (CCP 19431945) (c) If witness resides in state but out of county or more than 50 miles from place of hearing, or will be absent when testimony required, or is too infirm to attend, testimony may be taken by deposition (CCP 2021). Follow same procedure as outlined below for depositions of witnesses out of state. 

8. Witnesses-if out of state (CCP 2024 et seq.) (a) Fill out form of affidavit for commission to take deposition of subscribing witness (b) File affidavit and obtain order directing clerk to issue commission (c) Have commission to take deposition issued

(d) Obtain photostatic or photographic copy of will after will is filed showing filing stamp, or photostatic or photographic copy may be certified by clerk as a true and correct copy of original will on file (e) Have photostatic or photographic copy of will attached to interrogatories and send with commission to take deposition to the notary public named as commissioner. The commissioner returns the commission and the interrogatories to the Superior Court 

9. For hearing-have prepared: (a) Order admitting will to probate** †1 original 

 (b) Letters testamentary or letters of administration with will annexed (500) †1 original, with oath executed and sworn to before a notary public 

1 office copy 1 copy to be certified for each bank account, safe deposit box, issues of securities, etc. (c) Arrange for bond if bond is required (541) 

10. Order for appointment of appraiser may be signed at hearing on petition for probate. In Los Angeles 

County file a request for appointment of appraiser after letters have been issued, and court will make order. **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department, unless court instructs attorney to prepare same)

PROCEDURE

Following Issuance of Letters 

Following the issuance of letters and appointment of executor or administrator the procedure for both testate and intestate matters is substantially as follows: 

Notice to Creditors (700 et seq.)

Notice to creditors should be given to publisher immediately following the issuance of letters. Publishers of legal newspapers usually have prepared forms of notice. Better practice is to give notice to publisher the same day letters are issued. Failure to commence publication within a reasonable time after the issuance of letters is ground for revocation of letters. (701) 

Notice must be published not less than once a week for 4 successive weeks in a newspaper of general circulation, published in the county where probate proceedings are pending. (700, 701)

In event of resignation, death or removal of an executor or administrator prior to expiration of time for filing claims, but after the publication itself has been completed, it is not necessary to publish a new notice. (701)

Affidavit of publication must be filed with the clerk within 30 days of date of last publication. (702) The legal newspaper usually prepares affidavit and either files original with clerk or gives it to attorney for filing. If affidavit is not so filed the time within which claims may be presented is extended for a period equal to the period of delay in filing affidavit of publication.

Inventory and Appraisement 

Inventory and appraisement should be filed within 3 months after appointment of the administrator or executor. (600) 

1. Prepare inventory and appraisement +1 original to file with clerk 

1 copy to file with clerk for assessor 

1 for inheritance tax appraiser and as many other copies as may be required 

2. Have clerk certify the appointment of the appraiser on original inventory and appraisement before sending to appraiser. 

3. Forward original to appraiser (some appraisers require a copy for their files) 

4. When appraisement is returned conform copies to original 

5. File foriginal and 1 copy with county clerk

TAXES 

The executor or administrator should make all tax returns necessary as soon as possible after letters testamentary or letters of administration are issued. These returns should be made and taxes paid before distribution, to relieve the executor or administrator from personal liability therefor.

Inheritance Tax (Inheritance Tax Act of 1935, and amendments thereto) (1) As soon as an appraiser has been appointed for the estate, obtain from the appraiser or from the Inheritance Tax Department in your district form No. 22 (Inheritance Tax Affidavit). This form must be filed whether there is a full probate proceeding or not, and whether the estate is taxable or not. It must also accompany every inventory and petition to terminate joint tenancy. 

Have the form executed by the executor or administrator and send to the inheritance tax appraiser, together with 2 copies of the will, and 

2 copies of the inventory and appraisement. To avoid delay give full information, as requested on the form. (2) If any of the property is community property form No. 3 (Affidavit Concerning Community 

Property) should be filed with form No. 22. (3) If estate is that of a nonresident, form No. 2 should be filed with form No. 22. (4) If a hearing to determine inheritance tax is necessary the inheritance tax appraiser will advise you of the time and place. (5) Upon the filing of report of inheritance tax appraiser the clerk of the Superior Court gives notice of such filing to all persons interested and to all persons chargeable with any tax. Objections to said report should be filed within 10 days thereafter, and if none are made court makes an order confirming said report and fixing tax in accordance therewith. (Sec. 16(2) Inheritance Tax Act of 1935)

(6) Any inheritance tax imposed shall be and remain a lien upon the interest of decedent in the property which passes or is transferred and the person to whom such interest passes, and the administrators, executors and trustees shall be liable for payment of such tax. (Sec. 3, Inheritance Tax Act of 

1935) Any administrator, executor, or trustee, shall deduct the tax from any legacy or property for distribution, if not collected from the legatee or person entitled to such property. (Sec. 9(1) 

Inheritance Tax Act of 1935) (7) The inheritance tax must be paid and inheritance tax receipt or certificate of no tax be on file before the final account and petition for distribution is filed. (1024) If inheritance tax is due it should be paid within 6 months of date of death, to take advantage of the 5% discount provided by Sec. 

7(1) Inheritance Tax Act of 1935. 

Federal Estate Tax (U. S. Revenue Acts of 1926, 1932, as amended by 

1934, 1935 and 1936 Revenue Acts and Regulations 80 relating to Estate Tax)

If gross estate exceeds $40,000 in value at date of death a Federal Estate Tax Return must be filed. 

Failure to file will render executor or administrator liable for penalties. (Sec. 201 (b) Rev. Act 1935) (Gross estate includes, in addition to property passing under will or the intestate laws, transfers made during decedent's life without consideration, joint estates with right of survivorship, tenancies by the entirety, life insurance even though payable to beneficiaries, property over which deceased executed general power of appointment, and dower, curtesy or statutory

estate in lieu thereof, of the surviving spouse. (Sec. 302, Revenue Act of 1926, and amendments) 

(1) File Preliminary Notice of Estate Tax 

(form 704) in duplicate with Collector of 

Internal Revenue in district of decedent's domicile within 2 months after decedent's death, or if executor or administrator qualifies within such period within 2 months after qualification (Arts. 57 to 60, Regulations 80, relating to Estate Tax). 

(2) File Estate Tax Return (form 706) in duplicate within 15 months after date of death with Collector of Internal Revenue in district of decedent's domicile. Tax is also due and payable at that time, unless extension granted. (Sec. 304(a) Revenue 

Act of 1926) (Arts. 63 to 75, Regulations 80, relating to Estate Tax) Printed form available (a) If decedent was a resident and died testate 2 copies of the will must be attached to to the return, 1 certified (b) Other documents required for nonresident and nonresident alien (See 

Art. 65, Regulations 80) (c) Give full information as requested in the return, in order to avoid delay to the estate

For further information consult the Collector of Internal Revenue in your district, who will furnish forms and all instructions necessary.

Income Tax (1) State (Personal Income Tax Act of 1935, as amended)

All state income tax returns should be filed by executor or administrator, both for decedent prior to his death, and for estate during the period of administration, and all taxes due thereunder paid before the discharge of executor or administrator, in order to relieve the fiduciary from liability for nonpayment of taxes under 

(Sec. 26(b) of the Personal Income Tax Act of 

1935, as amended).

A certificate that all taxes due or to become due from decedent or estate for whom the fiduciary acts have been paid or secured will not be issued unless all the following requirements are complied with: (a) In the case of an estate, a return on form 540 or 541, or both if required, must be filed for each taxable year ending on or after January 1, 1935, or an affidavit under oath must be filed by the fiduciary stating that the net income for each such year did not amount to $1,000 or more and the gross income did not amount to $5,000 or more (form 542). 

Except as provided in Sec. 12(c) of the Act, the net income of estate or trust shall be computed in same manner and on same basis as in case of an individual. An exemption of $1000 is allowed the estate for the taxable year. (b) Although no tax will become due from an estate for the year in which it is distributed, since all income will be either properly paid or credited to the beneficiaries and, hence, deductible under Sec. 

12(d) (2) and (3) of the Act, a return on form 541 for such year must be filed

at time the certificate is requested, regardless of amount of gross or net income for such year. 

Such return must disclose all income to be distributed to beneficiaries upon the final distribution of estate as well as all income properly paid or credited to beneficiaries during the year covered by the return and prior to final distribution. (c) A return on form 540, 540NR, or 540A must be filed for each taxable year of decedent ending on or after January 1, 1935, including the taxable year in which his death occurred (if none has been filed) or an affidavit under oath must be filed by the fiduciary, setting forth amount of personal exemption to which decedent was entitled and stating that his net income for each such year did not equal or exceed such personal exemption and that his gross income did not amount to $5,000 or more. 

The return of decedent for fractional part of the taxable year in which death occurred must be filed on an accrual basis, and all items of income and expense must be accrued up to date of death, regardless of the fact decedent previously may have been filing on a cash receipts and disbursements basis. (d) Any certificate issued pursuant to the Act is issued on the assumption that the estate is to be distributed during a particular year. If, for any reason the estate is not distributed during year specified in the certificate, a return for such year or subsequent years must be filed and the tax, if any, paid or secured, before a new certificate will be issued.

A certificate is not required in connection with the distribution of an estate, unless the following factors are both present: (1) The value of the assets at date of decedent's death exceeds $20,000, and (2) one beneficiary or more lives outside Cali fornia (Sec. 26a, Personal Income Tax 

Act, as amended in 1939) Certificates are also not required upon termination of trusts or guardianships. 

Returns should be filed with Franchise Tax 

Commissioner of the State of California, Income 

Tax Division, Sacramento, California, and any tax due made payable to the State Treasurer. (2) Federal All Federal income tax returns should be filed by the executor or administrator, both for the decedent prior to his death and for the estate during the period of administration, and all taxes due thereunder paid before the discharge of the executor or administrator, in order to relieve the fiduciary from liability for nonpayment of taxes. 

The income of estates and trusts is determined in substantially the same manner and is subject to the same rates of tax as that of individuals. 

In general, the fiduciary pays the tax on income of estate or trust which is accumulated, while the beneficiaries pay tax on income which is distributed.

A return must be made by the fiduciary for estate or trust for which he acts: (a) if net income of estate or trust is $1000 or over, or (b) gross income is $5000 or over, regardless of amount of net income, and (c) if any beneficiary of estate or trust is a nonresident alien. 

(Sec. 142a, Income Tax Act of 1936)

Forms 1. 2. 

To show income retained by and taxable to estate or trust use form 1040, entitled "Individual 

Income Tax Return"

To show income which is distributed or is distributable to beneficiaries use form 1041, entitled "Fiduciary Income Tax Return" (this to cover each year of the period of probate and taxable earnings of income of estate acquired) (Forms can be obtained from local Collector of Internal Revenue) 

Time for Filing Returns

Returns of individuals and fiduciaries are due on 15th day of 3rd month after close of the fiscal year. Exemption

An estate is allowed the same personal exemption as is allowed to a single person under Section 25(b)1 of the Revenue Act. (For additional exemption see 

Sec. 163 and Sec. 25) (Consult local tax authorities on all tax matters if in doubt as to the proper procedure. They will gladly cooperate.)

County Personal and Real Property Tax 

A declaration of property statement should be filed with County Assessor on forms available in each county, between the first Monday in March and the first Monday in July of each year during administration of an estate. 

Such declaration may be handed to any deputy county assessor or may be filed at office of County Assessor.

Penalties on real estate tax are levied on first installments after December 5th and on second installments after April 20th.
