LIQUIDATING ASSETS AND RAISING FUNDS 

Assets may be liquidated and moneys raised in the following manner: 

A. Sales B. 

Borrowing Money and Mortgaging Property 

C. Leasing

D. Conveyance to Complete Contracts E. Exchange of Real Property 

A. Sales (750 et seq)

Personal Property (a) Perishable Property or Property Liable to 

Depreciate in Value or Cause Loss or Expense (770) 

May be sold without notice and without confirmation. In order to relieve the administrator of responsibility for the value of the property it is better practice to follow procedure outlined below: 

1. Prepare return of sale and petition for confirmation †1 original 

 (No notice of sale is required) 

2. Prepare order confirming sale and present order to judge for signing †1 original 

1 office copy (b) Stocks and Bonds (771) 

1. Prepare petition for order authorizing sale †1 original 

1 office copy 

If petition is for sale of stocks, bonds or other securities, it is advisable to state the name of the ex-

change where the stock is listed, if listed, and if not state that fact and supply a financial statement with petition 

2. Notice of sale may be dispensed with, (unless request for special notice under 1202 on file) if so requested in petition and set forth in the order authorizing sale (or court can order notice for shorter period); otherwise notice should be given as set forth in general procedure on sales listed below 

3. Prepare order authorizing sale** †1 original  

1 copy to be certified for each security sold 

4. Present original order to judge for signing 

Order may be obtained ex parte, if no requests for notice are filed, and if so a written waiver of notice may be secured. In such case petition should ask that notice of hearing on petition be dispensed with, as provided under (2) above 

5. To complete transfer deliver to security company 

1 certified copy of order 

1 certified copy of letters (when requested by security company) 

1 consent to transfer by Inheritance Tax Department (obtain from County Treasurer) **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department unless it is exparte order)

(c) Sales of all other Personal Property (772, 756.5) 

General Procedure— 

1. Prepare notice of sale †1 original 

3 copies to post 

2. Have notice of sale posted in 3 public places in county where proceedings are pending, at least 10 days before sale 

3. Prepare affidavit of posting and file with original notice 

4. When high bid is accepted prepare return of sale and petition for confirmation; have return signed and verified 5. File return of sale and petition for confirmation and obtain date for hearing 

6. Clerk will post notice of hearing for at least 10 days 

7. Note: Upon hearing of a petition for confirmation competitive bids are allowed when sales price exceeds $100 (756.5) 

8. At hearing on petition for confirmation-have prepared

Order confirming sale of personal property** +1 original 

1 copy to be certified if sale is made of automobile, boat, or other registered personal property 

9. Prepare bill of sale or other document necessary to convey property to purchaser 

Real Property (780 et seq.) (a) See that property has been appraised within 

1 year of the time of sale (784) (b) Prepare notice of sale, setting forth complete description of property (if property is registered land this should be included in notice) (c) (Give notice to publisher for publication at least 2 weeks before date of sale, in newspaper published in county in which property is located)

EXCEPT: When notice given by posting 

(When real property is appraised at less than $500 notice may be given by posting and publication is not required.

Post notice at the courthouse of county where property situated or building within which probate court is located -for 2 weeks before day of sale. **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree 

Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

Notice of sale and affidavit of posting should be prepared and filed with 

County Clerk of county where property is located.) (780) 

In some counties clerk posts the notice after it has been prepared by the attorney. (In case of private sale the date on or after which sale can be made must be at least 15 days from first publication or posting. It is therefore desirable to start publication 17 or 18 days beforehand when sales are private.) (d) When notice is published secure affidavit of publication from newspaper and file with County Clerk (or ascertain if filing has been made by newspaper). (e) When high bid is accepted prepare return of sale and petition for confirmation. Return must be verified and must be made within 

30 days after sale. (755) Attach bid to return of sale or file separately. (f) File return of sale and petition for confirmation; clerk will set for hearing. 

If clerk prepares notice of hearing furnish him with 2 copies of description of the property sold, which he will attach to notice and proof of posting. (g) *At hearing on return have prepared:

†1 original 

1 copy to be certified for recordation *Appearance by counsel, petitioner or purchaser **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree 

Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

(h) When order confirming sale is signed, filed and entered have copy certified and recorded in county where property is situate. (i) Prepare Administrator's or Executor's Deed for execution.

Sale of Contract to Purchase (800 et seq.) 

Follow same procedure as in sale of real property. (800) Instead of preparing deed prepare assignment of contract. (802) 

Sale of Mining Property (810 et seq.) (a) Prepare verified petition to sell (b) File with clerk, who will set petition for hearing (c) Notice of hearing given by clerk as provided in Sec. 1200 (d) Prepare for presentation at hearing

Order authorizing execution of agreement of sale or option** +1 original 

 1 copy to be certified for recording (e) Record certified copy of order in county where property is situated (f) Prepare agreement or option **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree

Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

General Rules Governing Sales 

1. When executor is given power to sell under will notice of sale is not required. (757) This may apply to both real and personal property. 

2. Returns of sales must be verified and must be made within 30 days after sale. (755) 

3. In all sales of real property, where original bond will not cover the amount to be realized from sale the court will require additional bond to be furnished before sale is confirmed. (542) 

4. Notice of hearing on return of sale must be mailed to all persons filing request for special notice at least 10 days prior to hearing, and affidavit of such mailing or proof of service made and filed. 

5. If a real estate broker is to be paid a commission on the sale, authority to pay the commission must be asked for in the return and petition for confirmation, and the commission ordered paid in the order confirming sale. (760) 

6. When property to be sold is registered under Land 

Title Law of 1914, be sure that property is so described in the petition and include in petition and decree or order that the court shall direct the 

Registrar of Titles to issue a certificate of title, or to note a memorial of the transaction, as the case may require, in accordance with such order or decree. A certified copy of the order or decree must be filed with the Registrar of Titles. (1223) 

7. If petition is filed for sale of stocks, bonds or other securities, it is advisable to state the name of the exchange where the stock is listed, if listed, and if not state that fact and supply a financial statement with petition.

B. Borrowing Money and Mortgaging Property 

(830 et seq) (a) Prepare verified petition (831) †1 original 

 (b) File with County Clerk (c) Prepare notice to be given by publication for at least 10 days, if published daily, or twice if published weekly. (1201) Also furnish clerk with notice for posting (d) Clerk will set petition for hearing and give notice of hearing as provided in (1200, 1201). (e) Obtain affidavit of publication from newspaper and file or ascertain if affidavit has been filed by newspaper direct (f) At hearing-have prepared 

Order authorizing loan** †1 original 

1 office copy 1 copy to be certified for recording (g) Record certified copy of order authorizing loan in county in which property is situated. (832) (h) Prepare necessary mortgage or deed of trust and note (833). **(In Los Angeles County order or decree is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

C. Leasing (840) (a) Prepare verified petition for leave to lease, setting forth description of property and terms of proposed lease. (841) †1 original 

 (b) File petition with clerk who will set hearing date (c) Notice by publication and posting in same manner as on mortgage (1201) (d) File affidavit of publication (e) At hearing have prepared

Order authorizing lease** 

†1 original 

1 office copy 1 copy to be certified, if lease is to be recorded (f) Prepare lease 

1 original-for lessor 

1 copy-for lessee 

1 office copy

D. Conveyance to Complete Contract (850) 

To complete contract entered into by decedent (a) Prepare verified petition for order directing conveyance of property sold on contract by decedent †1 original 

 (b) File petition with clerk-clerk will set for hearing (c) Prepare notice of hearing to be published for at least 10 days 

Note: When contract has been recorded prior to death of decedent, no publication is required, but notice of hearing of petition shall be given by posting for 10 days. (851) 

File affidavit of publication or posting. (d) At hearing have prepared 

Order directing conveyance** †1 original 

1 office copy 1 copy to be certified and recorded (if real property)

Deed or bill of sale (executed and acknowledged) (e) When order has been signed and entered record certified copy, where real property is involved. (852) (f) Deliver conveyance to purchaser **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree

Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

E. Exchange of Property (860) 

(a) Prepare petition †1 original 

Verification not required, but recommended. (b) File and have set for hearing (c) Notice of hearing by posting for 10 days (1200) (d) At hearing have prepared

Order authorizing exchange of property** 

†1 original 

1 office copy 1 copy to be certified and recorded (e) When order has been signed and entered, record certified copy, if property exchanged is real property. (f) Prepare conveyances effecting exchange of property as provided in order, have executed and acknowledged, and deliver to person to whom property conveyed. 

General Rules Re Borrowing Money and Mortgag ing, Leasing, Conveyance to Complete Contract, and

Exchange of Real Property 

1. Notice of hearing on all of the above petitions must be mailed to all persons filing request for special notice, at least 10 days prior to hearing and affidavit of such mailing or proof of service made and filed. (1202)

2. When the property is registered under the Land 

Title Law of 1914, property must be so described in notice and petition; also include in petition and order or decree that the court shall direct the 

Registrar of Titles to issue a certificate of title, or to note a memorial of the transaction, as the case may require, in accordance with such order or decree. A certified copy of order or decree must be filed with the Registrar of Titles. (1223)

PAYMENT OF DEBTS AND EXPENSES OF ADMINISTRATION Claims-General 

1. All charges against the estate must be paid in following order: (950) (a) Expenses of administration (b) Funeral expenses (c) Expenses of last illness (d) Family allowance (e) Debts having preference by the laws of the 

United States (f) Wages to the extent of $200 for services rendered to decedent within 90 days of death (g) Mortgages and other liens, in order of priority, to be paid out of proceeds of encumbered property (h) Judgments rendered against decedent in his lifetime, in order of their date (i) All other demands against estate 

Funeral expenses, expenses of last illness, family allowance and wage claims up to $200 may be paid as soon as sufficient money is received (951)

2. Procedure-presenting claim (Printed form available in most counties) (a) Prepare claim †1 original to file with clerk 1 copy to mail to attorney for executor or executor 

 (b) Claim must be verified (705) (c) If claim is on written instrument copy must be attached to claim and original exhibited on demand (706) (d) File original claim with clerk and mail copy to attorney for executor, or executor 

Note: Claim may be filed with attorney for executor, or executor, as directed in 

Notice to Creditors. 

3. Rejection of Claims 

(a) Prepare written notice of rejection (714) †1 original 

1 office copy 

1 copy to be served (b) Serve notice of rejection on claimant, or attorney for claimant, personally, or by mail. 

If address of claimant is not known service may be made by filing notice of rejection with County Clerk. (c) File original notice of rejection with proof of service (d) Claimant has 3 months from date of service of notice of rejection within which to bring suit and 10 days after filing complaint within which to file notice of said action in the estate proceedings or serve summons and complaint upon executor or administrator personally (712, 714) (e) Rejection must be endorsed on claim by executor and claim filed with clerk

4. Compromise, Compound or Settle Claims (718.5) 

Petition may be filed after 60 days from issuance of letters (a) Prepare verified petition for leave to compromise, compound or settle +1 original 

 (b) File with clerk who will set hearing date (c) Notice given by clerk by posting for at least 

10 days as provided in (1200) (d) Prepare notice of filing of such petition to be served on all persons requesting special notice, at least 10 days before hearing on such petition (e) Prepare affidavit of mailing or proof of service of such notice (f) File original notice of hearing on petition with affidavit of mailing or proof of service (g) At hearing-have prepared

Order authorizing compromise, compound or settlement** +1 original 

1 office copy 

5. Allowance of Claims (711)

When a claim is allowed by the administrator or executor before filing claim with clerk it should be (a) Endorsed by administrator or executor, with date and amount of allowance (b) Presented to judge for approval, or to Court 

Commissioner in counties having Court 

Commissioner, who will endorse his approval or rejection and the date thereof (c) Claim should then be filed **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree 

Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

PARTITION BEFORE DISTRIBUTION (1100) 

When two or more heirs, devisees, or legatees are entitled to distribution of undivided interests in any real or personal property of decedent, and they have not agreed among themselves before distribution to a partition, allotment or other division thereof, any one or more of them, or the executor or administrator, at request of any one or more of them, may petition court to make such partition, division or allotment of the property as will be equitable and will avoid the distribution of undivided interests. 

Petition for partition may be filed any time after expiration of the time to present creditors claims and before distribution has been ordered. Procedure (a) Prepare petition describing property and giving names of all persons claiming an interest in the property †1 original 

 1 copy for each person claiming an interest in the property, and for executor or administrator (b) Prepare citations directed to all persons claiming, and to executor or administrator, and have clerk issue after filing petition (c) File petition and have citations issued (d) Serve citation, with copy of petition, either personally or by publication, in same manner as a summons in a civil action (1101) (see 

SUMMONS-SERVICE, p 38)

(e) Parties served have 30 days within which to answer petition after service of citation. After 

30 days have elapsed have order of default signed by judge as to those not answering, and request clerk to set for hearing (f) Prepare notice of hearing †1 original 

 1 copy for each person who has appeared (g) Serve notice of hearing and file original with proof of service (h) After hearing-prepare decree according to court's decision (1103)** (i) If court appoints referees to partition, prepare order appointing referees and oath of referees, leaving names blank; be sure that oath is signed, sworn to and filed before proceeding (j) Have decree signed by court and record a certified copy **(In Los Angeles County decree is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

DISTRIBUTION 

Partial Distribution (1000)

Partial distribution may be applied for any time after 4 months following issuance of letters, by either an administrator, executor or any person interested. (1000) (a) Prepare petition +1 original 

 1 copy to be served on executor or administrator, if petition is made by other than executor or administrator (b) Notice of hearing by posting for 10 days by clerk, if petition is made by executor or administrator. If petition is made by other than executor or administrator in addition to notice by posting a citation must be served on executor or administrator (c) At hearing-have prepared 

Decree of partial distribution** +1 original 

1 office copy 1 copy to be certified for recording, if property distributed is real property, 1 for each county in which property is located 1 copy to be certified for each security distributed Arrange for bond as provided in (1001) **(In Los Angeles County decree is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

(d) When decree has been signed have copies of decree certified and recorded in counties where real property is located, or furnish certified copies to security companies for transfer of stocks, etc. (e) Prepare receipts to be signed by distributees (f) File receipts 

Note: Before partial distribution can be made the inheritance tax report must be on file, tax must have been fixed by court order and paid accordingly, or in lieu thereof there must be filed a written consent to the making of partial distribution signed by the State Controller, an inheritance tax attorney, or assistant inheritance tax attorney. (1001) 

Ratable Distribution (1010) 

Petition for ratable distribution may be filed any time after expiration of the time for presentation of creditors claims.

Inheritance tax must be paid prior to filing of petition, or in lieu thereof there must be filed a written consent to the making of ratable distribution signed by the State Controller, an inheritance tax attorney or assistant inheritance tax attorney (1011) (a) Prepare petition †1 original 

 1 copy to be served on executor or administrator if petition is made by other than executor or administrator

(b) File petition and certificate of no inheritance tax, if no tax, or consent to ratable distribution of State Controller, etc. (c) Notice given by clerk for 10 days by posting (d) Give notice of hearing on petition, if special notice is requested (1200, 1202), and to executor or administrator if petition filed by other than executor or administrator (e) For hearing-prepare 

Decree of ratable distribution** †1 original 

 1 copy to be certified and recorded, where real property is distributed 1 copy to be certified for each security to be transferred and distributed (f) When decree has been signed, have copies certified and recorded, in counties where real property is located or furnish certified copies to security company for transfer of stocks, etc. (g) Prepare receipts to be signed by distributees (h) File receipts

CLOSING ESTATE 

Final Distribution and Accounts (920 to 932 and 1020 et seq) 

Before final distribution may be made an account must be filed, setting forth receipts and disbursements, claims filed or presented, giving name of claimant, nature of claim, when due, whether allowed or rejected. (921) Before distribution to an assignee or transferee court may inquire into consideration, fees, charges, etc. (1020.1) Note:

Before preparing final account be sure that all claims, personal property taxes, inheritance taxes, and income taxes, both for the estate and for deceased prior to his death, have been paid. (1024) **(In Los Angeles County decree is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

(a) Prepare final report and account and petition for distribution, setting forth all matters necessary to show the condition of the estate (921, 1020). Account must be verified and vouchers filed with account (b) File final account and report and petition for distribution and vouchers, and obtain date for hearing from clerk (c) Notice given by clerk by posting for 10 days (d) Prepare notice of hearing and serve on all those requesting special notice, at least 10 days before hearing (1200) (e) File notice of hearing, together with affidavit of mailing or proof of service (f) File inheritance tax receipt or certificate of no tax if certificate has not been filed (1024) (g) If value of estate exceeds $20,000 and there are one or more beneficiaries living outside the state file Certificate of Franchise Tax Commissioner re Income Tax; otherwise certificate is not required to close estate. 

ERAL RULES, p 310) 

(h) At hearing-have prepared 

(See GEN- Decree of settlement of final account and for distribution** †1 original 

 1 copy to be certified and recorded, if real property is distributed and 1 copy to be certified for each security transferred and distributed *(In Los Angeles County decree is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

(i) After decree has been signed and entered record certified copy in each county where real property is situated, and arrange for transfer of securities and personal property (j) Prepare receipts for distributees and receipts for administrator's and attorney's fees allowed by court (k) Prepare affidavit for discharge and file together with receipts, and have order of discharge signed by court (1) Court will enter discharge of executor or administrator (m) If surety bond has been provided for administrator or executor, notify bonding company of discharge

GENERAL RULES ON ACCOUNTS AND DISTRIBUTION 

(1) If an estate is not closed within 1 year an account should be filed 

Note: Most counties have a general requirement for annual accounts. (2) On all accounts and petitions for distribution notice must be mailed to persons who have filed a request for special notice at least 10 days before the date of hearing, and an affidavit of mailing prepared and filed before hearing, or proof of service made (3) See that all creditors' claims have been properly endorsed as to allowance or rejection by the administrator, and approved by judge and filed with clerk before hearing on account

(4) See that Federal and state income tax returns have been made before distribution, both for the deceased prior to his death and for the estate, during the period of administration, and that said taxes have been paid. 

This should be done to relieve executor or administrator from personal liability for failure to pay such taxes. If estate is in excess of $40,000 or estate is that of a nonresident of the United States a Federal Estate Tax Return should be filed and tax paid. The 

Preliminary Return for Federal Estate Tax should be made within 2 months after decedent's death or appointment of executor or administrator. The Federal Estate Tax Return must be filed within 15 months after the date of death of deceased, and tax is also due at that time. (Local Collector of Internal 

Revenue will provide forms for these returns) (5) If value of the estate exceeds $20,000 and there are one or more beneficiaries living outside the state a certificate of the State Franchise Tax Commissioner should be filed before distribution for state income tax; otherwise no certificate is required to close the estate; however, income tax returns should be filed and taxes paid, as noted above, to relieve executor or administrator of personal liability. (6) On hearing for distribution arrange to have executor present to testify, in counties where such testimony is required. (7) On distribution of the estate to the heirs or legatees secure receipts for property distributed.

(8) When estate has been fully administered and it is shown by the production of satisfactory vouchers that administrator or executor has paid all sums of money due from him, and delivered up under order of court all property of the estate to parties entitled, and performed all duties lawfully required of him (a) Present affidavit and order for final discharge, and receipts of the distributees to court for signature on order (b) When discharge of executor has been entered notify bonding company, if surety bond has been provided for executor or administrator 

SUMMARY ADMINISTRATION 

Estates Less Than $1000 (630, 631, 631.1)

If an estate consists entirely of personal property, not exceeding $1000 in value and with no lien thereon, probate proceedings are not necessary and the money may be withdrawn or property transferred on the presentation of affidavit of the heir entitled thereto, or person who has the right to succeed to such property (630) which affidavit shall set forth the right of affiant or affiants to receive such money or property, or to have such property transferred. 

Estates Under $5000 (630.5, 631, 631.1)

If value of estate does not exceed $5000, irrespective of character, the spouse of decedent, if entitled by succession or by last will of decedent to any money of decedent on deposit in bank, may collect such money, not to exceed $500, without procuring letters, upon furnishing bank with affidavit showing right of affiant to receive such money.

Setting Aside Estate Under $2500 (640, et seq.)

Applicant may file a petition for letters of administration, or to set aside estate to surviving spouse or minor children, if value of estate, over and above all liens or encumbrances of record, does not exceed $2500, and if expenses of last illness, funeral charges and expenses of administration have been paid. (640, 645)

Application to set aside estate under $2500 may be made as an alternative relief under petition for letters of administration or letters testamentary, or may be filed separately by surviving spouse or minor children, through a guardian, at any time prior to hearing on petition for letters. (a) Prepare verified petition, setting forth a specific description of the real property, a list of liens and encumbrances of record at date of death and estimated value of the property, whether community or separate property. 

Petition should show that the expenses of last illness, funeral charges etc. (645) have been paid and allege that surviving spouse or minor child seeking estate has no other estate of the value of $5000. If the court finds the total value of the estate over and above all liens and encumbrances of record at date of death does not exceed $2500, as alleged in the petition, estate will be set aside to surviving spouse or minor children †1 original 

1 copy for appraiser (b) File petition and have set for hearing-filing fee $7.00 (c) Clerk will set for hearing and post notices for 

10 days (643)

(d) Notice of hearing also should be mailed to all heirs, as in the case of an ordinary petition for letters of administration, at least 10 days before hearing and affidavit of mailing made. (e) Prepare request for appointment of appraiser, or order for appraiser (whichever is used in particular county) and when appraiser has been appointed deliver to him copy of petition. 

After his appraisement secure certificate of no-tax (f) File certificate of no inheritance tax (g) At hearing-have prepared

Decree setting aside estate to surviving spouse or minor children** †1 original  1 copy to be certified and recorded where real property is involved 1 copy to be certified for each security to be transferred

Note: (When petition to set aside is included in petition for letters, the notice should include a statement to this effect.)

If letters of administration have already been issued and the inventory and appraisement filed shows estate to be less than $2500, a verified petition may also be filed to set aside estate to surviving spouse or minor children. 

Follow same procedure as hereinabove outlined. Mail notice of hearing to persons requesting special notice and file affidavit of mailing or proof of service as required in (1200) (641). **(In Los Angeles County decree is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same)

STATUTORY COMMISSIONS OF ADMINISTRA- TOR AND FEES OF ATTORNEY 

The executor, when no compensation is provided by the will or he renounces all claim thereto, or the administrator, shall receive the following commissions upon the amount of estate accounted for by him: (901) 

7% on the first $1000 

4% on the next $9000 

3% on the next $10000 

2% on the next $30000 

1% on all over $50000

Attorneys for executors and administrators shall be allowed out of estate as fees the same amounts as set forth for executors and administrators. (910)

Extra compensation may be allowed, as the court shall direct, for extraordinary services, such as sales or mortgages of real or personal property, contested or litigated claims against the estate, the preparation of estate, inheritance, income, sales or other tax returns which require the appointment of a tax counsel, tax auditor, or other tax expert for the preparation thereof, the adjustment and payment of extensive or complicated estate or inheritance taxes, litigation in regard to property of the estate, the carrying on of decedent's business pursuant to order of court, and such other litigation or special services as may be necessary for executor or administrator to prosecute, defend, or perform. (902)

JOINT TENANCY TERMINATION Jurisdiction-Superior Court 

(1170, 1171, 1172, 1173, 1174, 1175)

Whenever title to or an interest in real property is affected by death of any person, any person claiming an interest in such real property, which interest is affected by the death of such person, may file in the Superior Court of the county in which decedent was a resident at time of his death, or if decedent was a nonresident in any county in which any part of such real property is situated, a verified petition setting forth those facts, and particularly describing the real property and his interest therein. (1170)

Note: The petition may be included in the petition for probate of will or for letters of administration, or may be filed at any time prior to filing of petition for final distribution. When it is included in petition for probate or for letters the notice of hearing shall include a statement that a petition to establish the fact of death is included in the petition. (1171, 1173) (a) Prepare petition to terminate joint tenancy †1 original 

1 copy for appraiser (b) File verified petition-filing fee $7.00 (c) Clerk will set for hearing and give notice by posting for 10 days before hearing (d) Prepare request for appointment of appraiser or order for appointment of appraiser (which ever is used in particular county)

(e) Secure form of affidavit re joint tenancy property from inheritance tax appraiser, fill out and have executed by petitioner; return to appraiser with copy of petition (and inventory and other forms, when required) and secure report of appraiser. If no tax is due file Certificate of No-Tax (f) At hearing-have prepared 

Decree establishing death of joint tenant †1 original **  1 copy to be certified and recorded 

Testimony must be produced at hearing establishing fact of death (g) Record certified copy of decree establishing death of joint tenant in each county where property is situated Exception

When deceased joint tenant is a resident of this state proof of death may also be established by the following procedure: (a) Secure certified copy of death certificate (b) Prepare affidavit of identity, setting forth the following facts:

Description of property 

Facts surrounding acquisition of property 

Date of death of joint tenant, and that deceased was same person mentioned in deed 

Name of surviving joint tenant

That the value of the property comes within the exemption allowed by the inheritance tax laws **(In Los Angeles County decree is prepared by Decree

Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

(c) Attach certified copy of death certificate to affidavit and record (d) Obtain waiver from inheritance tax department

Note: This procedure is not provided for by statute, but is recognized by the California 

Land Title Association and accepted by them as establishing the death of a joint tenant. (Caution)-Check with title company in particular county, as this procedure is not accepted in all counties. Forms

Minors

GUARDIAN AND WARD 

Procedure for Appointment of Guardian

Application is made to the Superior Court of the county in which the minor resides or has estate. Court may issue letters of guardianship over the person or estate, or both, of more than one minor upon the same application. (1440) (a) Prepare verified petition for letters +1 original  (b) Prepare order directing notice to be given (1441) (c) File petition with County Clerk, Fee $7.00 (d) Present order directing notice to judge or to 

Court Commissioner in counties that have Commissioners. (e) Notice of hearing must be given, pursuant to court's order, to person having custody of the minor, and to such relatives of the minor residing in the state as the court shall direct. (1441)

In all cases, notice must be given to parents, if living, or proof made that their addresses are unknown. (f) For hearing-have prepared (1) Order appointing guardian** +1 original 1 office copy (2) Letters of guardianship (1481) +1 original 1 office copy 1 copy to be certified for each bank account and securities **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same)

(3) Guardian's bond (1480) if application is for guardianship of more than one minor, the court may permit a joint or separate bond (1440) 

Note: Where minor has attained the age of 14 years he may nominate his own guardian. 

Nomination should be prepared and filed with the petition for letters of guardianship. (1440) Incompetent

Testamentary guardians are appointed in the same manner as above. They must qualify and have the same powers and must perform the same duties with regard to the person and estate of the ward as guardians appointed by the court, except so far as their powers and duties are legally modified, enlarged or changed by the will by which appointed, and no bond need be required unless ordered by court. (1484, 1485)

Application is made to the Superior Court (1460) Any relative or friend may file a verified petition alleging that a person is insane or incompetent (1461) (a) Prepare verified petition and file with clerk. 

Fee $7.00 (b) Clerk will set petition for hearing and issue a citation directed to incompetent. (1461) (c) Citation must be served on the alleged incompetent in the same manner as provided for the service of summons at least 5 days before the hearing. If able to attend incompetent must be produced at hearing. 

Note: If the incompetent is confined in a state hospital in this state, serve citation on incompetent and obtain affidavit of service from person making service. Request med-

ical superintendent or acting medical superintendent of such state hospital to furnish certificate that such patient is unable to attend hearing. (1461)

In all other cases where incompetent is unable to attend hearing because of physical inability, such inability must be evidenced by the affidavit and certificate of a duly licensed physician or surgeon, or other duly licensed medical practitioner. (1461) (d) File original citation with proof of service as soon as service is completed (e) For hearing-have prepared (1) Order adjudging person incompetent and appointing guardian** †1 original 

 (2) If incompetent is unable to attend hearing, have in court the affidavit of a duly licensed physician or surgeon, showing incompetent's inability to attend; or if confined to a state hospital present the certificate of the medical superintendent of such institution, as noted above (3) Letters of guardianship (1481) †1 original 

1 office copy 1 copy to be certified for each bank account and securities (4) Guardian's bond (1480)

Procedure After Appointment of Guardian Following the issuance of letters and the appointment of the guardian, the procedure in the guardianship of both minors and incompetents is as follows: **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same)

Allowance for Support of Ward

The applicant for letters may request the court for an allowance for the support, maintenance and education of the ward at the time of the hearing of his petition and the issuance of letters of guardianship. The matter may be taken up ex parte and an order should then be prepared and filed, directing the allowance so ordered. 

After his appointment the guardian may ask for such an allowance for the ward's support by presenting the petition and order ex parte. 

Property may be sold, encumbered or invested under certain conditions. (1530, 1531, 1557) 

Inventory and Appraisement

Guardian must file an inventory and appraisement with the clerk within 3 months after his appointment, or within such further time as the court for reasonable cause may allow. (1550)

Letters of Guardianship may be revoked and guardian held liable for failure to so file. (1551) (a) Prepare order appointing appraiser (in Los Angeles County file request for appointment of appraiser-Order is prepared by court) (b) When order is signed it should be filed with clerk (c) Prepare inventory and appraisement +1 original and 1 copy (for filing) 

 1 copy for appraiser (some appraisers require a copy for their files) and 

1 copy for for each person requiring special notice (if ward is confined in state hospital copy must be mailed to Director of 

Institutions or the Attorney General) (d) Take original inventory and appraisement to 

Clerk's office for Clerk's certificate as to the appointment of appraiser (e) Deliver original inventory and appraisement (and copy when requested) to appraiser

(f) When appraisal has been made and inventory returned conform all copies with the original and file foriginal and one copy 

Liquidating Assets and Raising Funds 

Conform as nearly as possible to the provisions of the Probate Code concerning administrators, unless otherwise provided in Probate Code 1530 to 1540, inclusive, in proceedings pertaining to: 

Sales of 

Personal property 

Stocks and bonds 

Real property (1530, 1531, 1532, 1534, 1534a, 1536) 

or Contract to Convey Real Property (1537) 

or Notes, Mortgages, Deeds of Trust (1538) or Leases, and agreements for the sale of, or for options to purchase, mining claims or real property worked as mines (1538) 

EXCEPT as provided in (1538.5)

Order authorizing execution of lease shall prescribe the minimum rental or royalty, the period of the lease, and may provide other terms and conditions

Period of lease must be not longer than 10 years, except that for the production of minerals, oil, gas or other hydrocarbon substances the lease may be for not exceeding 20 years

Community leases may be joined in by the guardian and may be made for 20 years and so long thereafter as oil, minerals, etc., are produced from the total area in paying quantities (1538.5)

When the ward is, or has been, confined to a state hospital for the insane in this state notice of hearing on return of sale must be given the Director of Institutions or Attorney General at least 5 days before the hearing. (1535) If petition is for sale of stocks, bonds or other securities

it is advisable to state the name of the exchange where the stock is listed, if listed, and if not state that fact and supply a financial statement with petition Investments

Applications may be made by guardian, or any person interested in estate for an order of court authorizing guardian to invest proceeds of sale or other ward's money in his possession in real property or any other manner to the best interest of the ward. (1557) (a) Prepare verified petition and file with clerk +1 original 

If petition is for investment in stocks, bonds, or other securities it is advisable to state the name of the exchange where the stock is listed, if listed, and if not state that fact and supply a financial statement with petition. (b) Notice may be dispensed with by the court or time of notice shortened, except as to special notices. (1557) (c) File petition and clerk will set for hearing and give notice by posting at least 5 days before hearing, in the manner prescribed in 1200 (d) At least 5 days before hearing copy of notice should be mailed to all persons requesting special notice, and affidavit of mailing or proof of service made. (e) If applicant is other than guardian notice of hearing must be mailed to guardian at least 5 days before hearing. (f) For hearing-have prepared (1) Order authorizing guardian to invest funds** †1 original 

1 office copy 1 copy to be certified for each security **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same)

Compromise of Claims 

Against Estate of Minor or Suit Against Guardian

The guardian, with the court's approval, may compromise any claim against estate or suit against guardian (1530a) (a) Prepare verified petition and file (b) Clerk will set for hearing-notice is given as required by 1200 (c) At hearing-have prepared:

+1 original 

1 copy to be certified and recorded, if real property is involved 

In Favor of Minor (1430, 1431)

If minor has no guardian of his estate, money belonging to the minor not exceeding $500 or other property belonging to minor not exceeding $500 in value may be paid or delivered to a parent of the minor entitled to the custody of the minor to hold for the minor upon written assurance verified by oath of such parent that total estate of minor does not exceed $1000 in value, and the written receipt of such parent shall be an acquittance of the person making such payment of money or delivery of other property. (1430) 

Disputed claim held by Minor—for damages, money or other property against third person: 

Father of minor, or if father is dead or parents are living separate or apart and his mother then has care **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same)

or custody then his mother, shall have right to compromise, or execute a covenant not to sue on, such claim, but before compromise or covenant is valid it must be approved by the court. (1431) (a) Prepare verified petition and file (no filing fee for petition) †1 original 

 (b) Petition may be heard ex parte, or clerk may set for hearing (c) For hearing-have prepared

Order authorizing compromise or claim or to execute covenant not to sue** †1 original 

1 office copy 1 copy to be certified if third person desires copy 

If the court approves compromise or covenant and the money or value of the property to be paid or delivered does not exceed $2000 it may direct that the money or property be paid or delivered to the father or mother, with or without bond, or may require that the money so paid be deposited in a bank or trust company, subject to withdrawal only upon order of court, or The court may require a general guardian be appointed and the money or other property be paid or delivered to such guardian, or prescribe such other conditions as the court deems for the best interest of the minor. **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same.)

If the amount involved exceeds $2000 a general guardian must be appointed or in lieu thereof the court must require that any moneys so paid be deposited in a bank or trust company subject to withdrawal only upon the order of court or, as to any property to be received by the minor, the court shall prescribe such conditions as the court may deem proper. (1431) Accounts 

At the expiration of 1 year from time of appointment, and as often thereafter as required by court, the guardian must present his account for settlement and allowance. (1553) (Most counties have a general requirement for annual accounts) (a) Prepare verified account and report, and file same with vouchers +1 original 

1 copy for each person requesting notice (b) Clerk will set for hearing and give notice (c) Notice of hearing and copy of account must be mailed or served upon: (1) Director of Institutions or Attorney

General, if ward is or has been confined in a state hospital at least 8 days prior to hearing (1554) (2) To all person requesting special notice, as provided in 1600, 1601, within 2 days after the filing of such account (d) Affidavit of service by mail should be prepared and filed, or other proof of service made

(e) For hearing-have prepared:

Order settling account and allowing guardian's and attorney's fees** (leave amount of fees blank to be inserted in accordance with order of court) †1 original 

Termination of Guardianship-Minors

If estate of ward has been entirely exhausted, the court, upon settlement of the account, shall order the guardianship of the estate terminated and guardian discharged. (1559) (a) Prepare and file petition for termination, or incorporate petition in the petition for settlement of final account †1 original 

1 office copy (b) Prepare order for presentation to court** †1 original 

1 office copy

If appointment of guardian is made solely because of ward's minority, the guardianship is terminated on his attaining majority. (1590)

The marriage of a minor ward terminates the guardianship of the person, but not of the estate. (1590) (a) Prepare and file verified final account and report †1 original 

1 office copy 1 copy for each person requesting notice **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same)

(b) Clerk will set for hearing and give notice (c) Notice should also be given in the same manner as hereinabove directed for accounts (d) For hearing-have prepared Order settling final account of guardian, allowing fees of guardian and attorney's fees and directing payment of residue of estate in guardian's possession to ward** †1 original 

 1 copy to be certified and recorded if real property is involved 

Receipt of ward to be signed by ward on delivery of residue.

Guardian appointed by court is not entitled to final discharge until 1 year after the ward's majority. (1593)

When 1 year has elapsed after ward reaches majority (a) Prepare affidavit for discharge of guardian and order (printed form available in some counties) †1 original 

1 office copy (b) Present affidavit and order to court for signature (it is important that the order of discharge exonerate the bond of the guardian) (c) Notify bonding company of discharge of guardian if surety bond has been filed **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same)

Termination of Guardianship-Incompetent Restoration to Capacity 

Application for restoration to capacity may be made by ward, guardian, relative or friend, by petition to the Superior Court of the county in which letters of guardianship were issued. (1470) (a) Prepare and file verified petition for restoration to capacity; petition must state that person is now sane or competent †1 original 

 (b) Clerk will set for hearing (1471) (c) Jury is waived unless demanded (1471) 

If jury is requested summon witnesses to testify as in civil actions (1471) (d) 5 days notice of trial must be given to guardian, incompetent's spouse, if any, and to parents, if in this state (1471) (e) If trial is by court, court may require witnesses to appear and testify as in civil cases, and may be examined by the court on its own motion (f) Prepare judgment and order restoring to capacity and discharging guardian**

Judgment and order must be based on jury's verdict or judge's findings of fact. (g) Prepare receipt for residue of estate in guardian's possession (h) Have judgment and order signed by court and receipt signed by person restored to capacity **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same)

(i) File signed receipt and decree of discharge 

Decree of discharge should provide that guardian's bond is exonerated. (j) Notify bonding company of discharge of guardian if surety bond was filed 

Termination by Death of Ward (a) Prepare verified final account and petition to deliver residue of estate to administrator, or as the court may direct. +1 original 

File original (b) Clerk will set for hearing (c) Give notice as set forth under Accounts on page 347 (d) For hearing-have prepared (1) Order settling account and directing delivery of residue of estate to administrator** †1 original 

1 office copy 

1 copy for administrator or person to whom estate is delivered (2) Receipt and release from administrator (e) Have order signed by court (f) File receipt of administrator (g) Prepare and file decree of discharge 

Decree of discharge should exonerate the guardian's bond. (h) Notify bonding company of discharge of guardian if surety bond was filed **(In Los Angeles County order is prepared by Decree Department unless court instructs attorney to prepare same)

UNIFORM VETERANS GUARDIANSHIP ACT 

(1650 et seq)

Where estate of a minor or incompetent is derived from moneys received from the United States Veterans Bureau, in addition to the procedure outlined herein, the Federal regulations require certain special proceedings which are set forth in Probate Code 1650 et seq., and should be referred to. 

Copies of all proceedings must be sent to the Veterans' Administration office in your district, together with notice of hearings on accounts and petitions not less than 15 days prior to the date fixed for the hearing. 

Communications should be addressed to the Legal Department, U. S. Veterans Administration and that office will furnish instructions and all information necessary for the guardian.

Veterans' Administration Districts: 

The following counties in California are under the jurisdiction of the U. S. Veterans Administration located at 

Los Angeles, California:

Imperial, Inyo, Kern, Los Angeles, Orange, Riverside, San Bernardino, San Diego, San Luis Obispo,

Santa Barbara, Ventura (the District also includes 

Lincoln and Clark counties of Nevada)

The following counties in California are under the jurisdiction of the U. S. Veterans Administration located at 

San Francisco, California:

Alameda, Amador, Butte, Calaveras, Colusa, Contra 

Costa, Del Norte, Eldorado, Fresno, Glenn, Humboldt, Kings, Lake, Madera, Marin, Mariposa, Mendocino, Merced, Monterey, Napa, Nevada, Placer, Plumas, Sacramento, San Benito, San Francisco, San 

Joaquin, San Mateo, Santa Clara, Shasta, Sierra, Siskiyou, Solano, Sonoma, Stanislaus, Sutter, Tehama, Trinity, Tulare, Tuolumne, Yolo, Yuba

The following counties in California are allocated to the 

Reno, Nevada District of the U. S. Veterans Adminis tration: 

Alpine, Lassen, Modoc, and Mono

Fees Initial fee for filing first document in guardianship proceedings is $7.00. (San Francisco requires 50¢ additional for posting)

For filing a subsequent or second petition for letters of guardianship in the same proceeding the filing fee is $4.00.

Clerk's fees for certified copies vary according to length of document. 

Secure schedule of fees from County Clerk or check Political Code 4300a.

Forms Printed forms are furnished by County Clerk and a list should be procured from him 

Forms are usually furnished for 

Letters of Guardianship Notices Citations 

Petition for Guardianship of Minor and many others, but they vary in different counties

Definitions of Principal Terms Used in Probate of 

Estates

Executor A person appointed by deceased to carry out the terms of his will (feminine is executrix) Administrator A person appointed by court to manage the estate when the deceased dies without leaving a will (feminine is administratrix) Administrator With Will Annexed A person appointed by court when deceased has left a will in which there is no executor appointed or the named executor fails to act (feminine is administratrix with will annexed)

Public Administrator A public official who has prior right to administer an estate, when no other qualified person seeks appointment as administrator Special Administrator is appointed under certain conditions when the circumstances of the estate require an immediate appointment of a personal representative (feminine is special administratrix) Letters Testamentary are issued to executors (executrices)

Letters of Administration are issued to administrators (administratrices)

Letters of Administration With Will Annexed are issued to administrators (administratrices) with will annexed 

Testate Having made a will before decease

Intestate Not having made a valid will Will An instrument in which a qualified person legally and intentionally directs the disposition of his property to become effective after his death.

Holographic Will A will entirely written, dated and signed by the testator in his own handwriting

Nuncupative Will A will declared orally and not in writing Codicil An addition to a will Legatee One to whom personal property is bequeathed in a will

Devisee One to whom real property is given by will 

Devise A gift of real property by will

Residuary Legatee One who receives the residue of an estate after the payment of the testator's debts, devises and legacies

XV 

CRIMINAL PROCEDURE Statute of Limitations 

Like civil actions, criminal actions must be commenced within prescribed periods of time after the offense is committed, otherwise the action is barred. The respective statute of limitations applicable to the various types of criminal actions are: 1 year 

A misdemeanor action (Pe C 801, 1426a) 

3 years

An action for any felony other than murder, embezzlement of public money, acceptance of bribe by public official or public employee or falsification of public records (Pe C 800) 

6 years

Acceptance of bribe by public official or public employee (Pe C 800) Unlimited Murder 

Embezzlement of public moneys

Falsification of public records (Pe C 799) No time during which the defendant is not an inhabitant of or usually resident within this state, is part of the limitation (Pe C 802)

FELONY 

All crimes punishable by death or imprisonment in the state prison are felonies. All others are misdemeanors. (Pe C 17) Procedure 

1. Verbal complaint that crime has been committed is made to the District Attorney, either by individual victim or police. (May be made directly to grand jury)

CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 

361 2. District Attorney either draws a complaint accusing the defendant of the crime-the complaint being sworn to before a municipal judge, justice of the peace or police judge, or obtains an indictment against defendant after presenting evidence to grand jury. (a) If complaint method used:

Defendant is arraigned before municipal judge, justice of peace or police judge (usually the one who issued the complaint)

The judge informs defendant of his legal rights, sets bail and fixes date for preliminary examination.

At preliminary examination (Pe C 858883) evidence is presented by District 

Attorney; the judge either dismisses the complaint, or, if reasonable cause is shown, orders defendant held for trial in superior court, and sets date for arraignment in superior court, or upon plea of guilty commits defendant and certifies case to superior court (Pe C 859a) 

District Attorney must file an information in superior court within 15 days after preliminary examination (Pe C 1382)

Defendant is arraigned in superior court and (unless he has already pleaded guilty) enters his plea. There are five kinds of pleas to an information or indictment: Guilty 

Not guilty

A former judgment of conviction or acquittal 

Once in jeopardy

Not guilty by reason of insanity (Pe 

C 1016) Caution:

The plea is made orally, by defendant, in open court but when a special plea is entered it must be in the form prescribed by Pe C 1017 (Peo. v. Rogers, 112 C A 

615, 620) If plea is other than "guilty", trial date is set. Trial must be within 60 days after filing the information, unless the defendant consents to a longer period (Pe C 1382) (b) If indictment returned by grand jury 

3. Judgment

Defendant is arraigned in superior court, indictment read in open court by District 

Attorney (ordinarily reading is waived by attorney for defendant), defendant informed of his constitutional rights, plea entered and trial date set.

If defendant pleads guilty, or is found guilty by court or jury, sentence must be imposed within not less than 2 nor more than 5 days after plea of guilty or conviction. The time can be extended not more than 10 days if defendant makes a motion for new trial or motion in arrest of judgment (Pe C 1191); it can also be extended until insanity proceedings under Pe C 1368 or probation proceedings pursuant to Pe C 

1203 et seq are determined.

CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 

363 

MISDEMEANOR 

Procedure 

1. Written complaint is prepared by City Attorney, (in some cities by City Prosecutor) District Attorney or judge, and sworn to before a judge by the complaining witness. (Pe C 1426) 

2. Defendant is arraigned before municipal judge, justice of peace or police judge, informed of constitutional rights, plea entered, bail set and date of trial fixed. The same pleas apply to a misdemeanor as to a felony. In justice's or police court trial must be within 30 days from time defendant is arrested and brought within jurisdiction of the court (Pe C 1382, sub. 3); in municipal court, within reasonable time (see Peo. v. Molinari, 23 C 

A (2d) 761 

3. Judgment

Within not less than 6 hours nor more than 5 days after defendant pleads guilty or is convicted, the court must impose judgment. The time may be extended not more than 10 days if motion for new trial or motion in arrest of judgment is filed and not more than 20 days where probation is considered, provided that upon request of defendant the time may be further extended, not to exceed 90 days, also until question of insanity can be heard and determined, if necessary. (Pe C 1449)

PROBATION 

After conviction by plea or verdict of guilty, the defendant may apply for probation. The court may summarily deny the application, or refer same to a probation officer to investigate and make report to the court on date set by the judge. After hearing probation officer's report court may suspend the imposing, or the execution of the sentence and may direct that such suspension may continue for such period of time not exceeding the maximum possible term of such sentence, and upon such terms and conditions as the court deems proper. (Penal 

C 1203 and 1203.1)

The court has authority to revoke, modify or change its order or conditions of probation at any time during the term thereof. Before a change is made terminating probation and releasing defendant, written notice must be given the probation officer of the intention to revoke or modify the order. (Ordinarily changes in terms of probation, if made, are made on the recommendation of the probation officer.) (Pe C 1203.3)

If defendant complies with terms of his probation for the required period, he will be permitted by the court to withdraw his plea of guilty, enter plea of not guilty, and the court will dismiss the charge. If defendant was convicted, the court will set aside the conviction and dismiss the charge. (Pe C 1203.4)

SUMMARY PROBATION (Pe C 1203b) 

Courts having jurisdiction to impose punishment in misdemeanor cases have power to grant probation summarily in such cases.

VOLUNTARY BANKRUPT 

(Individual) (Partnership) (Corporation)

All pleadings must be verified. (Sec. 18-c, Bankruptcy Act) 

Petition and Schedules of Bankrupt 

(Quadruplicate sets of printed forms of Petition and 

Schedules obtainable from any stationery store.) 

1. Petition must show 

Full name of petitioner 

Correct residence address 

Nature of employment 

Name of employer (Fill in form completely) 2. Triplicate verified lists of creditors and their addresses may be filed with Petition in lieu of 

Schedules. (Sec. 7-a, Subd. 8, Bankruptcy Act)

This procedure only where there is necessity to have adjudication of bankrupt made immediately to preserve assets of estate without waiting to prepare complete schedules. Schedules must be filed within 5 days after adjudication (further time may be granted by referee). 

Subd. 8, Bankruptcy Act) 

(General Order XII). 

3. If corporation (Sec. 7-a,

Attach to petition certified copy of Resolution of Board of Directors authorizing filing.

4. If partnership 

Recite names of all partners, firm name and style, place of business, show petitioners as individuals, each owes debts which he is unable to pay in full and that petitioners and partnership are willing to surrender, etc. (Filing under partnership name does not relieve individuals of personal debts.) May be joint petition. (Sec. 5-a, Bankruptcy Act) 

5. Schedule A (1, 2, 3, 4, 5) Statement of Debts 

(Insert in all pages complete information requested in paragraphs printed on lefthand or top margin (forms differ) of schedules, if known, and if unknown, so state, and if none, so state. If in doubt as to status of creditors, insert name and address.) 

6. Schedule B (1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6) Statement of All Property. (Insert in all schedules, location, description, incumbrances thereof, if any, and dates thereto, and statement of particulars relating thereto, and names and addresses.) 

7. Schedule A-3

List every financial obligation, original owner and assignees and their attorneys, show actions pending and judgments, titles of courts and action numbers. 

8. Schedule B-5

Claim all exemptions. The homestead of bankrupt and personal property as governed by 

CCP 690-690.24, if any. Claim homestead exemption as governed by CC 1240 (Insert complete description of real property) (Insert

numbers of insurance policies as well as names of companies when claiming insurance policies exempt) 

9. Insert all property interest of debtor, whether present, future or contingent or possible, as an alleged fraudulent conveyance. 

10. Complete the Summary of Debts and Assets. 

11. Petition and Schedules must all be in triplicate and executed as triplicate originals (Sec. 59-c, Bankruptcy Act) 12. Every page must be signed. Petition and Oaths to Schedules A and B must be signed by debtor and verified. Attorney may act as notary. Name and address of attorney should be on front cover of Petition and Schedules. 

13. Retain quadruplicate as office copy 

14. Triplicate originals with filing fee are filed with the clerk of U.S. District Court. (Sec. 59-c, Bankruptcy Act) 15. If bankrupt does not have sufficient funds to pay the filing fee at the time of filing, it is possible to file an affidavit under Sec. 51-(2) stating "the petitioner is without and cannot obtain the money with which to pay such fees." If through investigation it develops the attorney has received a fee for services or that the bankrupt is able to pay the filing fee, a very serious situation may develop. 

16. Adjudication is made by the court immediately upon filing of petition, and the matter referred to the proper referee.

First Meeting of Creditors 

1. Referee requires an indemnity fee before calling the first meeting, even though petition filed without payment of fees, pursuant to Sec. 51-(2). 

2. Pay indemnity fee promptly upon notice from referee requesting payment, otherwise important rights may be lost. 

3. Notice of first meeting of creditors is mailed by referee to bankrupt, attorney and creditors. 

4. Upon receiving notice from referee, notify bankrupt to be present at first meeting. 

5. First meeting may be continued from time to time.

Pendency of Bankruptcy Proceeding 1. Bankruptcy matters having assets must be kept open for the statutory time of 6 months to allow creditors time to file claims. (Sec. 57-n, Bankruptcy Act) 

2. Claims must be filed within 6 months from date of adjudication (to participate in dividends, claims should be on file before hearing of accounts of receiver or trustee, even though 6 months have not elapsed). 

Exemptions for Bankrupt 1. Trustee files report on exempted property within 

5 days after his appointment as provided by 

General Order XVII, further time may be granted by referee. 

2. Exceptions to Report of Trustee must be filed within 10 days after filing of trustee's report. 

3. Obtain a certified copy of Order Determining 

Exemptions from referee. (If homestead property claimed, record copy of Order in Recorder's office.)

Amending Schedules (General Order II) (Sec. 39-a 

(3) Bankruptcy Act) 

1. Amendments to Schedule A must be made within 

6 months from date of adjudication and before discharge of bankrupt. 

2. A verified Petition to Amend must be filed in the Referee's office, together with an Order to Show Cause. (State all facts to be added to original schedules) 3. Referee requires fee for mailing notices of hearing petition. 4. After hearing, if petition is granted, file with 

Referee, 2 copies of Order Amending Schedules and 3 verified copies of section of schedules to be amended.

Discharge of Bankrupt (Voluntary or Involuntary) 

1. Petition for discharge of a corporation must be filed within 6 months after its adjudication. (Sec. 14-a Bankruptcy Act) 

2. An adjudication operates as an application for discharge of any person except a corporation. 

3. At least 30 days' notice given to creditors by referee on hearing of Application for Discharge and of time to file objections. (Secs. 14-b and 

58-b, Bankruptcy Act) 

4. Unless objections are on file at the time of hearing, discharge is usually granted by the referee as a matter of course. 

5. (Either bankrupt or objecting creditor may pay costs of hearing objections; if not paid, the referee may dismiss objections and recommend granting of discharge.) 

6. Obtain certified copy of Discharge of Bankrupt for bankrupt.

Schedule of Costs 

Filing fee (for each individual or partnership) $30.00

Referee's indemnity for first meeting of creditors (fixed by Referee).. Varies

Referee's fee for mailing notices on amendment (fixed by Referee)... Varies 

Referee's fee for mailing Notices of Discharge (fixed by Referee). Varies

RECEIVER IN BANKRUPTCY 

Petition for Appointment of Receiver 1. If by petitioning creditors on involuntary petition before adjudication, surety bond in a sum to be fixed and approved by the court executed by creditors making application. (Sec. 69-a 

Bankruptcy Act) 

2. File petition in duplicate with clerk, if no adjudication, after adjudication with referee, original only.

Order Appointing Receiver 

1. Order appointing and fixing amount of bond of receiver to be filed with clerk. 

2. Bond. Have receiver take and file oath. 

3. Obtain sufficient certified copies of Order Appointing for receiver to exercise duties. 

Petition of Receiver for Appointment of Attorney 

1. Have receiver sign and verify. 

2. Submit to judge, if no adjudication, to referee after adjudication.

Orders for Receiver 1. If necessary, prepare petition and order for appointment of custodian. 

2. If advisable, prepare petition and order to continue business. 

3. If advisable to complete existing contracts, secure order authorizing. 

4. If necessary, prepare petition and order for appointment of accountant. 

5. Prepare petition and order for examination of bankrupt and witnesses and for appointment of 

Special Commissioner. (Sec. 21-a Bankruptcy 

Act) Serve certified copy of order upon Special 

Commissioner and secure a date for hearing.

If adjudication has been had, examination is before referee and referee signs order requiring witnesses to appear for examination. 

6. Secure from clerk (or referee) subpoenas and serve on bankrupt and witnesses to be examined. 

7. See that receiver files written inventory in duplicate (original for records, copy for appraisers). 

8. Get date for sale, prepare notice of sale and post notices. File notice of sale and affidavit of posting notices. Engage services of auctioneer, if necessary (personal property only). 

9. Prepare Oath and Report of Appraisers and file, after same is properly executed and verified. 

10. If suits must be prosecuted without delay, secure authorization from court.

Account of Receiver 1. Prepare Receiver's Report and Account 

2. Prepare attorney's petition for allowance, and affidavit under Sec. 62-d, Bankruptcy Act 

3. Meeting of creditors set by referee when account is filed 

4. Prepare Order Approving Report and Account of Receiver, (show expenditures) 

5. Obtain discharge of receiver

ATTORNEY FOR TRUSTEE 

1. Petition by Trustee for employment of attorney. 

Must be verified. 

2. Order for employment of attorney. 

3. Prepare and file First Account and Report of 

Trustee. 

4. Prepare and file Order Approving Report (after hearing of account, same for all subsequent accounts). 

5. Prepare and file Petition and Affidavit for Allowance of Attorney's fee (See Sec. 62-d, Bankruptcy Act.) 

6. Prepare and file Final Account and Report of 

Trustee. (Must contain complete summary of receipts and disbursements of preceding accounts and statement of amount and number of claims filed.) 

7. Prepare and file Trustee's Final Report. 

8. Prepare and file Order Discharging Trustee.

Note: Miscellaneous papers to be prepared by attorney for trustee in accordance with the needs of the bankrupt estate-such as Orders to Show Cause, Petition to sell property, order to sell, Petition for Confirmation and Order Confirming Sale, etc.

Petition

INVOLUNTARY PROCEEDINGS 

1. Prepare and execute petition in triplicate (3 for filing and ). (Sec. 59-c, Bankruptcy Act) 2. If total number of creditors is alleged to be less than 12, only 1 creditor need petition; if 12 or more, 3 creditors must petition. 

3. Alleged bankrupt must owe debts to amount of $1,000.00; creditors must have provable claims aggregating in excess of securities, not less than $500.00. 

4. File triplicate petitions accompanied by $30.00 filing fee, with clerk of District Court in district where bankrupt conducted his business. (Clerk returns one of the duplicate petitions and a subpoena to alleged bankrupt for service) 5. Present copy of petition and subpoena to marshal of same district. Furnish marshal with address of bankrupt and pay necessary mileage fees. 

6. Subpoena returnable within 10 days, unless judge shall for cause fix a longer time. 

7. If personal service or service as authorized cannot be made, prepare petition for service by publication and order for service by publication, and publish, usually once and return day 5 days after publication, or as judge may direct. (Sec. 

18-a Bankruptcy Act and Sec. 118, Title 28 U. S. 

Code)

Answer 1. Alleged bankrupt, or any creditor, may appear and plead to petition within 5 days after return day, or within such further time as the judge may allow. 

2. Answer is executed in duplicate and verified under oath, and filed with the clerk of the District Court. (No filing fee)

3. If petition is invalid, defense in point of law made by motion to dismiss or in the answer, not by demurrer. (See Civil Rule 7-c and General 

Order XXXVII) 

4. Demand for jury trial should accompany answer, if jury trial is desired and is allowable, but must be made within 5 days after return day of subpoena.

Dismissal by Consent or for Want of Prosecution (Sec. 59-(g)) 

1. Petition for dismissal by consent or for want of prosecution, filed with clerk, original under oath 

2. Bankrupt's list, under oath, of all his creditors, and addresses 3. Petition for order on bankrupt, and order to file list of creditors and addresses 

4. Order to show cause on creditors of application of dismissal of involuntary petition 

5. Publish order (if so ordered) once, not later than 10 days prior to hearing. (If all creditors notified-no publication necessary) 

6. Copy of order mailed at least 10 days prior to hearing to all creditors 

7. Affidavit of mailing 

8. Order dismissing involuntary petition Adjudication 

1. Order of adjudication by the court is entered by clerk or referee. If dismissal on merits, prepare order or obtain copy of minute order of clerk 

2. Matter is referred to referee 

3. Bankrupt to prepare, make oath to and file in court, within 5 days after adjudication, a schedule of his property and list of his creditors and their addresses, in triplicate. (Sec. 7-a (8) Bankruptcy Act) 

4. Referee's order directing bankrupt to prepare and file his schedules

5. Verified list of creditors or schedules must be filed before first meeting is called. If bankrupt absent or absconded, subpoenas may be issued to persons likely to know business affairs of bankrupt and with assistance of attorney for petitioning creditors, prepare schedules 6. Prepare and file petition for allowance to attorney for petitioning creditors
