RULES OF THE UNITED STATES CIRCUIT COURT OF APPEALS FOR THE NINTH CIRCUIT 

The Federal Rules of Civil Procedure, whenever applicable, are hereby adopted as a part of the rules of this Court with respect to appeals in actions of a civil nature. 

RULE 1 TITLE OF COURT 

The court adopts "United States Circuit Court of Appeals for the Ninth Circuit" as the title of the court. 

RULE 2 

SEAL The seal shall contain the words "United States" on the upper part of the outer edge; and the words "Circuit Court of Appeals" on the lower part of the outer edge, running from left to right; and the words "Ninth Circuit" in two lines, in the center, with a dash beneath.

RULE 3 (as amended).. 

TERMS AND SESSIONS OF COURT 

1. A term of this court shall be held annually in the city of 

San Francisco, commencing on the first Monday of October. 

2. A term of this court shall be held annually in the city of 

Seattle, in the State of Washington, commencing on the second

Monday of September with a session in September and in March or April. 

3. A term of this court shall be held annually in the city of 

Portland, in the State of Oregon, commencing on the third Monday of September, with a session in September and in March or April. 

4. A term of this court shall be held annually in the city of 

Los Angeles, in the State of California, commencing on the fourth 

Monday of September. 

5. No adjournment in any of the terms of the court shall be made to the succeeding term save when made on the last day prior to the beginning of such succeeding term. Appeals shall be deemed decided when announced from the bench in a session of the court at the place of the term in which they are submitted, or when the signed opinions or other documents evidencing their decision are filed with the clerk.

RULE 4 (as amended) ASSIGNING OF CAUSES FOR HEARING 

1. The calendars of the Court shall be made up by the Clerk under the direction of the senior Circuit Judge subject to the approval of the majority of the judges.

2. The senior Circuit Judge after conference with the Circuit

Judges shall designate and assign the judges who are to hear the causes placed upon the calendars of the Court; such designation or assignment may be modified or set aside by a majority of the judges. 

3. Any appeal from the District Courts for the Districts of 

Washington, Oregon, Montana and Idaho and the Territory of Alaska, upon the written request of any litigant therein, filed with the clerk on or before the filing of the first brief therein, that such appeal be heard elsewhere than in San Francisco shall be heard in either Seattle, Washington, or Portland, Oregon, as determined by the Court: provided that in emergency or other special circumstances such appeals, upon motion, may be heard at any session of the Court in the Circuit, to be designated by the Court. 

4. Petitions to enforce, or review, orders or decisions of Boards or Commissions shall be heard at cities of sessions of the Court as are appeals from the Districts in which the proceedings were initiated, or of which the individual or corporation is a resident within the circuit; provided, that, on motion, another place of hearing may be ordered by the Court. 

5. Any cause entitled by law to preference shall be assigned upon the first calendar made up after briefs in such case have been filed. 

6. No change of the day assigned for hearing will be made except by order of the Court for reasons shown. 

RULE 5 QUORUM 

1. If, at any term or session, a quorum does not attend on any day appointed for holding it, any judge who does attend may adjourn the court from time to time, or, in the absence of any judge, the clerk may adjourn the court from day to day. If, during a term, after a quorum has assembled, less than that number attend on any day, any judge attending may adjourn the court from day to day until there is a quorum, or may adjourn without day. 

2. Any judge attending when less than a quorum is present may make all necessary orders touching any suit, proceeding, or process depending in or returned to the court, preparatory to hearing, trial, or decision thereof.

RULE 6 

CLERK 

1. The clerk's office shall be kept at San Francisco, Calif. 

2. The clerk shall not practice, either as attorney or counselor, in this court or any other court while he shall continue to be clerk of this court. 

3. He shall, before he enters on the execution of his office, take an oath in the form prescribed by section 794 of the Revised

Statutes, and shall give bond in a sum to be fixed, and with sureties to be approved by the court, faithfully to discharge the duties of his office and seasonably to record the decrees, judgments, and determinations of the court. A copy of such bond shall be entered on the journal of the court; and the bond shall be deposited for safe-keeping as the court may direct. 

4. He shall not permit any original record or paper to be taken from the courtroom or from the office, without an order from the court, except as provided in Rule 19. 

RULE 7 MARSHAL, CRIER, AND OTHER OFFICERS 

The marshal and crier shall be in attendance during the sessions of the court, with such number of bailiffs and messengers as the court may from time to time order.

RULE 8 ATTORNEYS AND COUNSELORS. 

1. All attorneys admitted to practice in the Supreme Court of the United States, or in any District Court of the Ninth Circuit, shall be deemed attorneys of the Circuit Court of Appeals for the Ninth Circuit; but such attorneys, on or before their first appearance in open court, in said court, shall take an oath or affirmation, in the form prescribed by Rule 2 of the Supreme Court of the United States and subscribe the roll of attorneys. All other persons who have been admitted to practice in the highest court of any State or Territory, upon presenting satisfactory evidence of good moral character and professional standing, may be admitted to practice in said court upon taking the oath so prescribed and subscribing the roll of attorneys. 

2. Briefs not signed by counsel who are members of the bar of this court and fully qualified under the provisions of this rule will not be considered by the court. 

RULE 9 PRACTICE 

The practice shall be the same as in the Supreme Court of the United States, as far as the same shall be applicable. 

RULE 10 PROCESS 

All process of this court shall be in the name of the President of the United States, and shall be in like form and tested in the same manner as process of the Supreme Court of the United States.

RULE 11 APPEALS, RETURN AND RECORD 

1. In all cases brought to this court by appeal, to review any judgment or decree, the clerk of the Court by which such judgment or decree was rendered shall annex to and transmit with the record a certificate under seal stating in detail the cost of the record and by whom paid, and, in bankruptcy and admiralty cases, the original citation issued in the cause. 

2. In cases prosecuted in forma pauperis, the record to be furnished to the clerk of this court shall be in typewritten form, upon paper 8 inches by 10½ inches, and bound on the left margin. 

In addition to the certified original, three identical legible copies must be transmitted to this court. 

RULE 12 TRANSLATIONS 

Whenever any record transmitted to this court shall contain any document, paper, testimony, or other proceeding in a foreign language, and the record does not also contain a translation of such document, paper, testimony, or other proceeding, made under the authority of the inferior court, or admitted to be correct, the record shall not be printed; but the case shall be reported to this court by the clerk, and the court will thereupon remand it back to the inferior court, in order that a translation may be there supplied and inserted in the record.

RULE 13 EXTENSION OF TIME TO FILE TRANSCRIPT OF RECORD 

For good cause shown, any judge of this court may enlarge the time to file the transcript of record before the expiration of the time fixed by order under Rule 73 of the Federal Rules of Civil Procedure, or valid extensions thereof, the orders of enlargement to be filed with the clerk of the trial court, and all such orders transmitted as a part of the certified transcript to this court. 

RULE 14 DOCKET FEE 

The clerk shall, upon payment to him by the appellant* of a deposit of $35.00 in each case, file the record and enter upon a docket all cases brought to and pending in the court in their proper chronological order.

RULE 15 DEATH OF A PARTY 

1. Whenever, in an appeal pending in this court, either party shall die, the proper representatives in the personalty or realty of the deceased party, according to the nature of the case, may volun- *Whenever the word "appellant" is used in these rules it includes "Petitioner" and the word "appellee" includes "respondent."

tarily come in and be admitted parties to the suit, and thereupon the case shall be heard and determined as in other cases; and if such representatives shall voluntarily become parties, then the other party may suggest the death on the record, and thereupon, on motion, obtain an order that unless such representatives shall become parties within 60 days the party moving for such order if appellee, shall be entitled to have the appeal dismissed, and if the party so moving shall be appellant he shall be entitled to open the record and, on hearing, have the judgment or decree reversed, if it be erroneous; Provided, however, That a copy of every such order shall be personally served on said representatives at least 30 days before the expiration of such 60 days. 

2. When the death of a party is suggested, and the representatives of the deceased do not appear within 10 days after the expiration of such 60 days, and no measures are taken by the opposite party within that time to compel their appearance, the case shall abate. 

3. When either party to a suit in a district court of the United 

States shall desire to prosecute an appeal to this court, from any final judgment or decree rendered in the district court, and at the time of such appeal the other party to the suit shall be dead and have no proper representative within the jurisdiction of the court which rendered such final judgment or decree, so that the suit cannot be revived in that court, but shall have a proper representative in some State or Territory of the United States, or in the District of Columbia, the party desiring such appeal may procure the same, and may have proceedings on such judgment or decree superseded or stayed in the same manner as is now allowed by law in other cases, and shall thereupon proceed with such appeal as in other cases. And within 30 days after the filing of the record in this court the appellant shall make a suggestion to the court, supported by affidavit, that the said party was dead when the appeal was taken out, and had no proper representative within the jurisdiction of the court which rendered such judgment or decree, so that the suit could not be revived in that court, and that said party had a proper representative in some State or Territory of the United States, or in the District of Columbia, and stating therein the name and character of such representative and the State or Territory or District in which such representative resides; and upon such suggestion, he may on motion obtain an order that, unless such representative shall make himself a party within 90 days, the appellant shall be entitled to open the record, and, on hearing, have the judgment or decree reversed if the same be erroneous: Provided, however, That a proper citation reciting the substance of such order shall be served upon such representative, either personally or by being left at his residence, at least 30 days before the expiration of such 90 days: Provided, also, That in every such case, if the representative of the deceased party does not appear within 10 days after the expiration of such

90 days, and the measures above provided to compel the appearance of such representative have not been taken within the time as above required, by the opposite party, the case shall abate: And provided, also, That the said representative may at any time before or after said suggestion come in and be made a party to the suit, and thereupon the case shall proceed, and be heard and determined as in other cases.

RULE 16 DISMISSING CASES BY AGREEMENT 

Whenever the appellant and appellee shall by their attorneys of record sign and file with the clerk an agreement in writing directing the case to be dismissed and specifying the terms on which it is to be dismissed, as to costs, and shall pay to the clerk any fees that may be due to him, it shall be the duty of the clerk to enter the case dismissed and to give to either party requesting it a copy of the agreement filed; but no mandate or other process shall issue without an order of the court.

RULE 17 

MOTION 1. All motions to the court shall be reduced to writing, shall contain a brief statement of the facts and objects of the motion, shall be supported by points and authorities, and, where the facts are not otherwise proved in the cause, by affidavits, and shall be served upon opposing counsel at least 5 days before the day noticed for the hearing. 

2. One-half hour on each side shall be allowed to the argument of a motion, and no more, without special leave of the court, granted before the argument begins. 

3. No motion to dismiss, except on special assignment by the court, shall be heard, unless previous notice as above has been given to the adverse party, or the counsel or attorney of such party. 

4. The motion and supporting papers unless printed shall be filed in typewritten form upon letter-sized paper, 8 inches by 10% inches, bound on the left margin. If in typewritten form an original and three identical legible copies must be transmitted to this court. 

RULE 18 MODELS, DIAGRAMS AND EXHIBITS OF MATERIAL 

1. Models, diagrams, and exhibits of material forming part of the evidence taken in the court below, in any case pending in this court, on appeal, shall be placed in the custody of the marshal of this court at least 10 days before the case is heard or submitted. 

2. All models, diagrams, and exhibits of material placed in the custody of the marshal for the inspection of the court on the hearing of a case must be taken away by the parties within one month

RULES- -U. S. CIRCUIT COURT OF APPEALS 

387 after the case is decided. When this is not done it shall be the duty of the marshal to notify the counsel in the case, by mail or otherwise, of the requirements of this rule; and, if the articles are not removed within a reasonable time after the notice is given, he shall destroy them or make such other disposition of them as to him may seem best.

RULE 19 PRINTING RECORDS 

1. Records shall be printed under the supervision of the clerk of this court. The clerk is charged with the duty of having the printing done at reasonable rates, and he shall, upon the docketing of the cause, cause an estimate to be made of the expense of printing the record and his fee for preparing it for the printer and supervising the printing and shall notify the party docketing the case of the amount of the estimate, which amount must be paid within 10 days. If the estimated expense is not paid as provided in this rule it shall be the duty of the clerk to report that fact to the court, whereupon the cause will be dismissed unless good cause to the contrary is shown. In such instance, the clerk shall notify the counsel for the defaulting party of the date on which the matter will be presented to the court. In cases appealed from the District 

Courts of Alaska and Hawaii, and the Supreme Court of the Territory of Hawaii, and United States Court for China, the clerk of this court shall fix the time for payment of deposit for printing the transcript. 

2. Upon payment of the amount estimated by the clerk, 60 copies of the record shall be printed, under his supervision, for the use of the court and of counsel. 

3. In all cases, the clerk of this court shall prepare the record for the printer, index the same, supervise the printing, and distribute the printed copies to the judges and the reporter and one or more printed copies to the counsel for the respective parties. 

4. If the expense of printing and supervision shall be less than the amount estimated and paid, the clerk shall refund the difference to the party paying same. If the expense is greater than the estimate the amount of such excess shall be paid to the clerk before he shall file the printed record or deliver copies to the parties or their counsel. 

5. In case of reversal, affirmance, or dismissal, with costs, the amount paid for printing the record and the clerk's fee shall be taxed against the party against whom costs are given. 

6. The appellant shall, upon the filing of the record in this court, in all cases, including those on petition to review, to enforce or to set aside an order of a United States Board or Commission, file with the clerk a concise statement of the points on which he intends to rely on the appeal, and designate the parts of the record which he thinks necessary for the consideration thereof, and forthwith serve on the adverse party a copy of such statement

and designation. The adverse party, within 10 days thereafter, may designate in writing, file with the clerk, additional parts of the record which he thinks material; and if he shall not do so, he shall be held to have consented to a hearing on the parts designated. 

If parts of the record shall be so designated by one or both of the parties, or if such parts be distinctly designed by stipulation of counsel for the respective parties, the clerk shall print those parts only; and the court will consider nothing but those parts of the record and the points so stated. If at the hearing it shall appear that any material part of the record has not been printed, the appeal may be dismissed, or such other order made as the circumstances may appear to the court to require. If the appellee shall have caused unnecessary parts of the record to be printed, such order as to costs may be made as the court shall think proper. 

All statements and stipulations filed hereunder shall distinctly and accurately refer to the pages of the original certified record as well as the documents to be printed or omitted. 

7. At the time of filing the record and docketing the cause counsel for the appellant in patent cases may furnish the clerk with copies of Patent Office drawings and specifications to be used as inserts, and the same, if in proper form and of convenient use, shall be used in printing the record. 

8. In all cases prosecuted to this court in which records or other matter shall be printed under the supervision of the clerk of this court, his fee for preparing the same for the printer, supervising the printing, indexing and distributing the copies shall be 12 cents for each printed page of the record and index. 

RULE 20 

BRIEFS 

1. Counsel for the appellant shall file with the clerk of this court 20 copies of a printed brief, and serve upon counsel for the appellee three copies thereof, within 30 days after the clerk has mailed to him copies of the printed record. 

2. This brief shall contain, in order here stated— (a) A subject index of the matter in the brief, with page references, and a table of the cases, alphabetically arranged, with citation to the official reports and National Reporter system, textbooks, statutes, treaties, regulations and rules cited, with references to the pages where they are cited. (b) A statement of the pleadings and facts disclosing the basis upon which it is contended that the District Court had jurisdiction and that this court has jurisdiction upon appeal to review the judgment, decree or order in question. The statement shall refer distinctly (1) to the statutory provision believed to sustain the jurisdictions, (2) to any treaty of the United States or statute, the validity of which is involved (giving the volume and page where

the treaty or statute may be found in the official edition), setting it out verbatim or appropriately summarizing its pertinent provisions; (3) to the pleadings necessary to show the existence of the jurisdictions, referring to the pages of the record in which they appear. (c) A concise abstract or statement of the case, presenting succinctly the questions involved and the manner in which they are raised. (d) In all cases save those of admiralty, or criminal nature, a specification of errors relied upon which shall be numbered and shall set out separately and particularly each error intended to be urged. When the error alleged is to the admission or rejection of evidence the specification shall quote the grounds urged at the trial for the objection and the full substance of the evidence admitted or rejected, and refer to the page number in the printed or typewritten transcript where the same may be found. When the error alleged is to the charge of the court, the specification shall set out the part referred to totidem verbis, whether it be in instructions given or in instructions refused, together with the grounds of the objections urged at the trial. In equity and admiralty cases, and at law when findings are made, the specification shall state as particularly as may be wherein the findings of fact and conclusions of law are alleged to be erroneous. When the error alleged is to a ruling upon the report of a master, the specification shall state the exception to the report and the action of the court upon such exception. (e) In admiralty and criminal cases, a specification by number of such of the assigned errors as are to be relied upon, with reference to the pages of the record where the assignments appear. 

Therafter each such assignment of error shall be printed in full preceding the argument addressed to it. Where the specified error is more than two printed pages in length, it may be summarized before the argument addressed to it, in which event the specified assignment must be printed in full in an appendix. The argument need not be arranged in the order of the number of the specified assignments. (f) A concise argument of the case (preferably preceded by a summary), exhibiting a clear statement of the points of law or facts to be discussed, with a reference to the pages of record and the authorities relied upon in support of each point. When a statute, treaty, regulation or rule is cited, so much thereof as may be deemed necessary to the decision of the case shall be printed at length. If the matter so to be quoted, or matter quoted from opinions, is long it may be set out in an appendix. Except after leave granted, the clerk will not receive a brief of either party which, exclusive of the appendix, is more than eighty pages, or a reply brief of more than twenty. 

3. Counsel for an appellee shall file with the clerk 20 copies of

a printed brief, and serve upon counsel for the appellant three copies thereof, within 30 days after receipt of copies of the appellant's brief. His brief shall be of a like character with that required of the appellant, except that no specification of error shall be required, and no statement of the case, unless that presented by the appellant is controverted. 

4. Counsel for the appellant may serve and file within 10 days after receipt of copies of appellee's brief, 20 printed copies of a reply brief. 

5. In cases appealed from the District Courts of Alaska and 

Hawaii, Supreme Court of the Territory of Hawaii, and United States Court for China, the clerk of this court shall fix the time within which briefs as mentioned in this rule shall be served and filed. 

6. When the cause is prosecuted or defended in forma pauperis, the party so circumstanced shall file his brief, which may be in typewritten form, upon letter size paper, 8 inches by 10½ inches bound on the left margin. An original, and three legible copies must be filed, and, in accordance with the other provisions of this rule. 

7. When the brief for appellant is not filed as required by rule, the clerk shall give notice to counsel for both parties that the matter will be called to the attention of the court on a day certain, for such action as the court deems proper, and the case may be dismissed. When, according to this rule, an appellant is otherwise in default, the case may be dismissed on motion; and when an appellee is in default he will not be heard, except on consent of his adversary or by request of the court.

RULE 21 FORM OF PRINTED RECORDS, PETITIONS, BRIEFS, ETC. 

All records, briefs, petitions, motions and briefs printed for the use of the court shall be printed in accordance with the Rules of the Supreme Court of the United States and in such form and size that they can be conveniently bound together, so as to make an ordinary octavo volume, having pages 6-1/8 by 9-1/4 inches, and type matter 4-1/6 by 7-1/6 inches. They and all quotations contained therein, and the matter appearing on the covers, must be printed in clear type (never smaller than small pica or 11-point type) adequately leaded; and the paper must be opaque and unglazed. The clerk shall refuse to receive any record, petition, motion or brief which has been printed otherwise than in substantial conformity to this rule.

No volume of the printed record shall exceed 500 pages, and all volumes of printed records, briefs, petitions and motions shall be bound by the sewing process.

RULE 22 ORAL ARGUMENTS 

1. The appellant in this court shall be entitled to open and conclude the argument of the case. But when there are cross appeals they shall be argued together as one case, and the plaintiff in the court below shall be entitled to open and conclude the argument. 

2. When no counsel appears for the appellee, and no printed brief or argument is filed, the court may proceed to hear an argument on the part of the appellant and to give judgment according to the right of the case, but only one counsel will be heard for the appellant. But, if a printed brief or argument is filed for one party, the adverse party will be entitled to be heard by two counsel. Only two counsel will be heard for each party on the argument of a case. 

3. Upon appeals from orders granting or refusing a preliminary injunction or appointing a receiver, in habeas corpus and bankruptcy matters and all motions, one-half hour on each side; in all other cases three-quarters of an hour on each side will be allowed. 

No more time than above specified will be allowed without special leave of the court granted before the argument begins. The time thus allowed may be apportioned between the counsel on the same side at their discretion, provided always that a fair opening of the case shall be made by the party having the opening and closing argument. 

4. Any case entitled to be heard at any time or session of the court may be submitted by either or both of the parties on briefs. 

Consent to submit a case on briefs may be filed at any time prior to or at the time the case is reached for hearing. 

RULE 23 COPIES OF RECORDS AND BRIEFS 

The clerk shall carefully preserve in his office one copy of the printed record in every case submitted to the court for its consideration, and of all printed motions, briefs, and arguments filed therein. 

RULE 24 OPINIONS OF THE COURT 

The original opinions of the court shall be filed with the clerk of this court for preservation, and when so filed the same shall be deemed to have been recorded within the meaning of this rule. 

RULE 25 REHEARING 

A petition for rehearing may be presented within 30 days after judgment. It must be printed, and briefly and distinctly state its

grounds, and be supported by certificate of counsel that in his judgment it is well founded and that it is not interposed for delay. 

Twenty printed copies must be filed with the clerk of this court.* 

RULE 26 INTEREST 

1. In actions at law where an appeal is prosecuted in this court, and the judgment of the inferior court is affirmed, the interest shall be calculated and levied from the date of the judgment below until the same is paid, at the same rate that similar judgments bear interest in the courts of the State or Territory where such judgment was rendered. 

2. In actions at law where an appeal shall delay the proceedings on the judgment of the inferior court, and shall appear to have been sued out merely for delay, damages at a rate not exceeding 10 per cent, in addition to interest, shall be awarded upon the amount of the judgment. 

3. The same rule shall be applied to decrees for the payment of money in cases in equity, unless otherwise ordered by this court. 

4. In cases in admiralty, damages and interest may be allowed, if specially directed by the court. 

RULE 27 

COSTS 1. In all cases where any appeal shall be dismissed in this court costs shall be allowed to the appellee, unless otherwise ordered by the court. 

2. In all cases of affirmance of any judgment or decree in this court costs shall be allowed to the appellee, unless otherwise ordered by the court. 

3. In cases of reversal of any judgment or decree in this court costs shall be allowed to the appellant, including the cost of the transcript from the court below, unless otherwise ordered by the court. 

4. Neither of the foregoing sections shall apply to cases where the United States are a party, but in such cases no costs shall be allowed in this court for or against the United States. 

5. When costs are allowed in this court it shall be the duty of the clerk to insert the amount thereof in the body of the mandate, or other proper process sent to the court below, and annex to the same the bill of items taxed in detail. 

6. In all cases certified to the Supreme Court or removed thereto by certiorari or otherwise, the fees of the clerk of this court shall be paid before a transcript of the record shall be transmitted to the Supreme Court. *For time to file petition for rehearing re review or enforcement of orders of Boards or Commissions see page 27.

RULES- -U. S. CIRCUIT COURT OF APPEALS 

393 7. Upon the clerk's producing satisfactory evidence, by affidavit or the acknowledgment of the parties or their sureties, of having served a copy of any bill of fees due by them, respectively, in this court, on such parties or their sureties, an attachment shall issue against such parties or sureties respectively to compel payment of said fees.

RULE 28 MANDATE 

In all cases finally determined in this court other than original proceedings, a mandate or other proper process in the nature of a procedendo shall, upon the payment of any costs due in the case, be issued, as of course from this court to the court below, for the purpose of informing such court of the proceedings in this court, so that further proceedings may be had in such court as to law and justice may appertain. Such mandate, if not stayed by the order of the court, shall be issued on the expiration of 30 days from the date of such final determination, unless within said time a petition for rehearing be filed, in which case the mandate shall be stayed until 5 days after the determination of such petition. 

RULE 29 CUSTODY OF PRISONERS ON HABEAS CORPUS 

1. Pending an appeal from the final decision of any court or judge declining to grant the writ of habeas corpus, the custody of the prisoner shall not be disturbed. 

2. Pending an appeal from the final decision of any court or judge discharging the writ after it has been issued, the prisoner shall be remanded to the custody from which he was taken by the writ, or shall, for good cause shown, be detained in custody of the court or judge, or be enlarged upon recognizance, as hereinafter provided. 

3. Pending an appeal from the final decision of any court or judge discharging the prisoner, he shall be enlarged upon recognizance with surety, for appearance to answer the judgment of the appellate court, except where, for special reasons, sureties ought not to be required.

RULE 30 BOARD OF TAX APPEALS AND BOARD OF REVIEW 

1. A party applying for review of a decision of the United

States Board of Tax Appeals shall file his petition with the clerk of said Board, which petition shall set forth briefly the nature of the controversy, shall declare the court in which review is sought and state the necessary allegations to establish the venue, and shall be signed by the petitioner or his attorney of record, and the petitioner shall serve a copy thereof, with notice thereof, upon the opposite party or parties. The review shall be taken by such filing and notice.

444 2. The clerk of the Board, within forty days from the filing of the petition shall transmit a typewritten copy of the record on review to the clerk of this court, prepared in accordance with the provisions of Rule 75 or 76 of the Rules of Civil Procedure for the District Courts of the United States. 

3. The procedure on petition to review decisions of the Board of Review established in the Treasury Department by section 906, c. 690 of the Act of June 22, 1936 (7 U. S. C. A. Section 648), shall be the same as that prescribed by these rules for review of decisions of the Board of Tax Appeals; and the provisions of this rule shall apply to such petitions to review decisions of said Board of Review, except that where the words "Board of Tax Appeals" occur in said rule the words "Board of Review" shall be understood as applicable. 

4. All rules of this court except when inapplicable or as herein modified, shall apply to petitions for review of decisions of the United States Board of Tax Appeals and Board of Review to which this rule relates.

RULE 31 EXTENSIONS OF TIME 

The time specified in the foregoing rules and the rules in admiralty for any proceeding may be extended by order of a judge of this court.

RULES IN ADMIRALTY, UNITED STATES CIRCUIT COURT OF APPEALS FOR THE NINTH CIRCUIT 

The foregoing general rules of practice, as well as Federal Rules of Civil Procedure, except when inapplicable or as hereinafter modified, shall apply to appeals in admiralty from interlocutory and final decrees.

RULE 1 APPEALS 

Appeals in admiralty shall be taken by filing with the clerk of the trial court a petition for appeal, and obtaining an allowance thereof, within the statutory time. 

RULE 2 OBJECTIONS TO EVIDENCE IN APOSTLES 

In all cases of admiralty jurisdiction heard in this court, no objection shall be allowed to be taken to the admissibility of any deposition, deed, grant, exhibit, or translation found in the record as evidence, unless objection was taken thereto in the court below and entered of record; but the same shall otherwise be deemed to have been admitted by consent. 

RULE 3 ASSIGNMENT OF ERRORS 

The appellant shall file with the clerk of the court below with his petition for appeal an assignment of errors which shall be prepared as required by subdivisions (a), (b) and (d) of Criminal 

Appeals Rule 2 of the Rules of this Court.

RULE 4 REVIEW IN PART ONLY 

The appellant may, at his option, state in his petition for appeal that he desires only to review one or more questions involved in the cause, which questions must be clearly and succinctly stated; and he shall be concluded in this behalf by such petition, and the review upon such an appeal shall be limited to such question or questions.

RULE 5 APOSTLES ON APPEAL 

1. The apostles on an appeal to this court shall, in cases where a general petition for appeal is filed, consist of the following: (a) The style of the court. (b) The names of the parties, setting forth the original parties, and those who have become parties before the appeal, if any change has taken place.

(c) If bail was taken, or property was attached or arrested, the process of the arrest or attachment and the service thereof, all bail and stipulations, and, if any sale has been made, the orders, warrants, and reports relating thereto. (d) The libel, with exhibits annexed thereto. (e) The pleadings of the respondent or claimant, with the exhibits annexed thereto. (f) The testimony as taken on the part of the libellant and any exhibits annexed thereto. (g) The testimony as taken on the part of the respondent or claimant and any exhibits not annexed to his pleadings. (h) Any orders and opinions of the court. (i) Any report of a commissioner or assessor, if excepted to, with the orders of the court respecting the same, and the exceptions to the report. If the report was not excepted to only the fact that a reference was made, and so much of the report as shows what results were arrived at by the commissioner or assessor are to be stated. (j) The interlocutory decree and final decrees, if any. (k) The petition for and order allowing appeal, assignment of errors, citation, bond or bonds and praecipe for apostles. 

2. The following shall be omitted: (a) The continuances. (b) All motions, rules and orders which are merely preparatory for trial and to which no exception was taken or error assigned. (c) The commissions to take depositions, notices therefor, their captions, and certificates of their being sworn to, unless some exception to a deposition in the District Court was founded on some one or more of these, in which case so much of either of them as may be involved in the exception shall be set out. In all other cases it shall be sufficient to give the name of the witness and to copy the interrogatories and answers, and to state the name of the commissioner and the place where and the date when the deposition was sworn to; and in copying all depositions taken on interrogatories, the answer shall be inserted immediately following the question. 

3. The clerk of the District Court shall page the copy of the record thus made up, and shall make an index thereto, and he shall certify the entire document at the end thereof, under the seal of the court, to be a transcript of the record of the District Court in the cause named at the beginning of the copy made up pursuant to this rule. 

4. In making up the record to be transmitted to the Circuit 

Court of Appeals the clerk of the District Court shall omit there-

from any of the pleadings, testimony, or exhibits which the parties, by their proctors, shall, by written stipulation, agree may be omitted; and shall receive and include in the record any statement of the case which may be signed by the proctors showing how the questions arose and were decided in the District Court and setting forth so much only of the facts alleged and proved, or sought to be proved, or evidence thereof, as is essential to a decision of such question by the Appellate Court, and such stipulation and statement shall be filed and certified up with the record. 

RULE 6 NEW ALLEGATIONS, ETC. 

Upon sufficient cause shown, this court, or any judge thereof, may allow either appellant or appellee to make new allegations or pray different relief or interpose a new defense, or make new proofs. Application for such leave may be made at any time after the perfecting of the appeal to this court, and within 15 days after the filing in this court of the apostles and upon at least 5 days' notice to the adverse party or his attorney of record. 

RULE 7 NEW PLEADINGS-NEW TESTIMONY 

If leave be granted to make new allegations, pray different relief, or interpose a new defense, the moving party shall, within 10 days therafter, serve such new pleading, duly verified, on the adverse party, who shall, if such pleading be a libel, within 20 days answer on oath.

If leave be given to take new testimony, the same may be taken and filed within 30 days after the entry of the order granting such leave, and the adverse party may take and file counter-testimony within 20 days after such filing. 

RULE 8

NEW TESTIMONY-HOW TAKEN 

Such testimony shall be taken by deposition before the clerk of this court, or any United States commissioner, or any clerk of a District Court of the United States, or any notary public upon reasonable notice, in writing, given to the opposite party or his attorney of record, either in this court or in the court below, which notice must state the name or names of the witness or witnesses and the time and place of taking his or their deposition or depositions; or by commission issued out of this court with interrogatories annexed. Upon sufficient cause shown, the court may grant an open commission.

RULE 9 PRINTING NEW PLEADINGS AND TESTIMONY 

If new pleadings are filed or testimony taken in this court, the same shall also be printed and furnished by the clerk, as in the nineteenth general rule provided.

CRIMINAL APPEALS 

The Rules of Practice and Procedure, after plea of guilty, verdict or finding of guilt, in criminal cases brought in District Courts of the United States and in the Supreme Court of the District of Columbia, promulgated by the Supreme Court of the United States May 7, 1934, as amended May 31, 1938, are adopted as a part of the rules of this Court with respect to criminal appeals. 

In addition thereto, and to the foregoing general rules whenever applicable, the following rules shall govern in criminal appeals: 

1. BILL OF EXCEPTIONS. Whenever a bill of exceptions is required, the judges of the district courts in allowing bills of exceptions, shall give effect to the following rules: (a) No bill of exceptions shall be allowed on a general exception to the charge of the court to the jury. The party excepting shall be required before the jury retires to state distinctly the several matters of law in such charge to which he excepts. (b) Only so much of the evidence shall be embraced in a bill of exceptions as may be necessary to present clearly the questions of law involved in the rulings to which exceptions are reserved, and such evidence as is embraced therein shall be set forth in condensed and narrative form, or question and answer form, and be settled and approved by the trial judge. 

2. ASSIGNMENT OF ERRORS. 

(a) The appellant shall file with the clerk of the trial court, an assignment of errors, numbering each, and shall set out separately and particularly each error asserted and intended to be urged. (b) When the error alleged is to the admission or to the rejection of evidence, the assignment of errors shall quote the grounds urged at the trial for the objection and the exception taken and the full substance of the evidence admitted or rejected. (c) When the error alleged is to the charge of the court, the assignment of errors shall set out the part referred to totidem verbis, whether it be in instructions given or in instructions refused, together with the grounds urged at the trial for the objection and the exception taken. (d) Such assignment of errors shall form part of the transcript of the record and be printed with it. When this is not done, counsel will not be heard thereon except at the request of the court; and errors not assigned according to this rule will be disregarded, but the court, at its option, may notice a plain error not assigned.

RULES—U. S. CIRCUIT COURT OF APPEALS 399 BANKRUPTCY APPEALS 

The foregoing general rules of practice, as well as the Federal Rules of Civil Procedure except when inapplicable or as hereinafter modied shall apply to appeals in bankruptcy. Appeals shall be regulated, except as otherwise provided in the Bankruptcy Act, by the rules governing appeals in civil actions in the Courts of the United States, including the Rules of Civil Procedure for the 

District Courts of the United States.

APPEALS IN CASES ALLOWABLE BY CIRCUIT COURT OF APPEALS 

1. Petitions to this court for leave to appeal under section 

24 (a) of the Bankruptcy Act (II U. S. C. A. Sec. 47) from any order, decree, or judgment involving less than $500, shall be docketed upon the petitioner filing with the clerk of this court an original and three typewritten copies of a petition for appeal and brief in support thereof. The petitioner shall file with the petition for appeal two typewritten copies of the transcript of the entire record in the trial court. The petitioner shall forthwith serve upon counsel for respondent a copy of said papers and transcript. 

If the appeal is allowed the cause will be heard on said record which shall be printed unless otherwise ordered. 

2. The respondent within five days after service of said appeal papers may file an original and three typewritten copies of any objection to the allowance of said appeal.

PETITION FOR REVIEW OR ENFORCEMENT OF ORDERS OF BOARDS OR COMMISSIONS 

1. An original petition in this court for review or enforcement or to set aside an order of a United States Board or Commission (except in cases governed by Rule 30, Board of Tax Appeals on Review) shall be docketed in this court upon the filing of the petition, with three typewritten copies thereof. 

2. Upon the filing of a petition for enforcement, the clerk shall forthwith issue a rule to show cause why the petition should not be granted, and deliver sufficient copies thereof to the appropriate United States Marshal for service upon the parties against whom the order sought to be enforced is directed. 

3. Upon the filing of a petition to modify or set aside an order of a Board or Commission the petitioner shall forthwith serve a copy of such petition upon such Board or Commission, together with a notice that the petitioner has filed the petition in this court and shall make a sworn return of such service and file the return with the clerk. 

4. In a case involving the review or enforcement of an order of a Board or Commission upon entry of final order, judgment or decree in this court, and the printing of the opinion, if any, the clerk shall prepare and mail to the Board or Commission whose order has been the subject of review, and to the other party or parties to the proceeding, a copy of such order, judgment or decree and of the opinion, if any, of the court, duly certified. Such order, judgment or decree shall be stayed until twenty (20) days after the entry thereof. If within said time a petition for rehearing be filed, the order, judgment or decree shall be stayed until after decision on the petition. 

5. Excepting as above set forth the rules governing appeals shall apply, after the filing of the record.

Submit proposed name to Secretary of State for ap proval and Certificate of Reservation. Approved corporate name may be reserved for 30 days upon payment of $2.00 fee. (CC 291) 

2. Preorganization Subscriptions (and Agreements) 

Section 33 Corp. Sec. Act provides that preorganization subscriptions for shares may be taken without first obtaining permit from Corporation Commissioner upon the conditions that (a) Corporation be incorporated within 90 days and (b) With reasonable diligence apply for permit authorizing the issue of the shares so subscribed for, in accordance with such subscriptions and 1

(c) No collection be taken of any portion of the consideration until a permit shall have been issued.

Preorganization agreements may be entered into between parties pursuant to said section. 

If it is proposed to collect any portion of the consideration on account of preorganization subscriptions, a permit must first be obtained. 

3. Articles of Incorporation Contents (CC 290) 

(a) Name of corporation (b) Purposes (c) County in which principal office is to be located 

(d) Capital structure: Number of shares and classification (e) If more than one class, statement of preferences, privileges and restrictions (f) Number of directors (at least 3) and names and addresses of first directors (g) Optional provisions, such as granting power to levy assessments, etc. (h) If corporation is a nonstock or nonprofit corporation, include statement to that effect (i) Signature of first directors (j) Acknowledgment of execution by first directors (CC 292)

The application must be made by the corporation through its officers as authorized by the directors and not merely by the attorney through whom it may appear. DISSOLUTION 

Voluntary Dissolution

GENERAL OUTLINE 1. Election of Corporation to Dissolve; Preparation and Execution of Certificate So Stating (CC 400) 2. File Certificate of Election to Dissolve With Secretary of 

State (CC 400) 3. Cease All Business Except to Such Extent as May Be 

Necessary for the Beneficial Winding Up of Corporation 

(CC 400a) 

4. Mail Written Notice of Commencement of Proceeding for Winding Up to All Shareholders and All Known Creditors and Claimants (CC 400a) 

5. Prepare and Execute Affidavit of Mailing Above Notice 6. File Certified Copy of Certificate of Election to Dissolve 

With County Clerk (CC 400) 7. Superior Court May Dissolve, Appoint Trustees, etc. (CC 

400.5, 403, CCP 565) 

8. Election to Dissolve May Be Revoked (CC 400a) 

9. Distribute Corporate Assets (CC 401a) 

10. Obtain Certificate of Discharge From Franchise Tax Com missioner 

11. File Certificate or Order That Corporation Has Been Com pletely Wound Up (CC 403c) 

For Involuntary Dissolution see page 426

VOLUNTARY DISSOLUTION 

1. Election of Corporation to Dissolve; Preparation and Execution of Certificate So Stating (CC 400) 

(Form may be obtained from Secretary of State)

The voluntary dissolution of a corporation may be accomplished by: (1) Vote or written consent of holders of shares representing 50% or more of the voting power or 50% or more of the members entitled to vote

CORPORATIONS 

421 (2) Resolution of board of directors of corporation which has not begun the business for which it was created and which has issued no shares, or of any corporation which has been adjudged bankrupt, or of corporation which has disposed of all of its assets and has not conducted any business for a period of 5 years immediately preceding the adoption of the resolution electing to dissolve the corporation (3) A corporation may be dissolved if the term for which it was organized expires without renewal or extension thereof. 

2. File Certificate of Election to Dissolve With Secretary of State (CC 400). 

When the winding up and dissolution of corporation is authorized by either method (1) or (2) above, a certificate stating that such election was made, signed and acknowledged by the president or a vicepresident and the secretary or an assistant secretary, or by a majority of the directors, or by at least three shareholders or members authorized by the shareholders or members to sign such certificate, shall be filed in the office of the Secretary of State. (Mail original and one copy to the Secretary, the copy to be certified by him and returned for filing in office of county clerk of county in which corporation has its principal office.) 

Fee to Secretary of State for filing..... $5.00 

Fee to Secretary of State for certifying copy.. 2.50 

Fee to County Clerk for filing.. 1.00

3. Cease Business (CC 400a) 

When the proceeding for voluntary winding up and dissolution of a corporation has been commenced the corporation shall cease to carry on business except to such extent as may be necessary for the beneficial winding up thereof. The proceeding shall be deemed to commence upon the adoption of resolution of shareholders, members or directors of the corporation electing to wind up, or upon the filing with the corporation of written consent of shareholders or members. If such proceedings are instituted because of expiration of the term of corporate existence or other dissolution of the corporation, then it shall be deemed to commence at the termination of its corporate existence. 

4. Mail Written Notice to Shareholders, etc. (CC 

400a)

Upon commencement of proceeding for dissolution and cessation of business as above outlined, the directors shall forthwith cause written notice of commencement of proceeding for such winding up to be given by mail to all shareholders and to all known creditors and claimants whose addresses appear on records of the corporation. 

5. Prepare and Execute Affidavit of Mailing Notice to Shareholders, etc.

When written notice of commencement of proceedings for winding up has been mailed at the instance of directors, as recited in preceding paragraph 4, an affidavit of such mailing should be executed by person so mailing such notice (usually secretary of corporation). This is not a statutory requirement, but should be done so that the fact of such mailing may be a matter of corporate record.

CORPORATIONS 

423 

6. File Certified Copy of Certificate of Election to 

Dissolve (CC 400)

When certified copy of certificate of election to dissolve has been returned from the Secretary of State it should be filed in office of county clerk of county in which principal office of corporation is located. 

Fee to County Clerk for filing... $1.00 

7. Superior Court May Dissolve, Appoint Trustees, etc. (CC 400.5; CCP 565; CC 403)

Superior Court may order dissolution upon petition of one or more stockholders when dissention exists among directors. (See CC 400.5)

Upon dissolution of any corporation, the superior court of county in which corporation carries on its business or has its principal place of business, on application of any creditors of the corporation, or of any stockholder or member thereof, may appoint one or more persons to be receivers or trustees of corporation. (CCP 565) 

If corporation is in process of voluntary winding up, the superior court of the county where principal office of corporation is located, upon the petition of corporation or holders of 5% or more of the number of its outstanding shares or of three or more creditors, upon such notice to the corporation and to other persons interested in the corporation as shareholders or creditors as the court may order, shall have power to order and adjudge as to any or all matters in and for the winding up of affairs of the corporation. (CC 403)

8. Election to Dissolve May Be Revoked (CC 400a)

When the winding up and dissolution of a corporation has been authorized by vote or consent of shareholders or members or directors, such action may be revoked by a similar* vote or consent at any time prior to distribution of the assets, and a certificate of such revocation shall be executed and filed in same manner as certificate of such election. *It has not been decided in California whether "similar" refers to 50%, majority or identical number as voted for original dissolution. 

9. Distribute Corporate Assets (CC 401a)

After determining that all the known debts and liabilities of a corporation in the process of winding up have been paid or adequately provided for, the directors shall distribute all the remaining corporate assets among the shareholders and owners of shares according to their respective rights and preferences, in accordance with CC 401a.

Though not a statutory requirement, it is customary for board of directors to adopt a resolution either (1) stating that it has been determined that all the known debts and liabilities of the corporation have been paid, or (2) that the contract with financially responsible persons guaranteeing payment of remaining debts of corporation is adequate provision therefor. 

For distribution of assets of charitable corporation to members after payment of creditors see CC 605e. 

10. Obtain Discharge From Franchise Tax Commis sioner 

Obtain from Franchise Tax Commissioner a discharge showing that any tax or penalty due under

CORPORATIONS 425 the Bank and Corporation Franchise Tax Act has been paid, and send to the Secretary of State with certificate or order of dissolution, as the Secretary of State will not file certificate that corporation has been completely wound up until he has this proof that these taxes have been paid. It will be returned when order or certificate of dissolution has been filed. (No charge for this discharge.) 

11. File Certificate or Order That Corporation Has 

Been Completely Dissolved (CC 403c)

When a corporation has been completely wound up without court proceedings therefor a majority of the directors or trustees shall sign and acknowledge a certificate stating such facts and also that any tax or penalty due under the Bank and Corporation Franchise Tax Act has been paid; or 

In lieu of filing the above certificate the directors or trustees, if the winding up has been accomplished without court proceedings, may file a petition in name of corporation with the superior court of county in which principal office is located for an order declaring the corporation duly wound up and dissolved. Upon the filing of such a petition the court shall make and publish an order to show cause why such order of dissolution should not be made; and upon the hearing of said petition the court may make an order declaring the corporation duly wound up.

If certificate is issued, send original and one copy to the Secretary of State, original to be filed with the Secretary of State and copy to be certified by him and returned, which copy is to be filed in office of county clerk of county in which principal office of corporation was located.

If order of dissolution is made, a copy thereof,

certified by the clerk of the court, shall be filed in office of the Secretary of State. (Whichever document is sent to Secretary of State should be accompanied by discharge from Franchise Tax Commissioner, as related in paragraph 

10 above.)

Thereupon corporate existence shall cease except for the purpose of further winding up, if needed. 

Fee for filing certificate or order with Secretary of State.. ..$5.00 

Fee for filing with County Clerk... 1.00 

Fee for certification of certificate by Secretary of State......... 2.50 

Involuntary Dissolution 

GENERAL OUTLINE 

1. Complaint for Involuntary Dissolution (CC 404) 

2. Procedure and Jurisdiction (CC 404a) 

3. Action by State to Dissolve and Forfeit Corporate Existence (CC 404b) 

4. Cessation of Business (CC 400a) 

5. Mail Written Notice of Commencement of Proceeding for Winding Up to All Shareholders and All Known Creditors and Claimants (CC 400a) 

6. Prepare and Execute Affidavit of Mailing Above Notice 7. Distribute Corporate Assets (CC 401a) 

8. File Copy of Decree (CC 404c)

INVOLUNTARY DISSOLUTION 

1. Complaint for Involuntary Dissolution (CC 404) 

A complaint for involuntary winding up and dissolution of a corporation, other than one subject to the Bank Act, the Public Utility Act, or the Building and Loan Act, may be filed in the superior court of county in which principal office is located either by a majority of the directors or by a shareholder or shareholders who have been record holders for a period of not less than three months of not less than twenty-five per cent of the number of outstanding shares, or by any shareholder if the period for

CORPORATIONS 427 which a corporation is formed has terminated without extension of such period.

The court upon the filing of the verified complaint may entertain proceedings for involuntary winding up when it is made to appear: (1) that corporation has abandoned its business for more than one year (2) that corporation has an even number of directors who are equally divided and cannot agree as to the management of its affairs * or that the holders of the voting shares are so divided into factions that they cannot agree upon or elect a board of directors consisting of an uneven number or (3) that the period for which the corporation was formed has terminated without extension of such period.

Relief by way of involuntary winding up shall not be granted by the court, except under section (3) above, unless no other method of relief is adequate under the circumstances. 

2. Procedure and Jurisdiction (CC 404a)

Upon the filing of a verified complaint for involuntary winding up or dissolution a summons shall be issued and served on the corporation; and at any time prior to the trial of the action any shareholder or creditor may intervene therein. The superior court of county of principal office of the corporation shall have jurisdiction of said action and may make such orders for winding up and dissolution of corporation as justice and equity require. 

Unless other persons be appointed by the court on good cause shown, the board of directors shall conduct the winding up of the affairs of the corporation, subject to supervision of the court.

3.

Action by State to Dissolve and Forfeit Corporate Existence (CC 404b) The Attorney General may bring an action against any corporation or purported corporation, in the name of the people of the state, upon his own information or upon the complaint of a private party, to procure a judgment dissolving the corporation, if (a) a corporation has seriously offended against any provision of the statutes regulating corporations or has fraudulently abused or usurped corporate privileges or powers; or (b) if the corporation has violated any provision of law by any act or default which under the law is a ground for forfeiture of corporate existence.

But, if the cause of action be a matter or act which the corporation has done or omitted to do that can be corrected by amendment of its articles or by other corporate action, such suit shall not be maintained unless and until the Attorney General, at least thirty days prior to institution of such suit, gives the corporation written notice of the matter or act done or omitted to be done and the corporation fails, neglects or refuses to institute proceedings to correct the same within said thirty days period or thereafter fails to prosecute such proceedings.

In any such action the court may order dissolution or such other or partial relief as it deems just and expedient; and the court may also appoint a receiver or receivers for winding up its affairs or may order that such corporation be wound up by its board of directors.

CORPORATIONS 

429 

4. Cessation of Business (CC 400a)

When the proceeding for involuntary winding up of a corporation has been commenced the corporation shall cease to carry on business (unless, of course, the proceeding was begun because the corporation had abandoned its business for more than a year) except to such extent as may be necessary for the beneficial winding up thereof. The proceeding shall be deemed to commence when the order for winding up is entered. 

5. Mail Written Notice of Commencement of Pro ceedings for Winding Up to Shareholders, etc. (CC 400a) Unless the order for winding up has been stayed by an appeal therefrom or otherwise, or unless the proceeding or the execution of the order has been enjoined, written notice shall be mailed to shareholders, creditors and claimants the same as in the case of voluntary dissolution. 

6. Preparation and Execution of Affidavit of Mailing 

Notice as Above 

Prepare and execute affidavit of mailing notices same as in voluntary dissolution (see 5 of voluntary dissolution). 

7. Distribute Corporate Assets (CC 401a). 

After determining that all the known debts and liabilities of a corporation in the process of winding up have been paid or adequately provided for, the directors shall distribute all the remaining corporate assets among the shareholders and owners of shares according to their respective rights and preferences, in accordance with CC 401a.

For distribution of assets of charitable corporation to members after payment of creditors see CC 605e.

8. Filing Copy of Decree (CC 404c)

When a corporation is dissolved or its existence forfeited by decree or judgment of court, a copy of the decree or judgment, certified by the clerk of the court, shall be forthwith filed in the office of the Secretary of State and in the office of the county clerk of the county in which the principal office of the corporation was located, unless the original is already on file therein. 

Fee to Secretary of State for filing.... 

Fee to County Clerk for filing.... $5.00 1.00

BILL OF PARTICULARS 

(CCP 454)

A detailed informal statement or account of the items of plaintiff's claim, or defendant's cross complaint, and manner in which they arose. 

It is not necessary for a party to set forth in a pleading the items of an account therein alleged, but he must deliver to the adverse party, WITHIN 10 DAYS after a demand therefor in writing, a copy of the account, or be precluded from giving evidence thereof. (CCP 454). (Order for preclusion must be obtained on motion prior to trial. 162 Cal 722, 725)

If pleading is verified bill of particulars must be verified.

Procedure Prepare †1 original to file 

1 copy to serve on opposing counsel 

(Note: 

Either original or copy may be served on opposing counsel and other filed with proof of service)

Formal Requirements 

BRIEFS Rule VIII of the Judicial Council Rules for Supreme Court and District Courts of Appeal prescribes formal requirements for the index and arrangement of briefs in those courts; certain local Superior Court rules such as Rule 7, Los Angeles County, prescribe formal rules for documents for filing, and although briefs in the Superior Court may not necessarily be filed, the documents should preferably conform to the rule. For regulations as to Points and Authorities on appeals to the

Appellate Department of the Superior Court see page 180. 

Practical Pointers

The following suggestions, while in some respects dealing with typewritten briefs particularly, are quite generally applicable and are made for the sake of securing uniformity and for the benefit of the court and of opposing counsel. Compliance with these suggestions will be a great aid to judges and lawyers, who are compelled to read many hours each day. 

1. Use good typewriter ribbon and carbon paper so that both original and carbon copies will be clear. 

2. Spacing. The entire brief should be double spaced, including quotations, for the reason that single space typing is difficult to read. 

3. Quotations. Indent about 8 spaces from each margin in order that the same may be readily distinguished from the argument. 

4. Citations. (a) Always underscore title of cases cited (b) If several cases are cited on one point they should be typed on separate lines (c) Always cite the official report, state or federal (d) If cases appear in more than one set of re-

ports such as Reporter System or any annotated series, give all citations if possible. (Some attorneys and courts have only the Reporter System or an annotated series to use for references outside California.) 

Citation of Cyclopedias, etc. If California 

Jurisprudence, Corpus Juris, or similar works are cited, do not rest on these citations alone, but cite the latest case or very few cases preferably of local jurisdiction, that actually support the proposition of law stated in the brief. This suggestion is made because some judges and lawyers have found that the text of reference books is not always supported by the cases cited in the notes. When such is the fact much unnecessary trouble is caused the reader of the brief in the examination of irrelevant authorities, and the force of the point made in the brief may be entirely lost. 

Also, citing too many cases on the same point wastes a judge's time. Merely copying all the citations in a note or annotation, without reading and selecting pertinent ones, is reprehensible. (e) If a citation is repeated on different pages, give book and page of official report each time it is repeated in addition to the word "supra". Do not refer to a citation merely as "supra" if it is on a different page-turning back and searching for it wastes time and eyesight. ALWAYS, IF POSSIBLE,

GIVE THE EXACT PAGE OR PAGES OF THE REPORT ON WHICH THE PARTICULAR PROPOSITION OF LAW IN POINT IS DISCUSSED. 

(f) Stating the year of decision (in parentheses after title of case) is a good practice and often of convenience to the court.

EXAMPLE OF BRIEF CAPTION 

(Giving title of court and cause and number of case.

) 

STATEMENT OF FACTS 

(Here give a brief statement of salient facts upon which the argument is to be made.)

QUESTIONS INVOLVED I. WAS ALL OF THE PROPERTY DE- SCRIBED IN PLAINTIFF'S COM- PLAINT OFFERED 

TION?

FOR DEDICA- II. WAS THE OFFER OF DEDICATION ACCEPTED BY THE PUBLIC? 

ARGUMENT 

ALL OF THE PROPERTY DESCRIBED IN PLAIN- TIFF'S COMPLAINT WAS OFFERED FOR DEDI- CATION. 

1. The Recording of the Map with the Dedicatory 

Clause thereon and the Subsequent Sale of Lots with Reference Thereto, Constituted an Offer to Dedicate.

When the owner of property subdivides it and files for record a subdivision map showing highways reserved for the use of the public, he thereby offers to dedicate such highway to public use.

San Francisco Sulphur Co. v. Contra Costa County, 

(1929) 207 Cal 1, 6, (276 P 570). 

City of Los Angeles v. McCollum, (1909) 156 

Cal 148, 151, (103 P 329). 

Archer v. Salinas City. (1892) 93 Cal. 43, 49, (28 P 839).

2. If an Examination of the Subdivision Map 

Leaves any Doubt as to the Extent of the "Strand" Dedicated, Such Doubt Must be Resolved Most Strongly in Favor of the Public.

In the case of Larkey v. City of Los Angeles, (1925) 

70 C A 635, at 641 (233 P 991), the court said: "The original owners, having filed the map, are bound thereby, and all doubt as to the meaning of the map, and any conflict appearing upon its face, are to be construed to be construed most strongly against them." II

THE OFFER OF DEDICATION WAS ACCEPTED BY THE PUBLIC. 

(Proceed as indicated under point I)

CERTIFICATE OF FICTITIOUS NAME 

(CC 2466-2472) Explanation

Every person or partnership transacting business under a fictitious name or a designation not disclosing the names of the persons interested must file with the county clerk and publish for 4 consecutive weeks a certificate of fictitious name. Actions cannot be maintained by the persons, the partners or an assignee on any account or transaction had in the business name until this procedure has been taken. Certificate is supposed to be filed and publication made within one month after commencement of business.

Procedure 

1. Prepare Certificate of Fictitious Name 

†1 original 

Contents 

(a) Business name (b) Location of business (c) Names and addresses of all members of the business (d) Whether a partnership (e) Signatures of all members (all acknowledged) 

2. Have all members sign and 

3. All signatures acknowledged 

4. File with county clerk (Fee $1.00) 

5. Publish once a week for 4 consecutive weeks in a newspaper in county 6. Newspaper makes and files its affidavit of publication within 30 days after completion of publication (note this on calendar and before expiration of period see that it has been done; newspaper should furnish copy of affidavit for your file) 

7. Upon any change in ownership, a new certificate must be filed and published. Forms 

Note: See C C 2478 for Limited Partnership. 

Furnished by legal newspapers.

COMPUTATION OF TIME 

(CCP 12, 12a, CC 10, Pol C 12)

The time in which an act provided by law is to be done, is computed by excluding the first day and including the last, unless the last day is a holiday, and then it is excluded. (CCP 12)

Time is extended (a) If last day of period within which act is to be performed falls on a holiday, to and including the next day which is not a holiday. (CCP 12) (b) By such number of days as equals the number of holidays (other than special holidays) appointed by President or by Governor and which occur within the period specified to perform an act., (ex. Thanksgiving) (CCP 12 a) 

Note: Remember this when computing expiration date of statute of limitation.

In California a contract may be executed, a check drawn, a deed acknowledged, or any other act of a secular nature performed on Sunday, or a holiday, with the same force and effect as any other day. 

Holidays (Pol C 10) Sunday

January 1 

February 12 22 May 30 

July 4 

1st Monday in September 

September 9

October 12 

November 11

December 25 

Every state election day 

Every day appointed by President or Governor for a public holiday. (Holidays falling on Sunday are observed the following Monday)

Special Holiday 

A holiday applying only to special class of business or persons, and not appointed to be generally observed by all. (Pol C 10) Year 

365 days (Pol C 3257) (Added day of leap year and preceding day count as one day; this does not apply when act must be done in specified number of days)

Month calendar month unless otherwise expressed (Pol C 17) Week Day 

7 consecutive days period between any midnight and midnight following (law disregards fractions of days) 

Service by Mail

See SERVICE BY MAIL, p 446 for com puting time in which act is to be performed after service by mail. COPIES (Number to Prepare)

In general, prepare 1 copy for each interested individual, or his attorney, plus 1 copy for the office file, and the original to be filed in court. However, in courts where branch sessions are held in places other than county seats, a duplicate as well as original must be prepared for filing in addition to service copies and office copy. 

Throughout this book a dagger (†) has been used as a reminder regarding the duplicate. Additional copies of certain documents are frequently required for certification.

One copy is generally prepared for each party to a contract or agreement in addition to the office copy, except, for instance when husband and wife are parties (unless in a property settlement agreement) one copy may be sufficient for the two.

GENERAL SERVICE AND PROOF 

Explanation (CCP 1011-1013a)

Generally, after service of summons and complaint, most documents are served on the attorney, or firm of attorneys, for each individual appearing separately. Service may be personal, by delivery to attorney (or party) or as set out in CCP 1011-13.

After appearance, a defendant, or his attorney is entitled to notice of all subsequent proceedings of which notice is required to be given.

No bill of exceptions, notice of appeal, or other notice or paper, other than amendments to the pleadings or an amended pleading need be served on any party whose default has been duly entered, or who has not appeared in the action or proceeding. (CCP 1010)

Proof of Service is usually made by an acknowledgment of service on document or by an affidavit of service. Procedure 

1. Personal (CCP 1011) (a) By signature of attorney or authorized representative, on blank usually printed on back of cover of original document. ex. Received copy of the within on 19......... 

Attorney for

(b) By affidavit of service when attorney or authorized representative refuses to acknowledge receipt of such service. †1 original for filing 

 (copy for adverse party unnecessary) 

Attach original affidavit of service to original document and file with clerk (attach to a copy if original has already been filed). 

2. By Mail (CCP 1012, 1013, 1013a) (a) Fill out affidavit of service by mail, either printed form on cover or separate document. +1 original for filing 

1 office copy (copy for adverse party unnecessary) (b) Mail copy of document to attorney for adverse party. (c) Sign affidavit and give oath (not acknowledgment) before notary, having notary sign and affix seal. (d) Attach original affidavit to original document and file with clerk. (e) When service complete-and extension of time (CCP 1013)

The service is complete at the time of the deposit in mail, but if, within a given number of days after such service, a right may be exercised or an act is to be done by adverse party, the time is extended one day, together with one additional day for every 

100 miles, but such extension shall not exceed 30 days in all.

Explanation

GUARDIAN AD LITEM 

(CCP 372-3) When an infant, insane or incompetent person is a party to an action he must appear by general guardian or by guardian ad litem, a person appointed for that particular action by the court in which the action is pending. (CCP 372)

Guardian ad litem for plaintiff must be appointed before issuance of summons. (CCP 373)

Guardian ad litem for defendant should be applied for on his behalf within 10 days after service of summons. 

If defendant neglects to have guardian ad litem appointed, then a party to the suit must make application in order to proceed, or appointment may be made by the court on its own motion. (CCP 373)

The guardian ad litem has no authority as such to compromise a claim or deal with the property of his ward except as authorized by the court appointing him and then only within the limits contemplated by the code section. (CCP 372)

Procedure 

1. Prepare Petition for and Order Appointing Guardian ad Litem (one document) †(original and office copy) (a) Who may make application 

Infant 14 or over

Relative Friend 

Party to action

Court on its own motion may appoint

(b) Necessary allegations 

Incompetency or minority of party (state age) 

No guardian previously appointed or facts showing expediency of appointing guardian ad litem notwithstanding the existence of a general guardian 

Nature of cause of action

Name and facts re relationship of proposed guardian

No previous application for guardian ad litem Guardian's consent to act when petition by minor 14 or over. 

2. Present petition and order to presiding judge for signature ex parte 

3. File (a) Complaint (see COMPLAINT, p 32, (k) (b) Petition and Order appointing guardian ad litem 

Note: Bring action in name of infant or incompetent by his guardian ad litem.

LIS PENDENS 

(CCP 409, 749, 755, 1243)

Lis Pendens is a document by which constructive notice is given to all persons of the pendency of an action affecting title to real property. It should be recorded in the office of the county recorder in county where real property is situated, at the time of filing complaint (or any time afterwards) in all actions affecting title or right of possession of real property, such as: 

(a) Quiet Title 

Must be recorded within 10 days of filing of complaint (CCP 749) (b) Mortgage foreclosure (CCP 409) (c) Condemnation (CCP 1243) (d) Partition (CCP 755)

Unless so recorded such action will be binding only upon the parties to it. When properly filed and recorded

it prevents sharp practices, such as the conveyance of a title by deed to a relative or friend who takes possessession and necessitates the bringing of another action to dispossess him. Procedure Prepare †1 original to be filed with county recorder when complaint is filed 

Fee $1.00 up Form (Caption same as ) Complaint)

NO......... 

) NOTICE OF LIS PENDENS 

NOTICE IS HEREBY GIVEN that an action has been commenced in the above entitled court on a complaint of plaintiff against defendant (s) (insert names of ALL KNOWN defendants) for (nature of action)

This action affects title to the following real property situated in.......... .County, California:

DATED: (description) 19......
